# Kernel-behavior findings (complementary to KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md)

**This doc is the complement of [`KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md`](KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md)** (the canonical kernel-truth prose — owner-authored, integrated in-repo 2026-07-09 from `a12-kernel-demo`). The layer split stays: KERNEL-SEMANTICS holds the settled canonical behavior; KERNEL-FINDINGS is the **evidence log** behind it — dates, oracles (source-read / probe / differential), source citations, and lock pointers, section-numbered to mirror it — plus the verified facts *outside* the canonical doc's evaluation scope. A new kernel fact lands HERE first (verified + test-locked); folding it into the KERNEL-SEMANTICS prose is a deliberate, separate upstream edit, not part of recording a finding.

Kernel-behavior facts **verified by experiment against kernel `30.8.1`**; we **consult KERNEL-SEMANTICS before each increment** to frame them. Each finding is locked by a test in this repo (named per entry) and carries its **fold status** against the canonical prose (the contract below): a *folded* finding keeps its claim + provenance + locks as the evidence skeleton, with the narrative detail living upstream. rulekit's *treatment* of each fact lives in [`KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md`](./KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md).

> **What lands HERE vs INTERPRETER-FINDINGS (the routing rule + decision test).** This doc holds **kernel truths** — a behavior, capability, error code, limit, or extensibility surface that is true *regardless of our code*. How the **interpreter** reproduces/diverges-from/locks such a truth is an `IF<n>` in [`INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md`](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md) (which cites the kernel fact here); how **rulekit/modelkit** treats it is in [`KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md`](KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md). **Decision test:** *"Would this fact be true, and worth recording, even if our interpreter and dmkit did not exist?"* → **yes ⇒ here.** The full three-home table + the worked mis-file lesson live in the INTERPRETER-FINDINGS header.

> **Fold status (the two-doc contract, 2026-07-11).** Every finding heading below carries a fold-status line as its first body line: `> Fold: folded → §n.` (the finding's canonical claim now lives in that [KERNEL-SEMANTICS](KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md) section — the entry here keeps the claim, the provenance, and the lock pointers; several targets join with ` + `), `> Fold: not-foldable — <reason>.` (nothing to fold — an authoring/structure/serialization/toolchain fact outside KERNEL-SEMANTICS's evaluation scope, or pure provenance), or `> Fold: pending — <reason>.` (genuinely unsettled content). A **folded** finding MAY be compressed to its evidence skeleton — claim + provenance (date, oracle, kernel version) + locks — but a finding is **never deleted or renumbered**: `-GAPS` deletions, code comments, and the SEMANTICS-MAP resolve through these headings, and the kernel-upgrade workflow re-verifies findings on a pin bump. The contract is enforced by `cli … KernelDocsFoldContractTest` (the `semanticsMapGuardTest` lane).

## Terminology — the kernel has NO "cell" noun; a repetition's value is a `FeldWert` / "field value" (source-verified 2026-07-11)

> Fold: folded → §3.

"Cell" (with "cell state" and "index column") is a house coinage, not kernel vocabulary: the kernel says *field value* (`FeldWert`) at a *repetition* (`Kontext`/`Kontextnummer`) and has no single noun for the pair, nor for a field's values across the repetitions; the value-state taxonomy maps to the predicates `isWertAngegeben` / not-specified / `nichtPruefungsrelevant`, not a named type. The surviving house terms are declared once in the KERNEL-SEMANTICS §3 house↔kernel vocabulary table; agent-facing surfaces use the kernel wording. *(Provenance: source sweep of the consult-only `../a12-kernel` checkout + the merged BA/dev docs, 2026-07-11, kernel 30.8.1. A vocabulary finding, no behavior; license-safe — no expression copied.)*

## DocumentModel expansion — include mounting, imported type-definitions, the synthetic RootGroup (source-verified 2026-07-11)

> Fold: not-foldable — model-assembly/structure semantics, outside KERNEL-SEMANTICS's runtime-evaluation scope; the clean-room reimplementation is the decoupling Stage 2 reader.

`IDocumentModelService.expand(model, resolver)` is a **mutating tree walk** that folds includes and resolves type-definitions in place. The mechanism, source-read from the consult-only `../a12-kernel` checkout (kernel 30.8.1; MECHANISM only, no expression copied):

- **Include mounting has NO synthetic alias group.** The mount point is a **pre-existing** group in the base carrying `Group.modelAlias` with empty `elements`; expansion appends the **children of the included model's root group** onto that mount group. The included root group *itself is discarded* — only its `usageType` is copied onto the mount. So an included field lands at `/<mountGroupName>/<childName>`; the included model's own root-group name **never appears** in any path. `modelAlias` is only a header-lookup key (→ `modelReferences[alias].reference`, the string the resolver actually resolves) + a transitive-dedup key, never a tree node.
- **`includeLevel`**: `SINGLE_RG` (default; the included model must have exactly one non-repeatable root group) mounts that root group's children; `MODEL_ROOT` mounts every top-level group (each kept as a subgroup of the mount, preserving its name). `excludeRules`/`excludeComputations` (valid only on a mount group) drop the included model's rules/computations *before* mounting; they are **inherited down the include chain** (OR-accumulated), except **direct children of an `attachment`-usage group are never stripped**.
- **Element ids are prefixed on include** (`includingGroupId + "_"`, recursively) but **names are not** — this is exactly the F-IDX suffixed-id-vs-name-path split (an included field's kernel id is `/Storefront/Inventory_/Catalog/ListPrice`, its name-path `/Storefront/Inventory/ListPrice`). **Type-definition ids are neither prefixed nor renamed** across models: `TypeDefCache` keys by `FieldTypeDefinition.getId()` and silently unifies two models sharing an id (origin tracked only via `allModelReferencePaths`).
- **Imported type-definitions** (header `modelReferences` with `purpose == "typeDefinitions"`) are collected recursively and merged into `content.typeDefinitions`, but **only if the base model declares no local type-definitions** ("a mixture of definition ways is not allowed"). Direct `include` references become `transitiveInclude` when hoisted into the base header.
- **Effective type**: `IField.getEffectiveType()` returns the resolved `TypeDefType`'s definition's own concrete `fieldType`, and **`Optional.empty()` for a dangling reference** (set to null on purpose — best-effort expansion). Type-defs resolve one level (a def's `fieldType` is concrete).
- **The synthetic RootGroup.** `content.modelRoot` is a fixed internal `Group` named+id'd `"RootGroup"` whose children are the modeler-visible top-level "root groups". The kernel's own `ElementUtils.getPath` **omits this synthetic root**, so a top-level group is `/Name`, not `/RootGroup/Name`. A reimplementation must index one level below the synthetic root (prefix `""` at the RootGroup level).
- **Included rule/computation condition RELATIVIZATION — only absolute paths and root-turning-point relatives are rewritten; relative-downward refs are carried VERBATIM (source-verified 2026-07-12).** When an included model carries its **own** rules/computations, its root group's name vanishes on mount, so the kernel rewrites the entity references in those conditions — but **only the ones that name the root group**: an **absolute** path (`[/RG/f]`, `Sum(/RG/L*/x)`) or a **relative-upward path with an explicit turning point that reaches the root group** (`../..RG/f`). Every **relative-downward** reference (`[Coverage]`, `[Sub/f]`) is left **unchanged** (`EntityPathsRelativizer.determineUpdatesForPathsInConditionWithRootGroupReference` → `isRelativeDownwardPath` `continue`; `RuleAndComputationConditionUpdaterVisitor` runs on the included model *before* it is integrated, so the rewrite is computed in the included model's own coordinates and the reference becomes relative-without-the-root-name). **Why the interpreter needs to rewrite even less:** its parser resolves every reference against the rule's *post-mount* group `base` (`ModelLoader` sets `group = parent(id)` after id-normalization) AND **counts `..` levels while discarding the turning-point label** (`Parser.readParentUps` / `skipTurningPointLabel`), so an absolute path is the **only** form that mis-resolves post-mount — a relative or root-turning-point reference already resolves correctly against the shifted `base`. Hence carrying a relative-only included condition verbatim is *kernel-exact*, and only an absolute-path-bearing one is flagged for the rewrite.

*(Provenance: source sweep of `DocumentModelExpandService`, `TypeDefCache`, `Field.getEffectiveType`, `EntityReferenceResolverVisitor`, `DocumentModelRoot`, `ElementUtils`, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-11; the relativization mechanism from `DocumentModelExpandService.RuleAndComputationConditionUpdaterVisitor` + `EntityPathsRelativizer`, 2026-07-12. Clean-room reimplemented kernel-free as `dm.core.ModelReader`; locked `core.ModelReaderTest`. The differential-vs-the-kernel bar is met by the Stage-2 parity net (`adapter.ModelIndexParityTest`) + the include-eval parity net (`cli.IncludeEvalDefaultPathTest`); the kernel remains the oracle behind it; R3 `checkConsistency` untouched.)*

## §5 supplement — a NumberType's minValue/maxValue read back at the AUTHORED LITERAL's exact scale (parity-surfaced 2026-07-12)

> Fold: not-foldable — a read-back representation fact, outside KERNEL-SEMANTICS's evaluation scope.

The kernel's `INumberType.getMinValue()`/`getMaxValue()` return the bound **at the authored literal's exact scale** — the deserialized `BigDecimal` keeps the JSON literal verbatim, NOT the field's `maxFractionalDigits`: `10` reads back `10`, `0.00` reads back `0.00`, `50.00` reads back `50.00` (all on a scale-2 field). Surfaced by the Stage-2 parity net (`adapter.ModelIndexParityTest`) over the workspaces corpus. To match, the kernel-free `dm.core.ModelReader` parses numbers with `USE_BIG_DECIMAL_FOR_FLOATS` **and `STRIP_TRAILING_BIGDECIMAL_ZEROES` disabled** (Jackson 2.15+ strips trailing zeros by default — `50.00` → `5E+1` — which would diverge); `ModelIndex.numberMin/numberMax` then returns the node's `BigDecimal` unrescaled. *(Provenance: differential vs the live kernel over authored own-domain fixtures + the workspaces + `a12-kernel-mm` corpora, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-12. Locked `adapter.ModelIndexParityTest`.)*

## §6 supplement — `DifferenceIn{Hours,Minutes,Seconds}` is instant subtraction; DST is a model-`timeZone` axis, NOT a codegen-target axis (source-verified 2026-07-12, refutes the old §6 note)

> Fold: folded → §6.

In every strategy the operation reduces to `floor(|instant₂ − instant₁| / unit)` with the sign from the operand order (truncation toward zero). The runtime service subtracts two epoch-millisecond instants (`BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDifferenceIn*`); Groovy and Java codegen emit a call to that same runtime controller (the `DateDiffOperation` template is `controller.get<Op>(a, b)` — it inlines no arithmetic), and JS codegen calls the TS runtime controller whose luxon instant-diff reduces to the same `floor(Δinstant/unit)`. So the runtime service and all codegen targets agree bit-for-bit — **the earlier claim that the result "can depend on the target language of the generated code" is mechanically wrong** (IG62). DST enters only via the model's configured `timeZone` (meta key `TimeZone`, **default `UTC`**, only other supported value `Europe/Berlin`), applied identically in every strategy at **parse time** (a zone-set `SimpleDateFormat`; JS: luxon with the zone). Under `UTC` the wall-clock gap equals the instant gap; under `Europe/Berlin`, `2024-03-31T01:30:00` (CET) → `2024-03-31T03:30:00` (CEST) is `DifferenceInHours = 1` (not the naive wall-clock 2 — the 02:00–02:59 local hour is skipped). **Interpreter status (re-scoped IG62):** the interpreter computes datetime diffs on naive 86 400 s/day epoch seconds with no timezone modeling, so it matches the kernel for `UTC` models (and same-period datetimes under any zone) but **diverges from a `Europe/Berlin` model across a DST transition** — the meta-model `timeZone` is a not-yet-surfaced customization point (a bounded follow-up, affecting `Add{Hours,…}`/`DifferenceInDays`/datetime comparison too, gated on the DST differential in `DateTimeArithmeticDiffTest`). *(Provenance: source sweep of `BedingungsOperatorHelper`/`RuntimeController`/`ValidationDateParser`/the codegen `DateDiffOperation` templates + the TS runtime, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-12. The refuted claim is corrected in KERNEL-SEMANTICS §6; the re-scoped gap stays open as IG62 — no green lock is possible until the timezone is modeled.)*

## §7 supplement — `Length` (and string min/maxLength) count UTF-16 code units (source + lock, 2026-07-12, graduates IG63)

> Fold: folded → §7.

The kernel measures string length in **UTF-16 code units** in every length-bearing path, with no code-point folding or grapheme clustering: `Length(field)` returns the value's UTF-16-unit count (runtime `BigDecimalUtils.laenge` → the platform string length — Java `String.length()` / TS `string.length`; the Groovy/Java/JS codegen reuse the same runtime helper, so no strategy diverges), and the `minLength`/`maxLength` formal checks (`stringZuKurz`/`stringZuLang`) compare that same count. So a **decomposed combining sequence** `e` + `U+0301` counts as **2** (the sense in which §7 says a combining sequence is "several characters"), not 1 grapheme. Grapheme clustering appears only in the *legal-charset / DIN-91379* acceptance check (IF43), never in the count. **Interpreter parity:** the interpreter counts with Kotlin `String.length` = UTF-16 units on both JVM + JS, matching the kernel; the combining case is differential-locked (`adapter.laws.AbsLengthDiffTest` — Length == 2, ≠ 1) + multiplatform (`interpreter … LengthUnicodeCountTest`). **Residual (recorded, unreachable in practice):** a supplementary-plane / astral character (`U+1F600`, a surrogate pair) is `String.length` 2 and the kernel would count 2 under a permissive charset, but the interpreter currently reads a supplementary-plane string as a non-plain operand (its `Length` is *unknown*, so a `Length == 2` rule does not fire) — a divergence that is unreachable under any realistic charset (the default is BMP, and DIN 91379 excludes astral chars, so such a value is itself an illegal-character formal error). Not locked; characterized here. *(Provenance: source read of the kernel `Length`/`FormatDefinitionString` paths + codegen templates, kernel 30.8.1, 2026-07-12; interpreter divergence probe-confirmed the same day.)*

## §1 supplement — Boolean/Confirm comparability codes

> Fold: folded → §1.

The Boolean≠Confirm split is enforced at parse time with distinct codes: a Boolean compares only to `True`/`False` or another Boolean — anything else (e.g. a string) is **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YESNO`**; a Confirm compares only to `True` or another Confirm — `[Confirm] == False` and Boolean↔Confirm cross-comparisons are **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YES`**. "Not confirmed" is written `[Confirm] != True` (an unset confirm counts as not confirmed). *(Locked: `validate.laws.BooleanConfirmLawsTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — Boolean three-state matrix (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §1.

Runtime-verified in a filled row: an unset Boolean satisfies neither `== True` nor `== False`; `FieldFilled` holds for both values — so `[Flag] == True` ≠ `FieldFilled(Flag)` and `[Flag] == False` ≠ "unchecked". Contrast a Confirm, whose unset value *is* `False` in comparisons. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.BooleanThreeStateLawsTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — the "not …" field-list quantifiers are not complements (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §1 + §12.

The three negative fill-quantifiers disagree at the edges; the trap is the all-empty case, which `NotAllFieldsFilled` and `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` include but `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` excludes (needs ≥ 1 filled) — canonical §1. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldListQuantifierLawsTest`; interpreter parity `adapter.laws.FieldListQuantifierDiffTest`.)*

**The message TYPE of a fired quantifier is data-dependent, not per-operator (runtime, 2026-06-23):** `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` types OMISSION at k=0 and VALUE at k=2, so a static per-operator polarity is wrong for it — canonical §12 "the same firing rule can carry either type". *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldListQuantifierDiffTest.messageTypeMatchesKernel`.)*

**"Kontext" = Group — a legacy German kernel term (owner-confirmed, 2026-06-26).** In older kernel/DSL vocabulary a repeatable **group** is a *Kontext* ("context"); the German operator `KontextAngegeben(G)` is today's **`GroupFilled(G)`**, and the structural over-repetition error `zuGrosseKontextnummer` (below) is literally "context number too large" = a field instance beyond the group's declared repeatability. So a real-world rule guarded by `KontextAngegeben(<group>)` transfers to `GroupFilled(<group>)` (rulekit: `m.group(path).filled()`). It is plain group presence — "≥1 descendant field of the group is filled" — not a field predicate. *(GroupFilled itself is locked by `adapter.laws.GroupFilledDiffTest`; this entry records only the terminology so a German-sourced rule reads correctly.)*

**Group-list quantifier asterisk bar (source + probe, 2026-06-17).** The *group*-list quantifiers `NotAllGroupsFilled`/`GroupsNotCollectivelyFilled`/`AllGroupsFilled` reject an **asterisk** argument — `NotAllGroupsFilled(Items*, ShippingAddress)` draws **`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED`** (source `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkNoWildCardsInGroup`, fired on `ref.hasWildCardsInGroup()`). **Group-list quantifier rootGroup bar (source + accept/reject lock, 2026-07-12; graduates IG65).** The **same three** also **bar any rootGroup argument**: `CheckKontextListenBedingungImpl` (A12 path) routes them through `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkNotRootGroupReferenced`, firing **`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED`** ("no Vordruck/root group may be referenced") when any arg is a top-level group — ahead of the arity/asterisk checks. The **other two** (`NoGroupFilled`/`AtLeastOneGroupFilled`) instead route through `checkNoRootGroupWhenMoreThanOneParameter`: a **sole** rootGroup is **accepted**; a rootGroup **with a second parameter** draws **`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_WITH_OTHER_PARAMETERS`** ("a root group may only be the sole parameter"). Confirmed on a **two-non-repeatable-root** fixture (`/Policy` + `/Claim`) — the single-root order fixture couldn't reach it (a rootGroup + sibling trips `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` first), and a *repeatable* root can't be referenced bare (it demands an asterisk, itself barred). *(Locked: `validate.laws.GroupQuantifierLawsTest` — the asterisk bar, the three→`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED`, the two accept a sole root + reject root+other→`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_WITH_OTHER_PARAMETERS`.)*

## §1 supplement — a STARRED single-group quantifier counts the group's INSTANTIATED repetitions (probe-verified, both strategies, 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §1.

All four claims below are canonical in §1 "How the fill quantifiers count"; the evidence skeleton:

`NoGroupFilled(G*)` / `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*)` (the kernel's selection idiom) tally the group's **instantiated** repetitions — a created-but-empty row is content — so they fire iff **zero** / **≥ 1** rows; polarity OMISSION / VALUE. Only these two admit the starred single-group form. *(IF68; locked `adapter.laws.StarredGroupQuantifierDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.StarredGroupQuantifierTest`.)*

**A field-fill quantifier over a GROUP SCOPE = "the fields inside the group", recursively — and the runtime uses TWO iteration ranges (source `RuntimeController` + `ExpandService`, probe-confirmed 2026-07-06).** `ExpandService` rewrites a group operand to a field-list quantifier over `getAllContainedFields(group)` (recursive; a repeatable group carries its `*` onto each descendant). `AllFieldsFilled`/`NotAllFieldsFilled` iterate the **full declared range** (`EntityIterator` over `1..repeatability`, no `setzeEndIndicesAufMaxGesetzt` clamp — an un-instantiated rep reads empty); `AtLeastOneFieldFilled`/`NoFieldFilled`/`MoreThanOneFieldFilled`/`NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` clamp to the **instantiated** rows; `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` mixes the two. *(IF49; locked `adapter.laws.GroupScopedFieldFillDiffTest` — bare/starred × both ranges × firing + polarity.)*

**…and a VALUE AGGREGATE accepts a GROUP operand the same way (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `Sum`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues` over a starred group feed the combiner every contained field's cells, recursively; a malformed contained cell poisons like a field-star cell; an empty group is the empty-selection 0 (`NumberOfDifferentValues`'s static scale is 0 — a non-scale-0 target draws `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`). A `Having` on a group star is codegen-rejected (`MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`), so only the unfiltered expansion is reachable. *(IF99; locked `adapter.laws.GroupStarValueAggregateDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … GroupStarValueAggregateTest`.)*

**A formally-INVALID (not-check-relevant) star cell is EXCLUDED from the fill tally — neither filled nor empty (source + differential 2026-07-08).** Malformed and declared-constraint-invalid cells behave identically (`isWertAngegeben == unknown`): counted in neither bucket (they do contribute to `n`) — so `AtLeastOneFieldFilled` AND `NoFieldFilled` are both FALSE over rows whose only cells are invalid — and any invalid element makes a value aggregate non-evaluable (the `NumberCombiner` poison). *(IF84; locked `adapter.laws.StarQuantifierInvalidCellDiffTest` + `AggMalformedDiffTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — `MaxValue`/`MinValue` over an EMPTY operand set = 0, not undefined (runtime, probe-confirmed 2026-07-06)

> Fold: folded → §2.

One empty operand is dropped from the fold (`MaxValue(-5, empty) == -5` — no `0` folded in); an **entirely empty** selection folds to the fillable `0`, the same empty identity as `Sum` — distinct from a malformed operand, which suppresses. Canonical §2 "aggregate identities" carries both, including why the identity stayed latent under most thresholds (`0` and "suppressed" coincide until a shape like `MinValue(...) < 10` separates them). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.MultiFieldAggregateDiffTest`.)*

## §1 supplement — the COUNT aggregates are monotonic non-decreasing → grow-only polarity (runtime, probe-confirmed 2026-07-06)

> Fold: folded → §12.

Filling can only raise a count, so a fired `count >= n` / `> n` is VALUE while `< n` / `== n` stays OMISSION — a count types like an unsigned `Sum` (canGrow while a fillable operand remains, never canShrink); a static per-operator polarity is wrong. Canonical §12 "the counts can only grow". Latent until the `>=` direction was pinned. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.MultiFieldAggregateDiffTest.firedPolarityMatchesKernel` + `NumberOfFilledDiffTest`.)*

## Stored conditions are canonical ENGLISH, regardless of authoring language (serialization, verified 2026-07-06)

> Fold: not-foldable — a serialization-tier fact (the stored form of conditions), outside the canonical doc's evaluation scope.

A rule/computation's `errorCondition`/`operation` is persisted in the DM-JSON with **English keywords**, even in a German-authored model. The condition *lexer* is language-parametric (`LexerTerminals_en`/`_de` — a German author may type `Und`/`FeldAngegeben`/`Heute`), and `DslRenderer(ConditionLanguage.DE)` can render to German for **display**, but the **stored** form is English. Verified against the real German A12 Tools workspace (2025.06-ext5): every stored `errorCondition` reads `GroupFilled(...)`/`FieldNotFilled(...)`/`And`/`NotExactlyOneFieldFilled(...)`/`MinValue(...)` — never `Und`/`FeldAngegeben`. **Consequence for the interpreter:** it only ever parses *stored* conditions, so its **EN-only parser is correct by construction** — German is a render (display) target, never a round-tripped input (INTERPRETER-GAPS §3, was IG28). The kernel (on the JVM, via the authoring/check path) handles DE authoring with its own language-parametric parser; the kernel-free interpreter needs no DE lexer.

## §2 supplement — empty-as-0 is a *filled-row* behavior (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §2.

The §2 substitutions apply inside a content-bearing row; an entirely empty instance is evaluated only when the condition **can fire empty** — a structural property (`canConditionFireEmpty`, runtime + source-audited 2026-06-23): the negative presence family fires on a blank instance, comparisons and the positive presence checks do not, `And` fires empty iff both branches do, `Or` iff either. All canonical in §2 "the row gate". *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EmptyRowGateDiffTest`; the original filled-row probe rode `[Quantity] < 100` via `IDocumentRtService.validateFull`.)*

Refinements, each canonical in §2 (or where noted):

- **An INSTANTIATED repeatable-group row is itself CONTENT** — per-row empty-as-0 fires on every created-but-blank repeat row, and on a non-repeatable ancestor whose only content is such rows; a non-repeatable group's bare structural presence is not content (runtime-probed 2026-07-06). *(IF53; locked `adapter.laws.RepeatableRowContentDiffTest`.)*
- **PARTIAL validation overrides the content gate** — a relevant instance is always evaluated, empty or phantom; the IF42 relevance gate still bounds a `*`-aggregate under a concrete relevant row (runtime-probed 2026-07-06). *(IF54; locked `RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.partialRelevantEmptyInstanceIsEvaluated`.)*
- **The type-asymmetry of the empty case** — number fires as `0`, confirm as `False`, boolean/string/date not evaluated, a string **in a `+` concatenation** evaluated as `""` — is §2's per-kind table + override table. The concatenation row is the trap an N6 run surfaced (2026-06-08): `[Full] != [First] + " " + [Last]` mis-fires on empty parts unless guarded `AllFieldsFilled(First, Last)`.
- **A bare STRING COPY computation (`X = [S]`) over an EMPTY operand CLEARS** — it does not store the concatenation's `""`; the empty-as-`""` rule is the `+`-position's only (tri-check-verified 2026-07-10, the IG56 Set-S probe; canonical §11). *(Locked `adapter.laws.StringCopyComputeDiffTest`.)*
- **Value-token representation** — a boolean stores lowercase `true`/`false` (any other token is `feldJaNeinFalsch`); a confirm accepts only `true` (`false` → `feldJaFalsch`) — canonical §1. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EmptyValueLawsTest`; the runtime oracle is `adapter.laws.RuntimeLaws` — KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES "Runtime behavioral-law oracle".)*

## §2 supplement — empty operand in a *computation* operation ⇒ no value (stale cleared) (runtime, 2026-06-08)

> Fold: folded → §11.

A DATE-family operation over an empty operand is not evaluated: the computed field gets no value and a stale stored value is CLEARED (probed via `DateRange(OrderDate, DeliveryDate)` on `IDocumentRtService.compute`). Consequence: an `AllFieldsFilled` guard over DATE operands is **redundant** (a false precondition clears exactly as the unevaluated operation does — this settled the 2026-06-08 T13 judge split), but the redundancy is per-type — it does NOT hold for strings-in-concatenation (empty concats as `""`) or numbers (empty-as-0); canonical §11 "one operation, three outcomes" carries the full per-kind table and the guard consequence. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeComputationLawsTest`, via the `RuntimeLaws.compute` oracle.)*

## §11 supplement — compute(): an out-of-range value is ERRORED; a computation reading an errored dependency is SKIPPED (runtime, 2026-06-26)

> Fold: folded → §11.

Two compute() facts, both probe-confirmed via `IDocumentRtService.compute`:

- **An out-of-range computed value is ERRORED, value returned.** When the computed value is not valid for the target field (e.g. a number above its `maxValue`), the kernel returns the value but flags the instance in **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors()`** rather than `…WithChanges()`. So `T (maxValue 999) := [A]` with `A = 5000` yields `T = 5000`, errored. *(Interpreter: `ComputeOutcome.ERRORED`; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeErrorBucketDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF34.)*
- **A dependent of an errored computation gets NO outcome — absent when its input cell was empty, CLEARED when it was filled** (refined 2026-07-08 by the poison finding below). In the cascade, an errored dependency's value is unusable: a dependent's READ of it throws (the poison mechanism), so the dependent computes nothing. Probed: with `T` errored (above), `U := [T] + 1` and `V := [U] + 1` are **absent** from the compute result when unseeded (only the errored `T` is reported) — and a **seeded** dependent is reported **CLEARED** (input-filled, not computed). The poison is READ-driven, not a static dependency skip: a dependent whose evaluation never actually reads the errored target (a short-circuit decided first) computes normally. *(Interpreter: read-driven poisoning, reported CLEARED under its report-all contract; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeCascadeErroredDiffTest` + `ComputePoisonReadDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF40/IF85.)*

The **number** branch is confirmed for computation too (2026-06-09): a numeric copy `EffectiveFee = [BaseFee]` with **`BaseFee` empty** computes **`0`** (a value), not nothing — the empty-as-0 substitution applies in a computation operation exactly as in a comparison, so an empty *number* operand is the opposite of an empty *date* operand (which clears). **Consequence (inverts the date redundancy above):** a presence guard over a NUMBER operand is **NOT redundant** — `EffectiveFee = [BaseFee]` guarded by `FieldFilled(BaseFee)` computes **nothing** (cleared) on an empty `BaseFee`, where the unguarded form computes `0`; the two **differ**, so a candidate that adds such a guard to a number computation is **not** equivalent to the unguarded gold. (The N6 computation/modify judges carry this type split so they don't mis-equate the two.) *(Locked: `cli.ComputeDocVerbTest.anEmptyNumberOperandComputesZero` + `aGuardedNumberComputationClearsWhereUnguardedComputesZero`, via the `model compute` verb / `KernelAdapter.compute` seam.)*

A **FILLED but FORMALLY-INVALID operand CLEARS the computation** (source-consistent + differential 2026-07-08). Distinct from *empty* (a number empty-fires as 0, above — and a REQUIRED-empty operand is STILL empty-as-0, NOT cleared: `nichtAngegeben`, its MANDATORY error is a separate check): a **filled** operand whose value is formally invalid — a NUMBER with the wrong fraction-digit count (violating `min/maxFractionalDigits`) or an out-of-**range** value (`min/maxValue`) — is **not check-relevant** (`nichtPruefungsrelevant`), so `compute()` cannot evaluate an operation reading it and the computed field is **CLEARED**. The SAME operand-level "not check-relevant ⇒ unknown" rule the validation side applies (§2/§3), on the compute path. Probed: `Density = RoundDown([Inhabitants] * 1000000 / [Area], 2)` with `Area` declared `minFractionalDigits = 2` → `Area = 257.71` (2 frac) computes; `Area = 710` / `-843.9` (0 / 1 frac, formally invalid) → **CLEARED**. It is the OPERAND's validity, independent of tier selection (the minimal repro is unconditional). The kernel rule is type-agnostic, and the interpreter now mirrors it for the **full formal-error class** (IF78 generalized IF76 past the initial NUMBER value-domain subset — every number code, string length/pattern, enum-domain, custom type, over-limit), using the SAME `computeFormalErrorAt` gate validation uses; a filled-invalid string used as a **concatenation** operand likewise clears (IF81). **Downstream, this clear is a POISON, not an empty read** — the target is marked invalid and dependents' reads throw (the poison finding below, IF85). *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.compute` threads the `invalid` gate `validate` uses over FILLED operands — a formally-invalid operand → `Val.Unknown` → CLEARED; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeInvalidOperandDiffTest` + `ComputeOperandInvalidityTest` + `ConcatInvalidOperandDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF76/IF78/IF81, found via the `ModelShrinker` delta-debug of a real `_CDM` model, IG44.)*

A **precondition-CLEARED computed field propagates to a downstream computation as EMPTY** (differential 2026-07-08; boundary carved by the poison finding below): a computed field its OWN computation CLEARED **silently** (a false precondition / no matching alternative) reads as **EMPTY** (`nichtAngegeben`) in a dependent — so a dependent's `FieldFilled(dep)` is FALSE and a number operand reads empty-as-0. Source mechanism: the calc run **strips every calculated field's input value** from its working data (`CalculationCommand.getDataWithoutEntitiesNotNeededForCalculations` — *"calculations always produce a new value anyways"*), so an un-computed, un-poisoned calculated field reads empty regardless of any seeded value. An **operand-invalidity clear is NOT this case** — it marks the target invalid, and dependents' reads POISON (below). *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.compute` records CLEARED cascade targets and `OverlayContext` reads them EMPTY, not the stale stored seed; locked `adapter.laws.CascadeClearedDependencyDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF77 — boundary IF85.)*

**…and both cascade read-rules reach INSIDE a `Having` filter and a precondition's quantifiers (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** A downstream computation's `Having` filter (`Sum(/G*/share Having [/G/flag] == True)` over a computed `flag`) and a precondition's filtered quantifier read a calculated field exactly like a direct operand read: a silently-CLEARED upstream target reads **EMPTY** (the input-strip above — the filter drops the row no matter what stale value the input document carried), and a POISONED one **poisons the reading computation** (below). Differentially pinned in BOTH directions on one instance — a stale-"true" ended row is dropped AND a stale-"false" active row is kept — plus the poison and precondition-quantifier arms (groovy-dynamic == java-static). *(Interpreter: the cascade dependency order includes a `Having` filter's refs and a precondition's starred refs, and the compute overlay WRAPS the row contexts a filter iterates; locked `adapter.laws.HavingOverComputedDependencyDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … HavingOverComputedDependencyTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF98.)*

**compute POISONS on reading a formally-invalid field — the read aborts the computation instance, order-dependently, and the poison cascades (source + 16-scenario tri-differential, 2026-07-08).** During `compute()` the kernel does NOT apply validation's "not check-relevant → excluded from the tally": `CalculationCache.convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation` converts the formally-invalid entities so that **any read throws** (`CalculationFieldException` — a value read via `getWertXML`, a presence check via `isEntityFilled`, a quantifier scan visit, a `Having` filter read). The generated alternative catches it, marks the instance **"fehlerhaft berechnet"** (silently — distinct from an ERRORED computed value, which also carries a message), **skips the remaining alternatives**, and produces no value — reported **CLEARED iff the input had the cell filled**, absent otherwise. The invalid-marking cascades: a dependent that READS the poisoned target (value or presence) is poisoned too. Because the poison is **read-driven**, evaluation order decides: the fill-quantifier helpers stop at their deciding cell (`mindestensEineEntityAngegeben` at the first filled — an invalid cell after the stop is never read; `keineEntityAngegeben` at the first filled; `alleEntities`/`nichtAlleOderKeine` at the first empty over the DECLARED range; the two-sided quantifiers — `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled`, `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` — COMPOSE two such scans, so each scan's own stop rule decides which cells are ever read and hence which invalid cell poisons), value aggregates feed ALL selected rows to their combiner (any invalid poisons), the generated `And`/`Or` collapse each conjunct 2-valued (`isKnownAndTrue`) and short-circuit — a FALSE **or UNKNOWN** left skips the right conjunct entirely — and a compute-side `Having` keeps only TRUE rows (the not-FALSE keep-law is validation-only). **Both strategies agree** (tri-check groovy-dynamic == java-static across all 16 lock scenarios). *(Interpreter: compute-mode poison-on-read + ordered scans + short-circuits; locked `adapter.laws.ComputePoisonReadDiffTest` + `interpreter … ComputePoisonTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF85.)*

A **computed NUMBER stores PADDED to the target's `minFractionalDigits` — and BOTH compute strategies render identically** (source + probe, 2026-07-08; **locus corrected 2026-07-09**: the store formatter is `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkBigDecimal, id)` — shared runtime code the codegen templates call — NOT `NumberConversion.determineFractionalDigits`, whose 15-digit render cap the compute store path never applies; the two rules agree on every ≤ 15-digit value, which is why the original 13-case probe couldn't tell them apart — the store-path finding below is where they part). The fit-arm rule is `destFractionalDigits = max(stripTrailingZeros().scale(), minFractionalDigits)`: a value `10` into a `minFractionalDigits`-2 field stores **`10.00`**, `5.5` → **`5.50`**, `0` → **`0.00`**; a field with **no** `minFractionalDigits` (0) stores the canonical **`10`**/`5.5`/`0`, and a value with more natural fractional digits than the min keeps them. **Both compute strategies agree** — the legacy V1 `compute(IDocument)` AND the modern V2 `compute(DocumentV2, config)` (the corpus capture/replay path) ride that one controller and render bit-identically (13-case probe: legacy == modern every time). This **disproves the IG53-filed hypothesis** that "the two kernel render paths disagree on trailing zeros." Corollary: a computed whole number into a `minFractionalDigits`≥1 field is a **VALUE** (padded → valid), not ERRORED. *(Interpreter: `toComputed` renders `Val.Num` via the `storedComputedNumber` store rule; locked `adapter.laws.ComputedNumberScaleDiffTest` — a QUAD-check pinning legacy == modern == java-static == interpreter; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF82, refined by IF92.)*

**The computed-NUMBER store path in full — scale-19 pre-round, an UNCAPPED fit render, a length-bounded no-fit arm; the stored form is never re-rounded to fit the 15-digit check** (source + quad-differential, 2026-07-09). `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkBigDecimal, id)` — both strategies — does, in order: **(1)** `setScale(19, HALF_UP)` (`VkBigDecimal.DEFAULT_SCALE` — observable only for a quotient/product whose scale exceeds 19); **(2)** if the value's natural (stripped) scale **fits** the target's `maxFractionalDigits`: render at `max(naturalScale, minFractionalDigits)` with **NO length cap**, run the formal check on that string (`checkField`), and store it — so a computed `RoundAccounting(product-of-many, 2)` worth `354027231847478.01` (17 digits) stores in FULL, trips `stelligkeitZuLang` (>15 digits, sign excluded), and the cell is **ERRORED with the full value** (`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` carries it); **(3)** if the natural scale does **not fit** (`minScale > maxNachkommaMeta`): render length-bounded to **16 significant digits** (`BigDecimalUtils.toStringWertLaengenBeschraenkt(v19, 16)` — ≤ 16 significant digits → canonical; longer → `setScale(max(0, 16 − integerDigits), HALF_UP)`), store it, and mark **"fehlerhaft berechnet" unconditionally** (ERRORED even when the bounded string is formally valid — e.g. a scale-4 product `2.4723` into a maxFrac-2 target). The no-fit arm is reachable on a legal model **only** through the computation's **`errorCodesToSuppress: ["MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES"]`** (`IComputation.getErrorCodesToSuppress` — the kernel's own model-level escape hatch for the static assignment-scale gate); without it the codegen REJECTS a scale-mismatched assignment — the gate is an **ERROR** for computations, riding the implicit `[target] != <op>` comparison check (differentially pinned: `[A]*[B]` of scale-2 operands has static scale **4**, a bare division static scale **"?"**, both rejected into a scale-2 target). *(Interpreter: `storedComputedNumber` (commonMain) reproduces all three steps and `toComputed` reports a no-fit cell ERRORED into the IF85 poison set; locked `adapter.laws.ComputedNumberDigitOverflowDiffTest` — a QUAD-check incl. the suppressed no-fit arm and the rejected-without-suppression control — and multiplatform `interpreter … ComputedNumberDigitOverflowTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF92.)*

## §11 supplement — TIERED computations: alternatives are selected in order by precondition; `commonPrecondition` gates the whole (source + probe-verified 2026-06-25)

> Fold: folded → §11.

A computation is a **list of `computationAlternatives`**, each an optional `precondition` + an `operation` (the value expression), optionally under a whole-computation `commonPrecondition`. The kernel codegen (`CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st`) emits, per alternative in order, `if (<precondition>) { handleValue(<operation>); continue; }` — so the runtime **evaluates alternatives top-to-bottom and the FIRST whose precondition holds supplies the value**; an alternative with no precondition always applies (an unconditional default); if **none** holds the field is **cleared** (computes nothing). A `commonPrecondition` guards the whole computation (false ⇒ cleared). This is the kernel's "tiered computation" feature.

**Probe-verified (2026-06-25)** — a `UnitPrice` computation with `[{precondition: "[Quantity] < 100", operation: "9.99"}, {precondition: "[Quantity] >= 100", operation: "0"}]`: `Quantity = 50` → `UnitPrice = 9.99` (alt 1), `Quantity = 150` → `UnitPrice = 0` (alt 2). So the precondition genuinely *selects* the operation — the discriminating-precondition case is NOT the redundant-guard equivalence above (§66, which is the narrow degenerate case of a single fill-style guard that empty-as-skip already subsumes). A precondition over an empty operand does not select its branch (it is not three-valued TRUE); a gap where **no** alternative's precondition holds → the field is **cleared** (probe-verified 2026-06-26).

**Mutual EXCLUSIVITY of tiered preconditions is a documentation-level contract — NO consistency-tier diagnostic flags an overlap (probe-verified 2026-07-10, the IG56 Set-S probe).** `checkConsistency` over a two-tier computation with OVERLAPPING preconditions (`[BaseFee] > 0` / `[BaseFee] > -100`) and even byte-IDENTICAL preconditions (`> 0` twice) emits an EMPTY diagnostic set — indistinguishable from the exclusive control. The BA's "mutually exclusive" requirement is enforced by no tier; the runtime resolves an overlap by the first-declared-wins selection above. *(Locked: `validate.laws.ComputationTierExclusivityLawsTest`.)*

**`MVK_ERROR_EMPTY_PRECONDITION` — an empty precondition is allowed only as the SOLE alternative (codegen-verified 2026-06-26).** A trailing **unconditional** alternative mixed with guarded ones is rejected at codegen — "Empty prerequisite is not allowed here." So a *tiered* computation's alternatives all carry preconditions; an unconditional operation is the ordinary **non-tiered** computation (one alternative, no precondition). The "default after guards" shape is therefore unauthorable — no consumer (or the interpreter) ever has to evaluate it. The **scale gate** also applies to an operation's *assignment*: a field operand whose scale differs from the computed field draws `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` (numeric **literals** are scale-exempt, §5), so a tiered operation either matches the target scale or rounds explicitly.

The **interpreter models this in full** (fixed 2026-06-26, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF21): `ComputationDef` carries the alternative list + `commonPrecondition`, and `DmInterpreter.compute` selects the first alternative whose precondition is 3VL-TRUE (null = unconditional), clearing the field when the common precondition fails or no alternative holds — matching the codegen selection above. dmtool's *authoring* side (rulekit `DraftComputation`, `computation add`) always supported it, so the earlier cross-stack divergence (authored-but-mis-evaluated) is closed. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationPreconditionDiffTest` — tiered low/high select, no-match→cleared, common-precondition true/false, all interp == kernel.)*

**A SEMANTIC-INDEX lookup in a computation poisons on an INVALID index COLUMN; a clean column keeps the validation read's empty-as-0 (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `Result = [Items/Price For LookupKey]` (a real-workspace idiom): with ANY invalid cell in the index column — a MALFORMED value (a 16-digit `stelligkeitZuLang`), a DUPLICATED key pair, or an EMPTY key cell — the kernel marks every reading computation "incorrectly calculated" (CLEARED), **even when a clean row matches the key** (the IndexFieldCache marking — the semantic-index analog of the §9/§11 parallel-iteration invalid-index rule). Over a CLEAN column the compute read equals the validation read: a no-match key and a matched-but-EMPTY target cell both read **empty-as-0** (`VALUE|0.00` into a min-frac-2 target), never cleared. Groovy-dynamic == java-static across all arms. *(Interpreter: `evalSemanticIndex` poisons compute-mode reads on an invalid column; the empty-as-0 no-match had been correct all along; locked `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … SemanticIndexComputeInvalidColumnTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF107.)*

**A matched formally-invalid indexed cell poisons the reading computation — per READ CELL, not per row (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** Over a CLEAN index column, a `[F For key]` compute read whose MATCHED cell is formally invalid (wrong scale, too long, …) clears the reading computation — a direct operand and a `FieldValueAsString(ref For key)` coercion alike, and a concatenation cannot rescue it as `""` (the same not-check-relevant read rule as a direct invalid field operand). The granularity is the **cell actually read**: a sibling computation reading a CLEAN cell of the same matched row stays VALUE, and an invalid target cell in an UNMATCHED row (never read) poisons nothing. The IF107 column marking also reaches a standalone `FieldValueAsString(… For …)` read — every indexed read goes through the same cache. *(Interpreter: one shared compute-guarded read for both sites; locked `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexMatchedInvalidCellDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF108.)*

**VALIDATION-side indexed reads are not-check-relevant PER LOOKUP, not per column (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10 — the validation counterpart of the compute column marking above).** A `[F For key]` read in a RULE (incl. the implicit computation-validation): a match on a CLEAN key cell resolves the read regardless of the other cells' states — a malformed key cell elsewhere does NOT suppress (where a computation would clear); a match on a DUPLICATED key cell is not-check-relevant (uniqueIndex marks both cells); a NO-match over a column carrying ANY not-check-relevant cell — malformed (a maybe-match), EMPTY (auto-required), duplicated — is unresolvable → np → the referencing rule is suppressed (the flag ORs through a concatenation, `VkString.add` → `isStringNotRelevant`); a CLEAN no-match stays the NOT-GIVEN read (na) — a referencing concat fires with `""` parts and OMISSION polarity. *(Interpreter: the shared guarded read's validation branch + the concat np gate; locked `adapter.laws.IndexedReadSuppressionDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF115.)*

**`FieldValueAsString` of a NOT-GIVEN read behaves per context — a standalone computation CLEARS, a concatenation gets `""` (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** The coercion of an empty/absent source (a plain empty field, a no-match key, a matched-but-empty indexed cell) is a not-given string, not a storable `""`: `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkString)` returns early on an empty value ("do like no calculation was done") → the standalone copy computes nothing (CLEARED — the generalization of the §11 bare-string-copy law to the coercion), while a `+`-concatenation maps it to `""` (the IF81 empty rule) and a comparison is suppressed. A formally-INVALID source read through the coercion poisons the computation (standalone and in-concat alike). **The operand must be a NUMBER/AMOUNT field** — `FieldValueAsString(stringField)` is model-rejected with `MVK_NO_NUMBER` ("Only numbers and amounts are allowed"; a string copies bare `[S]`). *(Locked `adapter.laws.FieldValueAsStringComputeDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF109.)*

**`FirstFilledValue` consumes its selection only UNTIL the first FILLED cell — an invalid cell poisons the computation only BEFORE it (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `FirstValueCombiner.addValue` ignores every add once the first filled value is captured: an invalid TAIL cell (e.g. a leading-zero number after a filled row) is invisible — the computation stores the first value (`VALUE`), it does not clear. An invalid cell BEFORE the first given one sets `nonRelevantFieldBeforeFirstGiven` → the non-relevant result → CLEARED (also when an empty row precedes the invalid one); an empty cell before the first given sets only the displaceability flag, never a poison. This makes `FirstFilledValue` the one stop-at-first value combiner — the "any invalid selected cell poisons the computation" rule holds for the consume-every-row combiners (Sum/Min/Max/NumberOfDifferentValues fold each row). *(Interpreter: a compute-mode ordered scan in `firstFilledValue`; locked `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueInvalidTailDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF111.)*

**Computed values are checked with the BASIC format check — no charset / leading-blank / trailing-blank baseline (source-confirmed both strategies, 2026-07-10).** A computed result is bucketed "fehlerhaft berechnet" by `CalculationController.checkField` → `FormalChecker.checkFieldValueFormatBasic`, which runs ONLY `IFormatDefinition.validiereFormat` (type format + declared constraints). The baseline legal-charset / leading-blank / trailing-blank checks belong to the full `checkFieldValueFormat` that stored INPUT values get — so a computed string that starts or ends with a blank (e.g. an empty operand concatenated before a literal, `" G"`) stores as a plain VALUE. Both strategies share the one `CalculationController` store path. *(Interpreter: `computedOutcome` runs the extracted `typeFormatError` (Basic) instead of the full `formalError`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF110.)*

**The root store takes a SECOND decision on the FINAL result value — a final EMPTY String text stores NO value, whatever expression produced it (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-15; feedback F10 → IG69 → IF123).** `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkString value, id)` — shared runtime code BOTH strategies' generated alternatives call (`CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st`, groovy and java, emit the same `controller.handleBerechnetenWert(<calcFunctionCode>, id)`) — returns **before** the format check and the store when `value.isNichtPruefungsrelevant() || !value.hasValue()`, with `VkString.hasValue()` = non-empty text: *"for empty values, do like no calculation was done."* So an **all-empty concatenation** (`[A] + [B]`, both clean-empty → the parts substitute `""` per IF81 and the result reaches the root store as empty text) computes **nothing** — the same no-value decision as the bare empty copy (§2 refinement) and the `FieldValueAsString` coercion (above); the §2 "empty-as-`""` is the `+`-position's only" names the *substitution* rule inside the expression, not a storable final `""`. The gate is **per result type**: `VkDate`/`VkDateRange` no-value on a null date (an unevaluated date op — already "computes nothing"), while `VkBigDecimal` has NO empty arm (a number is empty-as-0 → a real `0`; its gate is `wertIstUngueltig` → the calc error). Consequences, each differentially pinned: **(1)** the empty decision **precedes `checkField`** — an empty final text into a `minLength` target CLEARS, never ERRORED (the kernel never checks a value it does not store); **(2)** "like no calculation was done" is exactly the IF87 fall-through — a later computation targeting the same cell can still win, and a dependent reads the target EMPTY (nothing enters the calc cache); **(3)** the delta granularity applies **afterward** (a stale-filled target reports CLEARED, an already-empty/absent one reports nothing); **(4)** poison is separate — compute-path reads of invalid cells THROW (IF85) and an ERRORED computed value stores WITH its error mark, so neither is swallowed by the empty gate. *(Interpreter: `toComputed` converts a final empty rendered TEXT to the no-value CLEARED decision before the format check, and the cascade bookkeeping keys on the OUTCOME — an empty result is a not-decided tentative clear; locked `adapter.laws.EmptyConcatStoreDiffTest` (tri-check: the receipt's three divergent shapes + controls + the minLength gate-order arm, raw AND `kernelComputeGranularity`-projected) + multiplatform `interpreter … EmptyStringComputationStoreTest` + the string-concat compute-fuzz family `ComputationFuzz.stringModel` (mutation-checked: the gate disabled diverges on 17/200 (model, doc) pairs); INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF123, from the a12-kernel-lean receipt `string-computation-2026-07-15`.)*

**Multiple SEPARATE computations can target ONE field (composed models only) — the FIRST NON-EMPTY outcome wins, in document order (source + differential 2026-07-08).** Distinct from the *tiered* case above (one computation, many alternatives): a single flat model **REJECTS** two computations for one field (codegen error — differentially confirmed). But model **COMPOSITION** (includes / subtypes — a base calc + a subtype override living in *different groups*) yields a field with >1 computation, which the kernel accepts and evaluates. The combination: the field's computations run in **DOCUMENT (group-declaration) order**, and `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert` SETS the value **unless it is empty/not-relevant** — *"for empty values, do like no calculation was done"* (a no-op) — so the **first computation that yields a NON-EMPTY outcome** (a VALUE, or a POISON from an invalid-operand read) determines the field; an empty/clearing (precondition-false / no-matching-alternative) computation is skipped and control falls through to the next; if all clear → the field is CLEARED. Differentially confirmed (two sibling groups both computing one field): value-first wins over a later value AND over a later poison; an empty base calc falls through to a later value or poison; poison-first wins over a later value. *(Interpreter: `computeInstances` applies first-non-empty-wins per cell, `computationsInDependencyOrder` groups a field's computations without the old dedup; locked `adapter.laws.MultiComputationFieldDiffTest` + `interpreter … MultiComputationFieldTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF87.)*

**A computation is scoped to its COMPUTED FIELD, and a non-repeatable group is implicitly present (source + differential 2026-07-09).** A computation iterates over its **computed field's** repetition scope — where it *writes*, not the group it is *declared* in (a `*_Rules`/subtype container group can declare a computation whose `computedFieldRelPath` reaches into a different subtree). When the computed field has **no repeatable ancestor** (an all-non-repeatable target), the kernel computes exactly **one** instance — and it does so **regardless of instantiation**, because a non-repeatable group is always present (index 1). Differentially confirmed in BOTH strategies (groovy-dynamic + java-static): a constant computation declared in an uninstantiated container group targeting an all-non-repeatable field in another subtree computes its single value on an instantiated document AND on an empty one. *(Interpreter: the compute scope anchors to the target's parent group and `Document.computeContexts` yields the implicit single context for an all-non-repeatable uninstantiated scope — compute-local; locked `adapter.laws.CrossGroupConstantComputeScopeDiffTest` + `interpreter … CrossGroupConstantComputeScopeTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF89.)*

## §11 supplement — compute reporting granularity — VALUES are change-only (TYPED equality), CLEARED is input-filled-only, ERRORED is unconditional (source + probe, 2026-07-08)

> Fold: folded → §11.

What `compute()` *reports* is a **delta against the input document**, not a report-all (source: `DocumentComputationResultImpl.init` + the `IDocumentComputationResult` javadoc; probe-verified on the modern `compute(DocumentV2, config)` path):

- **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges` (→ VALUE)** — only instances whose computed value **differs from the input document's value at that cell**, compared as **typed values**: `fieldValuesEqual(originalV2Value, computedV2Value)` = `Objects.equals` (with a `Date[]` array special case). Two consequences: **(1)** on a **placement/harness-built** instance the cell values are raw *strings*, so the equality can hold **only for a STRING-kind computed target** (a number/date/boolean computes to `BigDecimal`/`Instant`/`Boolean` — never equal to the raw seed string) — probe: a scale-0 number seeded `20`, computing `20`, is **reported** (`String "20"` ≠ `BigDecimal 20`), while a string seeded `AB-X`, computing `AB-X`, is **absent**; **(2)** on a properly **typed** input (a kernel-deserialized document), `BigDecimal.equals` is **scale-sensitive**, so a computed `7.00` over a stored `7` is a *change* (the stored FORM is part of the equality) — this resolves the IG44-era granularity probe (its `VALUE(7)`-with-seed-`7` case was reported not for a form difference but because a number never types-equal a harness seed string).
- **`getClearedFieldInstances` (→ CLEARED)** — only instances that were **filled in the input document** (`getFilledComputedFieldInstances`, `valueExists`): clearing an already-empty computed cell reports nothing.
- **`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` (→ ERRORED)** — unconditional (not change-gated).

**Consequence for us:** `DmInterpreter.compute` deliberately reports **every** computed instance (report-all — the more useful library contract; result-envelope granularity is not behavior-parity territory, the IG47 BEST-gate ruling), so **every comparison surface** that diffs the interpreter against a kernel compute report projects the interpreter side first — `interpreter.conformance.kernelComputeGranularity`, used by the corpus capture/replay (JVM + JS), the real-corpus sweep, the model shrinker, and the compute fuzz oracle. *(Locked: `interpreter … conformance.ComputeGranularityTest` (JVM+JS) + `adapter.laws.CorpusCaptureTest` (string vs number seed-equal capture) + the committed corpus guard case `compute/order-name-seed-equal` (empty `computeExpected` must PASS). Contract: CONFORMANCE-CORPUS-SPEC §7.)*

## §9/§11 supplement — computations PARALLEL-ITERATE over sibling indexed groups; an invalid index anywhere marks every instance incorrectly calculated (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-09)

> Fold: folded → §11.

A computation whose operands reference OUT-OF-SCOPE sibling repeatable groups (all joined groups carrying the same-named INDEX field) runs the §9 parallel iteration: the target row keyed K reads each sibling's row with the SAME key — join-by-key, never physical row position (differentially pinned with scrambled row orders). An **unmatched side reads EMPTY** (empty-as-0 for a missing key row and for a wholly-uninstantiated side group — the calc still computes). Two calc-specific rules: the calc **never creates a repetition** (`CalculationController.isWiederholungGesetzt` — "no additional repetitions should be created by calculation"; a side-only key computes nothing), and an **INVALID index in ANY joined group** (a duplicated or empty key cell, target or side) marks **EVERY instance** of the computation "incorrectly calculated" — `CalculationCache.markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated`, deliberately *"marking more field instances as invalid than strictly necessary"* — so even clean-keyed instances CLEAR (and poison dependents). Both strategies agree (tri-checked). *(Interpreter: the compute path reuses the rule-side `parallelIterationContexts` anchored on the TARGET's scope + an `invalidIndexGroup` all-instances guard; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeParallelIterationDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … ComputeParallelIterationTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF96.)*

## §11/§12 supplement — validateFull does NOT run computations — validate and compute are separate operations (runtime, 2026-06-09)

> Fold: folded → §11.

The kernel keeps **validation** (`IDocumentRtService.validateFull`) and **computation** (`compute`) as distinct, independently-invoked operations — confirmed in the kernel dev docs (`a12-kernel/documentation/_merged/kernel-dev.md` §"Validating a document / Computations on a document": *"the use of validation and computation is very similar, therefore only one is described"*, and the worked sequence `validateFull(doc)` … `compute(doc)` … `result.appliedTo(doc)`). There is **no combined call**: `validateFull` validates the document's values **as-is** and does **not** compute computed fields first, and `compute` returns a *result* (the input is never mutated — applying it back via `appliedTo`/`apply` is a separate, opt-in step the consumer orchestrates).

**`applyTo` semantics — the apply-computed-back step (source: `IDocumentComputationResult`).** The third operation is `result.applyTo(doc)` → the **updated document** (`old + computed values`). Its javadoc is precise: it **applies the `getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges` values and CLEARS** the instances in **both** `getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` **and** `getClearedFieldInstances`. So an **errored** computed value is *emptied* on apply, not carried as the bad value — same as a cleared one; only a clean VALUE is set. (`applyTo(DocumentV2)` returns a new doc, original unchanged; the deprecated `applyTo(IDocument)` mutates in place.)

**The post-application cell STATE, per result kind (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-17; IG72 → IF126).** What `applyTo(DocumentV2)` *leaves in the applied document* — the per-cell refinement of the paragraph above, and the contract behind the capture boundary's `appliedState` channel and the interpreter's `Document.cellStateAt`. Mechanism (source: `DocumentComputationResultImpl.applyTo(DocumentV2)` folds cleared → errored → changed instances through `ComputedFieldInstanceImpl.applyTo(DocumentV2)`, which writes via `DocumentV2.withFieldValue` → `GroupDocCommonImpl.updateNested`/`withDirectFieldValue`; strategy-independent — `DocumentDynamicRtService` and `DocumentStaticRtService` share `DocumentAbstractRtService.compute` → `LowKernelApiUtils.callCalculator` → the same result impl):

| result kind (target's input state) | applyTo action | post-application state |
|---|---|---|
| VALUE, typed-unequal (`WithChanges`) — target **absent** | `withFieldValue(ptr, v)`: `updateNested` **creates missing ancestor rows**, `FieldInstanceV2.ofValue(v)` creates the cell | **present-value(v)** |
| VALUE, typed-unequal — target present (empty or stale) | `field.withValue(v)` | **present-value(v)** |
| VALUE **typed-equal** to the stored one (`WithoutErrors` only) | **not applied** (only `WithChanges` is) — untouched | present-value (indistinguishable from an applied write) |
| CLEARED (minted **only** for an input-FILLED cell — `getFilledComputedFieldInstances`, `valueExists` = non-null and non-`""`) | unguarded `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` — the instance **stays, value nulled** | **present-empty** — a clear is never a removal |
| computed-nothing — target present-EMPTY | **no instance minted** (not input-filled) → untouched | present-empty |
| computed-nothing — target **absent** | no instance minted → untouched | **absent** (the Set Y receipt's silence, as a state) |
| ERRORED (unconditional; non-empty computed value + error message) — target present | **guarded** `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` | **present-empty** |
| ERRORED — target **absent** | the guard `document.field(ptr) != null` fails → document returned **unchanged** | **absent** |
| untouched cells | never visited | placement state preserved (a stored `\r\n` stays — the IF124 normalization is eval-cache-side only, never the store) |

The errored branch's guard exists precisely because `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` would otherwise *create* a present-empty instance (`withDirectFieldValue` creates even for a null value) — so "errored is emptied like cleared" (above) holds only for a *present* target; an absent one stays absent. (The deprecated V1 `applyTo(IDocument)` clears errored instances *unguarded* via `DocumentUtils.clearFieldInstanceValue` — a V1-route difference outside the supported `DocumentV2` contract.) *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.applyComputations` → `Document.withComputed` reproduces every row of the table with no internal-store change — a null override empties a present placement and no-ops on an absent one; read back via `Document.cellStateAt` (IF126). Locked: `adapter.laws.AppliedCellStateDiffTest` — tri-check groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter on all nine shapes + the CRLF raw read — + multiplatform `interpreter … CellStateAtTest`; the capture boundary's kernel-route read is `KernelRouteSupport.appliedStateAt`, guarded by `AppliedStateDistinctionTest`.)*

**Consequence for us:** a *bare* `validateFull` sees a **computed field** as whatever the instance supplies — **empty** if absent (verified: `FieldFilled(CoverageWindow)` does **not** fire when only the `DateRange` operands are present), so a required-or-referenced computed field false-fires `mandatoryField` / misses its value. The **form-engine** answer applies the computation first. So our runtime-validate verbs **compose by default**: `model eval` / `rule eval` run **compute → applyTo → validateFull** (the form-engine flow), and **`--no-computations`** drops to the bare `validateFull`. The interpreter mirrors `applyTo` as the clean-room `DmInterpreter.applyComputations(doc)` (VALUE set, ERRORED/CLEARED emptied — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF45), the kernel side (the differential oracle) as `KernelAdapter.evaluateApplied`. `model compute` stays the pure `compute` (what does a field compute to?); the document file is never mutated by any verb (read-only — `compute`/eval report, never write `--instance`). The **engine-level** `DmInterpreter.validate == validateFull` parity stays foundational (the differentials rest on it); the *composed* path has its own differential. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EvalDocSeamLawsTest.validationDoesNotRunComputations` (the engine split) + `adapter.laws.ComputeApplyDiffTest` (interp compute-then-validate == kernel `compute().applyTo().validateFull()`).)*

## §12 supplement — Full vs Partial validation — `model eval` is FULL; partial may skip `*`-all-reps + `Having` rules (BA appendix, prose-only, 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §12.

The kernel has two validation modes (BA "Full Validation and Partial Validation", L5303-5321). **Full validation** — our `model eval` = `validateFull` — formal-validates all fields and evaluates every rule. **Partial validation** is a page-level pre-check over an app-specified *relevant-field* set: it guarantees never to report an error fixable only *outside* that set, but **does NOT guarantee complete checking of the relevant fields** — some checks are skipped (performance/simplicity). Named skip examples: rules referencing **all repetitions** via `*` when only some are relevant (explicitly incl. `NotAllFieldsFilled`, `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled`), and **`Having`-filter** rules. The exact skip-set is an **unstable implementation detail** (may change any release; projects must not depend on it). **Consequence:** every law we lock and every catalog example we ship is *full-validation* behaviour — a rule that fires under full validation may *not* fire under a partial page pre-check. **Prose-only by design** (the skip-set is explicitly unstable — nothing to formalize/lock). Related: the mandatory-"star" in a field's label (BA "the star method") is a *separate decidable heuristic* over a fixed allow-list of presence constructs — a UI affordance outside rulekit's scope.

## §3 supplement — a fired message's `getErrorText` is the *interpolated* end-user text (runtime, 2026-06-14)

> Fold: folded → §13.

`IMessage.getErrorText()` returns the message with its tokens already resolved against the evaluated document, not the authored template (`$<fieldname>$` → the display string, `$<field.value>$` → the display value; no other substitution). Only the runtime can produce it. Treatment: `KernelAdapter.evaluate` surfaces it per message; the CLI returns it as `data.messages[].message` / `data.message` (FEATURE-GAPS G1). *(Locked: `adapter.MessageInterpolationTest`; CLI surfacing by `cli.RuleTestVerbTest.firesOnAViolatingInstance`.)*

## §3 supplement — `$field$` is the field's *display name*, and the kernel sources it from a caller-supplied `ILabelProvider` (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §13.

The token resolves via the caller-registered `ILabelProvider` (`IDocumentDynamicServiceConfig.getLabelProvider()`), not a direct model-label read; a faithful provider returns label-for-locale-else-name. With NO provider the kernel emits the `fullName/idx.idx` path+index debug fallback (verified); surveyed shipped models leave labels empty, so in practice the name shows. Treatment: both engines and the `RuntimeLaws` harness wire the label-for-locale-else-name resolver, so comparisons are label-to-label. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DisplayNameDiffTest`; value tier by `LocalizedMessageDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — a formal error silently suppresses the author's rule (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §3.

All four claims are canonical in §3 ("Suppression is branch-scoped — and it reaches presence checks"); the evidence skeleton:

- **The silent suppression** — a malformed value raises only the kernel's own formal-error code; a referencing author rule never fires. Observed codes: `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen`, `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen`, `fuehrendesBlank` — German internal identifiers, not `MVK_*`, locked as the observed contract. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FormalErrorLawsTest`.)*
- **The sentinel attribution** — a formal error reports `rulePath = "formalePruefung"`; an author rule's is its slash-path — the discriminator in a validation report. *(Verified 2026-06-09; locked: `cli.EvalDocVerbTest.aFormalErrorIsReportedAsAKernelCheck`.)*
- **Branch-scoped, not rule-scoped** (RT-semantics drain, probe-corrected 2026-06-10): `healthyTrue Or [broken] > 0` still fires; the `And` twin is absorbed. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FormalErrorScopeLawsTest`.)*
- **Even a presence check is UNKNOWN on a not-check-relevant field** (fuzz-caught 2026-06-24): `isWertAngegeben` backs `FieldFilled`/`FieldNotFilled` AND the fill quantifiers, so a malformed operand makes them undecidable — the quantifier tally counts UNKNOWN in neither bucket. *(Interpreter fixed at the shared root — `filledTri` + the 3-state `quantCount`; locked: `adapter.laws.CurrentRepetitionDiffTest.malformedErrorFieldAtFiringRow` + `QuantifierMalformedDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — two more NUMBER sign formal errors: signed-zero and leading-plus (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §3.

Surfaced by the **differential fuzz net** (`adapter.perf.InterpreterKernelFuzzTest` — random valid documents, assert interp == kernel), which fed a `-0`:

- **A signed zero is a formal error** — `-0`, `-0.0`, `-0.00` (any minus sign on a zero magnitude) → **`zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen`**. A non-zero negative (`-5`, `-0.01`) and a plain zero (`0`, `0.00`) are clean.
- **A leading plus is a formal error** — `+5`, `+0`, `+0.5` → **`zahlMitPlus`** (distinct from `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen`; the explicit `+` is its own error, not "invalid characters").
- **Ordering: signed-zero is checked BEFORE the decimal-separator/scale checks.** `-0.0` on a **scale-0** field is `zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen`, *not* `zahlHatDezimalTrenner` — so a malformed value can match several checks and the kernel's precedence matters. (Leading-plus likewise wins over signed-zero: `+0` → `zahlMitPlus`.)

Both ride `formalePruefung` (the sentinel rule path) like the other formal errors, and suppress a referencing author rule (branch-scoped). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.NumberSignFormalDiffTest` — interp vs kernel over a scale-2 and a scale-0 field; the precedence is part of the lock.)*

## §3 supplement — BASELINE value checks run for EVERY kind before the type format: charset + leading/trailing blank (probe + source, 2026-06-26)

> Fold: folded → §3.

(The charset half reconciles with the "`modelConfig.supportedCharacters` is enforced at runtime" finding below: the *model-level* slot IS DM-JSON-declarable and runtime-enforced; what is not declarable is a *per-field* charset — the BMP default below is the no-slot case.) `FormalChecker.checkFieldValueFormat` runs three baseline checks on **every filled field value**, regardless of kind (string/number/date/…), **before** the type-specific format check — and they **pre-empt** it:

- **legal charset** → **`ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz`**. The legal set is `metaData.getLegalCodepoints()` — a **programming-language/codegen** config (`MetaModelImpl`, the codegen target's restricted charset; cf. the codegen `MVK_INVALID_CHAR_IN_RESTRICTED_CHARSET` "not permitted for the programming language"). It is **NOT a DM-JSON field/model property** (no `charset` key on `StringType`/`IStringType` in 30.8.1). With none configured — a plain DM-JSON model — the **default legal charset is the BMP** (`0x0000-0xFFFF`; `CharsetUtil`: "empty list ≡ 0x0000-0xFFFF"). So **any supplementary-plane character** (a UTF-16 surrogate, e.g. an emoji `😀` U+1F600) is illegal; BMP chars (`é`/`ü`/`€`/`™`/CJK) are legal. Probe-confirmed on a plain string field.
- **leading blank** → **`fuehrendesBlank`**; **trailing blank** → **`nachfolgendesBlank`** (distinct codes). Probe-confirmed across STRING, NUMBER, and DATE — and they pre-empt the type check (a ` 2024-01-01` DATE is `fuehrendesBlank`, not a date-format error).

Order: charset → leading → trailing → (type format). This **corrects** the earlier suspect §3 "string charset is not DM-JSON-declarable / no serialized key" note — the basis was about a per-field `StringType.charset` key (which indeed doesn't exist), but the runtime check fires anyway from the **default BMP** charset; the conclusion ("out of scope") was WRONG.

**Is the charset customizable? Yes — but at the CODEGEN/PROJECT level, not via the DM model.** A restricted charset *can* be declared (`KernelUtils.validateCharset`/`simplifyCharset` process a `restrictedCharset`), and the legal codepoints are **baked into the generated meta-model** by the codegen templates (`CodeGenMetaModel.st`) from the project's programming-language config — they do NOT travel with the DM-JSON model. The real-world case is a standard restricted set such as **DIN 91379** (the EU public-administration / e-government character set for names & addresses) — far NARROWER than the BMP: it would make `中文`/many symbols illegal. The probe confirms a DM-JSON model gets the **full-BMP default, NOT DIN** (`é`/`ü`/`€`/`™`/`中文` all legal), so a project-configured DIN charset is genuinely a separate input. A model loaded purely from DM-JSON (the interpreter's path **and** the dynamic-codegen `RuntimeLaws` differential) gets the BMP default, and the interpreter's BMP check matches the kernel for every such model. **That trigger has FIRED:** the interpreter carries the **`LegalCharset` SPI** (IF43 — default BMP, matching the DM-JSON-only kernel; a consumer-supplied set such as DIN 91379 is enforced instead), the `legalCodepoints` half of the customization-surface parity rule. *(Interpreter: a single cross-kind `baselineValueError` in `eval/FormalError.kt`, IF43; locked `adapter.laws.BaselineValueFormalDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — DECLARED field-type CONSTRAINTS are formal errors too (range / length / pattern / digit-count / enum-domain), source + probe-verified 2026-06-25

> Fold: folded → §3.

The format checks above (decimals, invalid chars, blanks, signed-zero) test whether a value is *well-formed for its kind*. But the kernel's `validateFull` **also** auto-validates the value against the field type's **declared constraints** — and these ride the **same `formalePruefung` mechanism**: an out-of-constraint value is a formal error, the field becomes **"unknown"**, and a referencing author rule is **suppressed** (branch-scoped, §3 / IF17). No rule is involved — the kernel emits these purely from the field-type declaration.

The constraints are declared on the field type (kernel `IStringType` / `INumberType`, source-read) and the formal-error codes are the kernel's full runtime set (`FormalErrorCodesProvider.getFormalErrorCodes`, ~55 codes). The constraint → code map (the ones a DM-JSON model can declare and a document can violate):

| field type | DM-JSON declaration | runtime formal-error code |
|---|---|---|
| **NUMBER** | `minValue` / `maxValue` | `zahlZuKlein` / `zahlZuGross` |
| | `minFractionalDigits` (too few / none) | `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen` / `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` |
| | `zeroNotAllowed: true` *(inverted key — NOT `zeroAllowed`)* | `zahlIstNull` |
| | **leading zeros** — forbidden BY DEFAULT; `leadingZerosAllowed: true` opts out | `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen` |
| | **> 15 TOTAL digits** (int + frac, sign/separator excluded) — a GENERIC parse check on EVERY number, not a declaration | `stelligkeitZuLang` |
| | `maxIntegerDigits`, OR `trait:"amount"`'s implicit `15 − scale` cap (only without explicit `maxIntegerDigits`) — the kernel's one `maxDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace` | `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` |
| | `minLength` / `maxLength` (value-string length) | `zahlZuKurz` / `zahlZuLang` |
| **STRING** | `pattern` (regex) | `stringFalschesMuster` |
| | `noValueValidation: true` — disables the WHOLE string check (pattern/length/newline) | *(skips all — only `maxLength` may accompany it, and it is not enforced)* |
| | `minLength` / `maxLength` | `stringZuKurz` / `stringZuLang` |
| | **newline** — forbidden BY DEFAULT; `lineBreaksPermitted: true` opts out | `stringMitNewline` |
| **ENUMERATION** | the value domain (out-of-domain stored value) | `stringFalschesMuster` |

**Two are kernel DEFAULTS, not declarations** (follow-up probe 2026-06-26): a plain number rejects a leading zero (`"007"` → `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`; `"0"`/`"0.50"` clean) and a plain string rejects a newline (`"a\nb"` → `stringMitNewline`) unless the field opts in.
