**More NumberType integer-digit / length constraints DO serialize (corrected 2026-06-26; the `stelligkeitZuLang`/amount attribution CORRECTED again 2026-07-07 — see the digit-check supplement below):** `maxIntegerDigits` → `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` (integer-digit count above the cap; sign + decimals excluded); `trait:"amount"` → an implicit integer-digit cap of `15 − scale` **firing the SAME `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`** (only without explicit `maxIntegerDigits`); NumberType `minLength`/`maxLength` → `zahlZuKurz`/`zahlZuLang` on the value-string length (sign + decimal point counted). All modeled by the interpreter (IF30/IF67). **Still out of scope (probe-confirmed):** a **charset** check (`zeichenNichtImZeichensatz`) is not declarable and does not fire by default (tab / non-ASCII / control chars on a plain string are all clean); a field-level `uniqueValue` is not DM-JSON-deserializable.

**Probe-verified (2026-06-25/26)**: `/Order/BirthDay` (`minValue:1, maxValue:31`) — `"99"` → `zahlZuGross`, `"0"` → `zahlZuKlein`; `/Order/Priority` (enum) — `"BOGUS"` → `stringFalschesMuster`; precedence — string pattern/newline fire **before** length; the **suppression** was confirmed (a rule `[BirthDay] == 99` with `BirthDay = "99"` does **not** fire — only `zahlZuGross`, the field is unknown). So an out-of-constraint value is observable two ways: the **auto-emitted formal error** *and* the **suppression of referencing rules**.

This is the same class as §94/§98 (format-error suppression) — only the trigger differs (a declared constraint / kernel default vs raw well-formedness). The **interpreter now models this dimension** (the per-cell family + the two defaults) — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF20, differential-locked by `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest`.

## §3 supplement — CRLF is normalized to LF at EVAL INGESTION — the format measure, rule reads, and compute reads all see `\n` (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-15; feedback F12 → IG70 → IF124)

> Fold: folded → §3.

One normalization, `RuntimeUtils.normalizeLinebreaks` = replace the `"\r\n"` PAIR with `"\n"` (a **lone CR stays**), applied at two source loci with one observable effect — *every evaluation-side read of a valid string value sees the normalized text*:

- **Inside the String format check** (`FormatDefinitionString.validiereFormat`), the clause order is: `noValueValidation` → full-check gate → **line-break permission on the RAW text** (`stringMitNewline`; the gate applies to ENUM fields too — the enum format-definition constructor hard-codes `lineBreakAllowed = false` — so a break-carrying out-of-domain value is the NEWLINE, never `stringFalschesMuster`) → **normalize** → pattern XOR enum-domain (on the normalized text) → min/max length (on the normalized text). So a permitted `"AB\r\nCD"` measures **5** units, not the raw 6: `maxLength 5` is clean, `minLength 6` fires `stringZuKurz`, and a `AB\sCD` pattern (exactly one whitespace char) matches. (A pattern whose own text contains a literal line break is model-REJECTED — "Only values without line breaks are allowed" — so break-y patterns discriminate only via character classes.)
- **At the formal-check stage of every run** (`CheckCommand` → `FormalChecker.checkAndConvertFieldValue`), a checked-and-valid value enters the eval caches as its **`normalizedValue()`** — which **refuted F12's "measure-only" hypothesis**: a RULE read is normalized too (`Length([F]) > 5` over the permitted CRLF does NOT fire while `> 4` DOES — a real normalized 5-read, not a suppression), and a COMPUTATION read is normalized — a bare copy of the CRLF source **stores** `"AB\nCD"` (the stored computed value witnesses the read form). Both strategies agree on every arm. (A **`noValueValidation`** field is OUT of this class — its rule read never surfaces the stored text at all: value operations are model-rejected `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`, `Length` is authorable only as `Length(f) > <constant>` (`MVK_INVALID_LENGTH_OF_RAW_TYPE`) and is unconditionally non-evaluable at runtime (silent even at `> -1`, filled or empty; presence checks work normally) — the raw-type ELIMINATION mechanism, the §3 raw-type supplement below / IF125.)

*(Interpreter: the twin locus is the `Document` eval-INDEX ingestion — value-bearing placements are CRLF-normalized once at index build, so the format measure (`stringConstraintError`, whose newline gate now also precedes the enum-domain arm), rule operands, compute operands, and `$field.value$` interpolation all read `\n`, while the consumer's raw placements stay untouched (`copy()` reproduces them verbatim; the compute delta projection keeps comparing against the RAW seed, matching the kernel's change-vs-input-document equality). Locked `adapter.laws.CrlfLengthNormalizationDiffTest` — 12 tri-check arms: the min/max boundary pair, LF + lone-CR controls, the no-breaks clause-order control, the pattern + enum arms, the Length-rule read (no-fire `> 5` AND fire `> 4` — the positive discriminator), the compute copy — + multiplatform `interpreter … CrlfNormalizationTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF124.)*

## §3 supplement — raw-type (`noValueValidation`) fields: EVERY value window is model-closed; the one allowed `Length` shape is an ELIMINATED max-length declaration (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-15; IG71 → IF125)

> Fold: folded → §3.

A `noValueValidation` STRING field (nvv is an `IStringType`-only property — `FieldUtils.isNoValueValidation` returns false for every other kind, enums included) is a **raw-type**: its value exists in the document but is **never interned for evaluation** — the formal-check stage stores `ValidationCache.PSEUDO_VALUE` for it (`CheckCommand`: *"considered valid, but the value should not be interned"*) instead of the IF124 `normalizedValue()`. The kernel then closes **every value window at the MODEL tier**, so no legal rule or computation ever reads the value:

- a **value operation** anywhere — a comparison `[nvv] == "…"`, a value list, a computation operand (bare copy, concatenation), `FieldValueAsNumber` — is rejected **`MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`** (*"It is not allowed to use a field which defines 'no value validation'"*; the parser's `checkIsValueValidation`, called from every value-position check impl — field-value ops, value lists, semantic index, `Valid(f, type)`, `FieldValuesNotUnique`);
- **`Length`** is authorable **only as the WHOLE rule condition** `Length(f) > <constant>` / mirrored `<constant> < Length(f)` — strict GT/LT only (`AnalyseService.getNameAndLengthOfAllowedValueValidation`); any other shape — `>=`, a surrounding `And`, a computation operation, even the allowed comparison as a computation **precondition** — is **`MVK_INVALID_LENGTH_OF_RAW_TYPE`** (the shape gate is `ParserService.checkNormalRule`, rules only; computation snippets reject `Length(nvv)` outright);
- **`$field.value$` message interpolation** of an nvv field is rejected **`INVALID_NO_VALUE_VALIDATION`** (*"the reference to its value is not allowed"*).

**The one allowed shape is not a rule — it is a disguised MAX-LENGTH declaration.** `ValidationCodeGenerator.handleLengthOfAllowedValueValidationRule` **ELIMINATES the rule** from the generated validation (both strategies — the shared generator, not a template) and lifts its `(field, c)` into the generated meta-model's max length (`addMaxLengthDefinedByRules` → `CodeGenStringTypeImpl.maxLaenge`/`maxLaengePrintable` — a forms/host metadata concern; the runtime `validiereFormat` still short-circuits on nvv, IF29, so nothing fires at runtime either way). Differentially pinned in BOTH strategies: `Length(nvv) > 5` / `> -1` / mirrored `-1 < Length(nvv)` are **silent** on filled and empty values alike, in full AND partial validation — while **presence semantics stay ordinary** (the pseudo-interned cell is content: `FieldFilled` fires, quantifiers and `NumberOfFilledFields` counts over nvv cells behave normally). The facade exposes the same extraction as `RuleCollectionMetaInformation.getNoValidationLength` (tool/editor metadata).

*(Interpreter: the mechanism-exact twin is RULE ELIMINATION, not a per-read np value state — `DmInterpreter.rowOutcomes` returns no rows for the eliminated shape (`eliminatedNvvMetaLengthRule`: whole-condition GT/LT-mirrored `Length` of a `noValueValidation`-constrained field vs a numeric literal), so it never fires and `testRule` reports PASSED; presence/content paths untouched. On kernel-legal inputs every other value read is unreachable (the MVK gates above), so no `Val` change is needed. Locked `adapter.laws.NvvRawTypeDiffTest` (tri-check: the three eliminated arms, presence, quantifiers, the count aggregate, partial validation) + `validate.laws.NvvRawTypeLawsTest` (the accept/reject tier incl. the `>=` precision arm and the computation rejects) + multiplatform `interpreter … NvvEliminatedLengthRuleTest` + the `ModelFuzz.nvvModel` fuzz family (mutation-checked: the gate disabled diverges on 26/200 (model, doc) pairs); INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF125.)*

## §3 supplement — the two DISTINCT number-digit checks: generic `stelligkeitZuLang` (> 15 total) vs the integer-cap `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` (source + probe-verified 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §3.

Corrects the earlier attribution (which wrongly gave `trait:"amount"` its own `stelligkeitZuLang` code). There are **two** independent digit checks, with different triggers, codes, and precedence:

| check | trigger | code | when it runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| **total-digit sanity** | > 15 **total** digits (integer + fractional, sign + decimal separator stripped) — on **EVERY** number, no declaration needed | `stelligkeitZuLang` | at **parse** (`NumberConversion.sanityCheck` / `FormatDefinitionZahl.validiereFormat`), **third** — right after the plus-sign + invalid-character checks, **before** sign / scale / leading-zero / integer-cap |
| **integer-digit cap** | integer-part digits > the cap, where the cap = explicit `maxIntegerDigits`, else `trait:"amount"`'s `15 − maxFractionalDigits` (the kernel's `TypeConverter` maps both to the one `maxDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace`) | `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` | later, in the full format check (only reached when total digits ≤ 15) |

**Precedence, probe-verified:** the total-digit check pre-empts leading-zeros, the scale-0 decimal-separator check, and signed-zero (e.g. `01234567890123456` → `stelligkeitZuLang`, not `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`). The **decisive** case that separates the two: amount `scale=5`, value `12345678901` (11 integer, 0 fractional, 11 total) → **`zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`** (11 > `15−5`), because total 11 ≤ 15 so the generic check does not fire. Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF67, differential-locked by `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` (+ multiplatform `interpreter.NumberDigitFormalTest`).

**STRING `noValueValidation` DISABLES the whole string format check** (source + probe-verified 2026-06-26). When `StringType.noValueValidation:true`, the kernel's `FormatDefinitionString.validiereFormat` returns `Valid` *immediately* — pattern, length, and the default newline check are all skipped. The key is MVK-constrained: it **forbids** `pattern`/`minLength` (`MVK_STRING_TYPE_PARAMS_INCONSISTENT` — only `maxLength` may accompany it) and **requires** `lineBreaksPermitted:true` (`MVK_STRING_TYPE_NO_VAL_AND_NO_LINE_BREAK`). So on a *kernel-valid* model the single observable runtime effect is that a declared **`maxLength` is NOT enforced** (a longer value is clean). Reachable on shipped A12 Tools StringType fields (Country/City/Person). The interpreter models it — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF29.

## §3 supplement — the full NUMBER formal-error PRECEDENCE order (source `FormatDefinitionZahl.validiereFormat`, 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §3.

The number format checks fire in ONE fixed sequence (the kernel returns on the first failure), so a value matching several reports the FIRST in this order. Extends the digit-check note above with the whole ladder:

| # | check | code | trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | leading plus | `zahlMitPlus` | a `+` sign |
| 2 | invalid chars | `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen` | not a number |
| 3 | > 15 total digits | `stelligkeitZuLang` | int+frac digits > 15 (fires even pre-`fullCheck`) |
| 4 | signed zero | `zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen` | `-0` on a signed, zero-allowed field |
| 5 | minus on unsigned | `zahlEnthaeltVorzeichen` | a `-` on a `positivesOnly` field |
| 6 | separator in integer field | `zahlHatDezimalTrenner` | a `.` where scale = 0 |
| 7 | missing separator | `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` | `minFractionalDigits` > 0 but no `.` |
| 8 | wrong # decimals | `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen` | too few OR too many fractional digits |
| 9 | integer-digit cap | `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` | integer digits > `maxIntegerDigits` / amount `15−scale` |
| 10 | zero not allowed | `zahlIstNull` | value == 0, `zeroNotAllowed` |
| 11 | leading zeros | `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen` | `07`/`00`/… — the **LAST** format check |
| 12 | value too short | `zahlZuKurz` | string length < `minLength` |
| 13 | value too long | `zahlZuLang` | string length > `maxLength` |
| 14 | below min | `zahlZuKlein` | value < `minValue` |
| 15 | above max | `zahlZuGross` | value > `maxValue` |

Two traps this order encodes (probe-verified, IF70): **zero-not-allowed (10) fires AFTER the decimal (7-8) and integer-cap (9) checks** — so a `0` on a `zeroNotAllowed`+`minFractionalDigits` field is `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner`, not `zahlIstNull`; and **leading-zeros (11) is the LAST format check** — every earlier check beats it (`07.5` on a scale-0 field is `zahlHatDezimalTrenner`, not `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`; `0007` over an integer cap is `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`). Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF70 (reordered `numberConstraintError` to this sequence, moved `leadingZeros` out of the parse-level `numberFormalError`), differential-locked by `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` (the precedence matrix, each case pinning the kernel winner) + multiplatform `interpreter.NumberDigitFormalTest`.

## §3 supplement — a DATE value is stored and formal-checked in the field's DECLARED format, not ISO (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §6.

Also surfaced by the **fuzz net**. A `DateType` field's value is the date **rendered in that field's `format`** — `OrderDate` (`yyyy-MM-dd`) holds `2024-06-15`, but `ApproxDate` (`dd.MM.yyyy`) holds `15.06.2024`. The formal check is **against the declared format**: a value in the field's own format is well-formed; a value in a *different* format is `datumFormatFalsch` (verified both ways — `ApproxDate = "2024-06-15"` → `datumFormatFalsch`; `OrderDate = "15.06.2024"` → `datumFormatFalsch`). So there is **no single canonical wire format** — a consumer must read each field's `format` to parse/validate its dates (the same is already known for `DateRangeType`, IF12). The interpreter canonicalizes every date to ISO internally via the declared format. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.PlainDateDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — a DATE value has an always-on lower bound: `Constants.DATE_FROM = 1583-10-16` (source + differential, 2026-07-09)

> Fold: folded → §6.

A well-formed date VALUE that resolves to a **real calendar date before 1583-10-16** ("the begin of the gregorian calendar") is the formal error **`datumFalsch`** (`FEHLER_DATUM`) — for **every** DATE field, stored or computed, with **no** opt-in required. Source: `FormatDefinitionDatum.validiereFormat` calls `dateInvalid(date) = date.before(getDateFrom(Constants.DATE_FROM))` unconditionally (after the format/calendar parse), where `Constants.DATE_FROM = "16.10.1583"`. A **DATE_RANGE** floors its **start** the same way (`FormatDefinitionDatumBereich extends FormatDefinitionDatum`, `datumBereichDatumFalsch` / `FEHLER_DATUM_BEREICH_DATUM`; end ≥ start covers the end).

This is **distinct from the opt-in "younger than 1900" additional check** (`Constants.DATE_FROM_FOR_ADDITIONAL_CHECK = "01.01.1900"`, gated on the field's `Zusatzpruefung` meta = `IDateType.isYoungerThan1900Check()`, **default false**): the always-on floor is 1583-10-16, so **1899 is valid** while 1000 / 1583-10-15 are not. Boundary pinned exactly by the differential — 1583-10-15 errors, 1583-10-16 is valid (strict `Date.before`). A **DAY_OPTIONAL** date completes an omitted day to `01` **before** the floor check (`PartiallyKnownDatesUtil.replaceUnknownDateParts` runs ahead of `dateInvalid`), so `00.10.1583` (→ 1583-10-**01**) errors while `00.11.1583` (→ 1583-11-01) is valid — kernel-differential-pinned. A computed date below the floor is bucketed **with-errors** (`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors`) → reported ERRORED; a stored one is a `validateFull` formal error that **suppresses** a referencing rule. **Both kernel strategies apply it** (the runtime format-definition + the codegen `CodeGenDateTypeImpl` that wraps it — java-static tri-checked). Interpreter treatment + lock: [INTERPRETER-FINDINGS](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md) IF88. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputedDateGregorianFloorDiffTest`, both strategies.)*

## §6 supplement — the RUNTIME tolerates any date `format`, but AUTHORING (SME) accepts only 3 canonical ones (source-verified 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §6.

(The runtime-lenient half is canonical in §6 — a value parses by its field's declared format; the SME-openability split below is the authoring/tooling evidence.) Two consumers read a date-ish field's `format`, and they disagree — a **runtime-lenient / authoring-strict** split that the conformance corpus tripped over when its models had to become SME-openable.

- **Kernel RUNTIME** (`IDocumentRtService`, the JVM differential's oracle): `format` is *the pattern to parse stored values with*. Any pattern works — `dd.MM.yyyy` is a first-class German format (`STANDARD_DE_DATE_FORMAT`/`DATUM_FORMAT` in the runtime), so a field declaring `format: "dd.MM.yyyy"` with values like `15.06.2024` validates and evaluates normally. This is why our `order` fixture's `ApproxDate`/`ItemCoverage`/`CoverageWindow` (German format, `-` range separator) have always passed the differential (locked by the date DiffTests — legitimate coverage of the runtime's format flexibility; see the §3-supplement finding above).
- **AUTHORING / SME** (the `simple-model-editor` model deserializer): `format` is *an enum of the canonical A12 storage formats*. Its field-type mapping is a hard `switch` that accepts **only** `yyyy-MM-dd`, `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`, `HH:mm:ss` (for Date / DateTime+Time / DateRange alike) and otherwise **`throw new Error("unsupported date format: …")`** — so a model with `dd.MM.yyyy` in `format` *evaluates fine but cannot be opened in the SME*. A localized **display** format is not stored in `format` at all; it belongs in the sibling `notInDCustomFormat` (and a non-`/` range separator in `notInDCustomRangeSeparator`) — the A12 default range separator is `/` (kernel `FieldTypeConstants.A12_DATE_RANGE_SEPARATOR`). The mechanism is verified from the SME source and re-expressed here (license-safe — behavior, not code).

**Consequence for us.** Openability is a stricter bar than runtime-validity: `dmtool model describe` / `rule check` use the lenient runtime read path, so they are *not* a faithful SME proxy for this class (they accept `dd.MM.yyyy`). A model meant to open in the modeling tools must keep `format` in the canonical set. The conformance corpus therefore **canonicalizes date `format`s when it writes a standalone model** (`CaseJson.writeModel` → `CANONICAL_DATE_FORMAT`) and a guard rejects any non-canonical one (`CorpusSchemaGuardTest` via `CaseJson.unsupportedDateFormats`); the shared *differential fixture* keeps its `dd.MM.yyyy` fields (they test the runtime and are never SME-opened). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.CorpusSchemaGuardTest`.)*

## §4 supplement — the "required" property and its two modes (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §4.

(The generated-rule shape, the content notion behind "parent filled", the index auto-requirement, and the branch-scoped unknown are canonical in §4 "The generated rule, precisely"; the DM-JSON mode encoding and probe detail stay here as evidence.) The field-editor "required" checkbox is encoded in DM-JSON as `Field.requirednessConfig: {mode: …}` (kernel enum `IRequirednessConfig.Mode`). An unfilled required field raises the `mandatoryField` **formal error**, so the field is **"unknown"** (§3). The two modes — and the gotcha the BA docs flag — verified at runtime:

| mode (DM-JSON) | BA "Only if parent filled" | when required |
|---|---|---|
| `absoluteOrRelativeToNextRepAncestor` | **no** | a non-repeatable field is **always** required; a field under a repeatable ancestor is required once that ancestor is filled |
| `relativeToParent` | **yes** | required **only when the parent group is filled** |

**The gotcha — what "filled" means here.** For `relativeToParent`, "the parent is filled" means **at least one descendant field of the parent is filled** (`IRequirednessConfig` Javadoc), **not** the §9 "row exists" `GroupFilled` notion. Verified on a sub-group field (`/Order/ShippingAddress/Street`): a created-but-empty `ShippingAddress` row → **not** required (no `mandatoryField`); once a sibling (`City`) is filled → required → empty Street raises `mandatoryField`. Under `absoluteOrRelativeToNextRepAncestor` the same empty sub-group still requires Street (always). The requiredness is **stronger than `FieldNotFilled`**: it makes the field unknown, so e.g. a required-but-empty **number** is *not* the §2 empty-as-0 — a `[Quantity] < 100` rule does not fire on it (only `mandatoryField` does). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.RequiredPropertyLawsTest`.)*

**Mechanism + four more facts (source-read `AutogeneratedRulesService`/`MandatoryFieldRuleImpl` + differential, 2026-06-23).** The kernel implements `mandatoryField` as an **autogenerated rule** named `vk_vk<field>_req`, error code `mandatoryField`, severity ERROR, **type OMISSION** (filling the field clears it), with the condition decided by `relativeTo = lowestRepeatableGroupPath(field)`:

1. **`absoluteOrRelativeToNextRepAncestor`, no repeatable ancestor** → `FieldNotFilled(field)` — **unconditional**: fires even on an *un-instantiated* document (an empty doc still raises `mandatoryField` at the canonical all-1s pointer; a not-instantiated non-repeatable sub-group still requires its absolute fields).
2. **…, with a repeatable ancestor R** → `GroupFilled(R) And FieldNotFilled(field)` — **per instantiated R row** (no row ⇒ no `mandatoryField`).
3. **`relativeToParent`** → `GroupFilled(parent) And FieldNotFilled(field)`.
4. **Index fields are auto-required** — a group's `indexFieldName` gets an auto-generated mandatory rule with `relativeTo = parent` (i.e. `relativeToParent` semantics), on top of any explicit requiredness. So an instantiated repeatable row with an empty index field fires `mandatoryField` on it.

The **gotcha resolves by the parent's repeatability** (the `GroupFilled` split, §9 below): for a **repeatable** parent a *created-but-empty* row already counts as filled (the row index is content) → the field IS required; for a **non-repeatable** parent a filled descendant counts. **What "a filled descendant" means was refined 2026-07-06 (runtime-probed): it includes an INSTANTIATED repeatable descendant ROW, not only a valued field** — `Quantity` (required `relativeToParent`, parent the non-repeatable `/Order`) becomes required as soon as a `/Order/Items` row is instantiated (even all-empty), and is *not* required when `/Order` has no content at all. So this is the SAME "content" notion as the §9 `GroupFilled` operator — requiredness and `GroupFilled` share it (they diverge only in that requiredness's *absolute* mode ignores it). And the "unknown" is **branch-scoped, not whole-rule** (§3 refinement): a required-empty operand makes its *comparison* unknown, so `[ReqNum] < 100` is suppressed but `[ReqNum] < 100 Or <true>` still fires. *(Interpreter parity locked: `adapter.laws.RequirednessDiffTest` — both modes, the repeatable-row gate incl. the instantiated-repeatable-child-fills-a-non-rep-parent case, the unconditional-absolute case, and the index field; the branch-scoped unknown for format errors by `adapter.laws.SuppressionDiffTest`.)*

## §4/§8 supplement — field-level custom error messages live in a per-locale `errorMessage` array (source-verified, 2026-06-21)

> Fold: not-foldable — a DM-JSON model-structure/serialization fact (where the custom texts live), outside the evaluation scope.

A field carries **two** kinds of custom validation error text, both the "Use Default Error Messages" deselected case (SME 840, 1123) — and both are stored the same way: a per-locale `errorMessage` array `[{locale, text}]`.

| message | DM-JSON location | kernel accessor |
|---|---|---|
| **required-field** (shown when a required field is empty) | `Field.requirednessConfig.errorMessage` | `IRequirednessConfig.getErrorMessage(Locale)` |
| **enum invalid-value** (shown for an out-of-domain value) | `EnumerationType.errorMessage` | (validated by `FieldErrorTextParser`) |

- **"Use default" = absence.** There is **no** explicit `useDefaultErrorMessage` boolean — omitting the `errorMessage` array uses the kernel default; providing it is the custom case. (So the editor checkbox maps to presence/absence, not a stored flag.)
- **Must cover every declared locale** (like enum value labels): a partial map is kernel-rejected. The required-message array also needs the field to *be* required (have a `requirednessConfig`).

Source-read from the kernel (`IField.IRequirednessConfig.getErrorMessage`, `FieldErrorTextParser.hasValidErrorTextInRequirednessConfig`); rulekit's treatment (the `requiredMessages` / `enum.errorMessages` field-spec keys, G23) is in KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES. *(Locked: `cli.field.FieldAddSpecTest` — per-locale enum + required messages round-trip; `modelkit.FieldConfigExtrasTest` reads the enum message back.)*

## §5 supplement — numeric literals are exempt from the scale check

> Fold: folded → §5.

The `==`/`!=` decimal-scale gate applies to **field/expression operands only**. Against a scale-2 field, `== 0`, `== 0.0`, `== 0.00`, `!= 0`, and `<= 0.000` are all accepted — only field-vs-field/expression comparisons of differing declared scale draw `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`. Consequence: scale-explicit literals (`0.00`) are a rendering-fidelity nicety, not a correctness requirement. *(Locked: `validate.laws.NumberLawsTest`.)*

**Field-vs-field comparison (finding 18, verified 2026-06-04).** Comparing two *fields* (not just field-vs-literal): **strings** compare with `==`/`!=` only (ordering → `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`, "only for numbers, amounts and dates"); **numbers** — the scale gate is **`==`/`!=`-only**: equal-scale fields compare freely, different-scale fields draw `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` ("max fractional digits of lhs(0) and rhs(2) differ"), but **ordering (`< <= > >=`) is scale-exempt** (verified: `[Quantity(0)] < [UnitPrice(2)]` validates; source `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl.checkNumberComparison` — the scale check runs only when `isUnEqualsComparison`, comparing the two `getMaxFractionalDigits`; a `null` (derived/unknown) scale is the `"?"` in the message, source-read 2026-06-17). String-vs-number → `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_ENUM_OR_STRING`. rulekit: `StringRef.eq/ne(StringRef)` and `NumberRef.{lt,le,gt,ge,eq,ne}(NumberRef)` — the number `eq`/`ne` **pre-check equal scale** (the D13 gate made cheap, since the index knows both scales), ordering does not. **Enum-vs-enum is now implemented** (`EnumRef.is/isNot(EnumRef)`, compared by stored value; type-compatibility stays the kernel's call) — KERNEL-SEMANTICS §8, locked by `adapter.EnumFieldComparisonReadTest`. An arithmetic **expression** carries a *derived* scale — **multiplication raises it** (a scale-2 × scale-2 expression is scale 4, division/power are unknown), so `[x] == [a] * [b]` can draw `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` **even when every field shares a scale**; wrap the expression in `RoundAccounting(expr, n)`, or waive the check with `@SuppressWarning(MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES)` at the start of the condition (source-read 2026-06-17). *(Also locked: `validate.laws.NumberLawsTest.fieldVsFieldScaleGateAppliesToEqualityOnly`/`stringFieldVsFieldEqualityIsAccepted`/`multiplicationRaisesScaleSoEqualityRejectsEvenWithMatchingFieldScales` + `RenderConditionTest`.)*

**Tolerance comparisons (implemented 2026-06-07).** The kernel's `ComparisonOperation` has four tolerance members — `NE1`/`NE2`/`NE5`/`NE10` — rendered as the worded keywords `DiffersWithToleranceRange1`/`2`/`5`/`10` (DE `UngleichMitToleranz{N}`): `[a] DiffersWithToleranceRange5 [b]` is true iff the two numbers differ by more than 5 ("unequal within a tolerance"). Only for numbers/amounts. Confirmed against the kernel source: `FormatterUtil.getComparisonOperator` renders them via the same comparison-operator path as `<`/`==` (so they ride the existing `ComparisonCondition` rendering). Modeled by extending `CompareOp` with the four variants + a public `Tolerance` enum (`RANGE_1/2/5/10`, making any other range unrepresentable); authored via `NumberExpr.differsWithTolerance(Tolerance, …)` / `NumberRef.differsWithTolerance(...)`. *(Locked: `adapter.ArithmeticReadTest.roundTripsToleranceComparison` + BA-corpus `ToleranceCmp`; N7 catalog examples for all four.)* **Read also converts a tolerance op in an EXPRESSION / AGGREGATE comparison** (not just field-vs-field) — the `numberExprComparison`/`exprVsExpr` paths route `NE1`/`NE2`/`NE5`/`NE10` through `differsWithTolerance` (`adapter.AggregateToleranceReadTest`, e.g. `Sum(Items*/Count) DiffersWithToleranceRange1 [Quantity]`).

**Runtime semantics (interpreter, probe 2026-06-24).** Fires iff `|a − b| > N` — the band is **STRICT**: a difference *exactly* equal to N does NOT fire (`Range1` on diff 1 → no, diff 2 → yes; `Range5` on diff 5 → no, diff 6 → yes). An **empty** numeric operand reads as **0** (empty-as-0, like any comparison — `[Quantity=9] DiffersWithToleranceRange1 [empty]` → `|9−0|=9 > 1` fires); a **malformed** operand suppresses the rule. Polarity is **EQ-like** (kernel-probed message type): both operands present → **VALUE**; an empty (fillable) operand whose filling could close the gap → **OMISSION**. The interpreter models it as a dedicated `Cond.Tolerance` AST node (NOT a desugar to `Abs([a]−[b]) > N`): the two differ in **polarity** — Tolerance is EQ-like (either operand moving closes the band), a `> N` desugar would be GT-like (only the left shrinking), so a desugar mis-types even now. *(Historically the desugar was also fill-INVISIBLE — `leafOmission` didn't recurse into `Expr.Arith`; that half is moot since IF69/IF71 made `fill` propagate through arithmetic AND `Abs`, but the EQ-vs-GT polarity gap stands, so the dedicated node remains correct.)* Polarity reuses the EQ `fill`-based rule. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ToleranceDiffTest` — firing + polarity in one assertion — + a fuzz rule `DT` = `[Qty] DiffersWithToleranceRange5 [Price]`.)*

## §5 supplement — a NUMBER field type is ALWAYS scale-bounded; `maxFractionalDigits` is mandatory (no unbounded decimal) (source + metamodel + probe, 2026-06-28)

> Fold: folded → §5.

An a12 `NumberType` carries a **mandatory** decimal scale — there is **no unbounded-scale decimal**. Three independent oracles agree:

- **Typed API** — `INumberType.getMaxFractionalDigits()` returns a **primitive `int`** (no `Optional`), unlike the genuinely-optional slots on the same interface (`getMinValue`/`getMaxValue`/`getMaxLength`/`getMaxNumberOfDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace` are all `Optional<>`). There is no "unset scale" in the model.
- **Metamodel** — `MM_SMM_FieldTypeChecks` states it in prose (";; maxFractionalDigits is a required Field in case of a number") and flags `NotAllFieldsFilled(.../maxFractionalDigits)` as an integration error for any Number-supertype field.
- **Behaviour (probe)** — a DM-JSON that **omits** the slot is **accepted**, with the scale **silently defaulting to 0** (Jackson fills the primitive `int` → 0) — never unbounded, never rejected.

Consequences: modelkit emitting `maxFractionalDigits` with a `null → 0` default is **correct** (it makes the implicit kernel default explicit), and a bare JSON-Schema `number` (no scale signal) has **no faithful import** — schemakit omits it by default rather than silently coercing a decimal to an integer (SCHEMAKIT-SPEC §4b). *(Locked: `modelkit.NumberScaleKernelLawTest`. This resolves the DEV-API-GAPS "unbounded-scale NUMBER" investigation — a kernel fact, not a modelkit gap.)*

## §5 supplement — power-operator parse laws + the quiet non-execution edges (source-read + 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §5.

The `^` power operator (parser `CheckOperationImpl`): **unparenthesized nesting is rejected** — `a ^ b ^ c` draws `MVK_TOO_MANY_POW_FOR_CALC` (nest via grouping); the **exponent must have no decimal places** — a scale-0 field or integer literal is accepted, a decimal-scale field (or an unknown-scale expression) draws `MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP` (`checkNoFractionalDigitsInExponent`). Arithmetic sub-expressions group with **curly braces `{ }`**, and precedence is `^` → `*`/`/` → `+`/`-`. **Runtime (BA "Power Operator", source-read but not yet law-locked here):** `a ^ b` is *not executed* — so a rule using it silently does not fire — when `b` is outside `[-1000, 1000]` or for `0` raised to a negative power, the same quiet behavior as division by zero. *(Locked: `validate.laws.NumberLawsTest.powerOperatorRejectsUnparenthesizedNesting` / `powerExponentMustHaveNoDecimalPlaces` for the validation side; the runtime non-execution edges are now locked by `adapter.laws.DivPowDiffTest` — `powZeroToNegativeIsQuiet` (`0` to a negative power) and `powExponentOutOfRangeIsQuiet` (`|exp| > 1000`), both quiet like division by zero, interp == kernel.)*

## §7 supplement — pattern-comparison slot rules and the portable-subset gate (2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: folded → §7.

(The enforced portable subset is canonical in §7; the slot rules below stay as authoring evidence.) `PatternMatched`/`PatternViolated` (DE: `WieMuster`/`NichtWieMuster`) are grammar-level *infix comparison operators* (`vglOp` alternatives like `==`), but their slot rules are far stricter than the grammar:

- **Left side: a bare string field value only.** A non-string field (number, enum, boolean) → **`MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`** ("only allowed for string values" — note: *enums are not pattern-comparable* even though they compare to strings with `==`). A string *expression* (e.g. concatenation) → **`MVK_INVALID_PARAMETER_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`** ("only allowed with a field value as first parameter"). A constant → the general **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_CONST`**.
- **Right side: a string constant only.** A field reference → **`MVK_MISSING_CONST_IN_PATTERN_COMP`**; a number constant → `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`.
- **The regex gate is Java `Pattern.compile` *plus* a portable-subset check** (`PatternUtils.isPatternValid`; the extmp world uses `CommonUtils.isRegExValid` instead): lookbehind `(?<=a)b` is Java-valid yet rejected with **`MVK_INVALID_PATTERN`** — KERNEL-SEMANTICS §7's "portable subset" is *enforced*, not advisory. Consequence: a Java-side `Pattern.compile` pre-check is a necessary condition only; final validity is the kernel's call.
- **The empty pattern `""` is accepted** for document models (the non-extmp branch; extmp additionally requires non-empty).

*(Locked: `validate.laws.PatternLawsTest` + N7 `PatternMatched`/`PatternViolated` examples.)*

## §7 supplement — string-literal escaping is quote-doubling, not backslashes

> Fold: not-foldable — concrete-syntax/grammar tier; the canonical doc defers grammar pitfalls to the origin project's grammar doc.

The condition lexer's rule is `StringLiteral : '"' ( '""' | ~["] )* '"'` — an embedded quote is **doubled** (`"say ""hi"""`), and **backslash is a plain character** (no escape sequences; regex classes like `\d` are written as-is). Backslash-escaping a quote (`\"`) is a parse error (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`). Found while building the pattern operators: rulekit's renderer had C-style escaping and would have emitted wrong regexes for any pattern containing `\` — fixed and locked. *(Locked: `validate.laws.PatternLawsTest` + `RenderConditionTest.quotesStringLiteralsTheKernelWay`.)*

## §8 supplement — a category's `description` is an editor-level note the kernel tolerates (probe + source, 2026-06-21)

> Fold: not-foldable — an editor/tooling model attribute the kernel ignores; no evaluation behavior.

An enumeration category's **optional `description`** (SME 1133 — "an optional description can be set") is **not** modeled by the kernel core: both `IEnumerationType.getCategoryValues()` and the a12internal `IEnumerationDataTable` expose categories as `name → values` only. A `description` key on a category node is **tolerated** by `checkConsistency` (probe: injected into a category, `model check` → `valid: true`, no diagnostic) — it's an editor/tool attribute the kernel ignores. Per the owner rule (*what's editable is part of the model, unless workspace-related*), it belongs **in the model** (the category node), not workspace settings — so rulekit writes it there (`EnumCategory.description`), preserved by the generic-tree round-trip and harmless to the kernel. *(Locked: `cli.field.FieldAddSpecTest.enumCategoryCarriesAnOptionalDescription`.)*

## §8 supplement — `FieldValueIncludedInValueList` rides the 3VL: an empty operand is UNKNOWN (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §2.

On an empty field the membership check is UNKNOWN, not "absent from the list" — NEITHER form fires (the `Not` form does not fire just because the field is empty); canonical in §2's override table (value-list membership row). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ValueListDiffTest`.)*

The **authoring-side** constraints are already in the MVK ledger, not repeated here: members are type-matched to the field — number field → number literals, string/enum → string literals; mixing them is `MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON` (ledger: *erased*), and a boolean/confirm field has no value-list (`MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED`, ledger: *erased*). For `GroupFilled`/`GroupNotFilled`: a repeatable group reference needs the `*` aggregate form (`MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, ledger: *kernel-delegated*) and the error field must be referenced — a `GroupFilled(G)` reference covers `G`'s members *indirectly* (`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`, ledger: *pre-checked*).

## §8 supplement — enum-category codes and the positional mapping

> Fold: folded → §8.

In DM-JSON, a category's `values` list pairs **positionally** with the enumeration's `values` (entry *i* categorizes enum value *i*); the comparison domain is the category's distinct value set. Codes: comparing to one of the *enum's own* values instead of a *category* value → **`MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON`**; an unknown category name → **`MVK_CATEGORY_UNKOWN`** (the kernel's spelling); `->` on a non-enumeration field → **`MVK_NO_ENUMERATION`**. Curiosity: `[Enum -> Category] == [Enum]` (category vs. plain enum field) is *accepted*. *(Locked: N7 `EnumCategory` examples + `dsl.operators.EnumConditionTest`.)*

**Runtime (2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain):** the many-to-one mapping fires for *every* value sharing the category value (`STANDARD,EXPRESS,OVERNIGHT → NORMAL,FAST,FAST`: both `EXPRESS` and `OVERNIGHT` satisfy `== "FAST"`, `STANDARD` does not), and an empty enum rides the not-evaluated track — the category hop doesn't change that. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EnumCategoryLawsTest`.)*

*(Interpreter, probe-reconfirmed 2026-06-24: `Expr.EnumCategory(path, category)`, parsed in the `[...]` atom via the `->` arrow (same symbol in every language). `EvalContext.enumCategory` maps the stored value to its category value by POSITION in the enum's value list (`FieldDef.enumValues`/`enumCategories`, loaded from `EnumerationType.{values,categories}`); the comparison is string-based. An **empty** enum → `Val.Unknown` (the empty-string compare rule → no fire); a value **not in the enum** → `Val.Unknown` (the kernel emits `stringFalschesMuster` and suppresses — the interpreter's out-of-enum→Unknown reproduces the same no-fire). Polarity is always VALUE when it fires (the enum is filled). Locked: `adapter.laws.EnumCategoryDiffTest` — EXPRESS/OVERNIGHT→FAST, STANDARD→NORMAL, empty/out-of-enum no-fire, the `!=` direction.)*

## §8 supplement — predefined-type validity (`ValidType`/`InvalidType`) is a runtime CLIENT SPI, not kernel-core behavior (probe + source, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: not-foldable — client-SPI registration/wiring mechanics (the operator form itself is canonical in §6/the field-types table).

`Valid(field, "EMail")` / `Invalid(field, "IBAN")` validate the field's value against a **predefined custom field type**. The type name is a free string-literal operand and is **NOT checked at `checkConsistency`** (the model accepts any string). The reason is structural: the predefined types are a **runtime client SPI** — `IPredefinedType` ("*intended to be implemented by clients … Predefined types are project specific … The runtime must include the code for the validation*"), with `ValidationStrategy ∈ {NONE, NOT_SUPPORTED, PROVIDED_VALIDATION_CODE, LENGTH_AND_PATTERN_CHECK}`. The codegen emits a call into client-provided runtime methods; the kernel CORE ships **no** "EMail"/"IBAN" validators.

**The differential needs a registered type — and CAN have one (probe-confirmed).** With NO registered `IPredefinedType` the bare kernel is a **degenerate no-op** — `Valid` AND `Invalid` **both fire on ANY filled value** (`alice@example.com` and `not-an-email` indistinguishable). But the kernel exposes a registration SPI, `CustomFieldTypeService.getInstanceV2().registerV2(factory)` (a `ServiceLoader`-free static registry of `ICustomFieldTypeFactory` → `ICustomFieldValidator`; the runtime path the oracle uses consults it). **Registering a factory for `"EMail"` makes the oracle do a REAL check** (probed: `Valid` fires only on a valid e-mail, `Invalid` only on an invalid one, empty → neither — VALUE polarity). The kernel core itself ships only the German set (`kernel-rt/kernel-core-customfieldtype-impl`: IBAN/BIC/Steuernummer — EUPL, reference-only, never vendored).

*(Interpreter, SUPPORTED 2026-06-24: the interpreter mirrors the client SPI with a **consumer-supplied registry** — `PredefinedType(minLength,maxLength,pattern)` (the kernel's `LENGTH_AND_PATTERN_CHECK`) keyed by name, injected into `DmInterpreter`, default the built-in `PredefinedTypeRegistry.DEFAULT` (`EMail`; no tax/gov). `Valid(field,"Name")`/`Invalid(...)` dispatch by arity off the date-construct `Valid`/`Invalid`; an EMPTY field is "not specified" → `UNKNOWN`/no-fire; an **unregistered** type name → the rule is reported **unsupported** (fail-fast), never a silent no-op. Differential-locked by `adapter.laws.PredefinedTypeValidityDiffTest`, which registers the SAME validator in BOTH engines via the SPI so the test verifies the operator semantics (firing/polarity/empty), not the regex. Checksum types (IBAN mod-97 — `PROVIDED_VALIDATION_CODE`) are a future function-valued-registry extension.)*

## CustomCondition — a runtime CLIENT SPI with TWO API levels; the interpreter must support it (probe + source, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §14.

(The runtime semantics — fires iff `check` returns true, on an evaluated row, VALUE polarity, formal-error suppression, the bare-form rejection — are canonical in §14; the SPI levels, registration, and oracle wiring below stay as the evidence and treatment record.) `CustomCondition <Name>` delegates the whole fire/don't-fire decision to **client/host code**, resolved by **name** at runtime (like the predefined types — a client SPI, not kernel-core behavior). The kernel's own metamodels use it heavily (MM_Rule/MM_Field/MM_Computation/MM_Typedef…), and the owner confirms real project models use it heavily — so the interpreter MUST support it (decided 2026-06-24).

**Two API levels** (this is the key fork):
- **Core / classic** — `com.mgmtp.a12.kernel.core.rt.a12internal.validation.ICustomCondition` (formerly `IApplicationCondition`): `boolean check(IData data, Set<IIdentifier> relevant, Set<IIdentifier> formallyIncorrect, IIdentifier errorField)`. `IData` = `getValue(IIdentifier)`/`getValueForDisplay`/`iterator`/`identifierToString`; `IIdentifier` = `getName()` (path, ≥2 parts) + `getIndices()` (long[] reps) + `getUsb()`. A constructible `IdentifierImpl(name, long[])` exists in `kernel-core-utils`. The kernel's own metamodel conditions (`CheckEnumOrCategoryValue`, `CheckStringPattern`, `CheckHintList`) implement THIS level.
- **Modern / md** — `com.mgmtp.a12.kernel.md.rt.api.ICustomCondition`: `check(DocumentV2, Set<DocumentMultiPointer> relevant, Set<DocumentPointer> formallyIncorrect, PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer errorEntity)`. `CustomConditionWrapper` (md-runtime-service) adapts md→core and **requires the runtime data be a `ValidationData`** ("a custom condition provided through the md-API must not be used by validation calls which bypass the md-API"). **The owner's projects target THIS (md/DocumentV2) level (decided 2026-06-24).**

**Registration:** the static `CustomConditionService.getInstanceV2().registerV2(ICustomConditionFactory)` (core-level; deprecated, `forRemoval` A12K-3589, but present in 30.8.1) OR the modern `DocumentProcessingConfig.builder(locale).customConditionFactory(...)` passed to `validateFull(DocumentV2, DocumentProcessingConfig)`. The facade `DocumentRtCustomExtensionService.registerCustomConditions(mdFactory)` wraps an md factory in `CustomConditionFactoryWrapper` and registers it into `CustomConditionService`. A factory maps name→condition (`createCustomConditionV2(name): Optional<ICustomCondition>`); first-registered name wins; `cleanRegistry()` for tests.

**Runtime semantics (probe-confirmed via constant conditions):** `CustomCondition X` fires iff `X.check(...)` returns **true**, on an **evaluated** row, and only when the relevant fields are **formally correct** (a formal error suppresses it); polarity is **VALUE**; it does **not** fire on an all-empty row. A **bare** `CustomCondition X` (referencing no field) is rejected at codegen with **`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`** — it must be guarded by something that references the error field (the idiom is `FieldFilled(errorField) And CustomCondition X`). DM-JSON: the condition text is literally `… And CustomCondition <Name>` (e.g. `FieldFilled(errorField) And CustomCondition InvalidReference`).

**Oracle-harness path (for the differential) — CONFIRMED 2026-06-24.** Registering a *core* `ICustomCondition` via the static `CustomConditionService` and validating through the deprecated `validateFull(IDocument, Locale)` path invoked **constant** conditions reliably but **not data-reading ones** in the test harness (a throwing condition never threw, yet the rule fired). The reliable path — now wired into `RuntimeLaws` as the `messages/fires/typeOf(modelJson, Doc, Map<String, ICustomCondition>)` overloads — is the **modern** `validateFull(DocumentV2, DocumentProcessingConfig.builder(locale).customConditionFactory(ICustomConditionFactory.fromMap(name→mdCondition)).build())`: convert the built V1 `IDocument` to `DocumentV2` via `new DocumentServiceFactory(resolver).createDocumentV1V2Converter().ofDocumentV1(doc)`. A probe confirmed it **reliably invokes data-reading md conditions** (ACME→fire VALUE, Other→read+no-fire, empty→**not even invoked**). The md `ICustomCondition` may implement the modern `check(DocumentV2,…)` or the deprecated `check(IDocument,…)` (the default V2 method converts and delegates) — the oracle reads the error field from `IDocument.getEntityInstances()` filtered to the matching `IFieldInstance.getValue()`. `kernel-core-runtime-api` resolves to **32.3.4** in the 30.8.1 distribution; `kernel-core-utils` 30.8.1. md (`DocumentProcessingConfig`/`ICustomConditionFactory`/md `ICustomCondition`/`DocumentV2`) is already on the adapter classpath via `kernel-md-runtime-api`.

**JVM bridge for EXISTING kernel conditions — `KernelCustomConditions.fromCore` (`:adapter`, 2026-06-24).** A JVM consumer adapts a project's existing kernel **core** `ICustomCondition` (or a `name→condition` map) onto the interpreter's pure `CustomCondition` SPI, so the kernel-free interpreter runs the SAME condition object the kernel runs — replacing the kernel's generated code. The bridge supplies its **own** `IData`/`IIdentifier` over the interpreter's `CustomConditionData`/`FieldPointer` (`FieldPointer.indices: List<Int>` ⇄ `IIdentifier.getIndices(): long[]`), constructs **no** kernel `…Impl` (the kernel API documents the caller provides these — with content `equals`/`hashCode` — and the runtime re-wraps them; honors the N8/D15 "never construct kernel Impl" guardrail), and refuses mutation (`IData.addValue` throws — a condition must not mutate the data). This made `:interpreter` an `:adapter` **MAIN** dep and added `kernel-core-runtime-api:32.3.4` (`implementation`). The md `DocumentV2`-shaped conditions are reachable via a thin future `DocumentV2`→`IData` view on top of this core bridge — not built, since core `IData` is the unified cross-platform contract. Locked: `CustomConditionDiffTest` (the real-core-condition-through-the-bridge differential) + `KernelCustomConditionsTest` (bridge data-shaping).

**The JS/TS kernel runtime mirrors the `IData`-based contract EXACTLY — so `IData`-based is the UNIFIED cross-platform contract (re-checked 2026-06-24 at the owner's prompt).** The kernel ships a TypeScript runtime (`kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime-ts` + `kernel-core-runtime-api-ts`) whose JS codegen path DOES support custom conditions, via TS twins of the **core** API: `ICustomCondition.check(data: IData, relevant: Set<IIdentifier>|undefined, formallyIncorrect: Set<IIdentifier>, errorField: IIdentifier): boolean`, `IData.getValue(IIdentifier)`/`iterator`/`addValue`, `IIdentifier.getName()/getIndices()/getUsb()`, and a `CustomConditionService` singleton (`register`/`getCustomCondition`/`cleanRegistry`) + `ICustomConditionFactory.createCustomCondition(name)` — **identical in shape to the Java core API**. There is **no `DocumentV2` in TypeScript** — `DocumentV2` is a JVM-only modern facade (`CustomConditionWrapper` adapts md→core). So the contract that exists on **both** platforms (and that the kernel's own conditions + JS codegen use) is the **`IData`-based** one — the natural, only fully cross-platform choice for a JVM+JS interpreter SPI.

**Interpreter design (kernel-free multiplatform SPI; decided 2026-06-24 — JVM AND JS/TS, prefer modern APIs):** the core defines a **multiplatform pure SPI** in `commonMain` mirroring the **`IData`-based** `ICustomCondition` contract (`CustomCondition.check` over pure `IData`/`IIdentifier`-shaped interfaces: `getValue(path,indices)`, the relevant + formally-incorrect sets, the error entity), so it compiles to JVM **and** JS and existing **Java core** AND **TS** impls plug in (their shapes already match). A project's JVM **md `DocumentV2`** impl is adapted via a thin JVM-only `DocumentV2`→`IData`-view adapter if needed. **"Prefer modern APIs"** applies to the **oracle/runtime path** (`validateFull(DocumentV2, DocumentProcessingConfig.customConditionFactory(...))`, not the deprecated `Locale`/static-service path), NOT to the SPI *contract* (which must be the cross-platform `IData`-based one). `CustomCondition X` parses to `Cond.Custom(name)`; unregistered name → reported unsupported (fail-fast). Implementation: INTERPRETER-ARCHITECTURE.md §7 (custom conditions).

## Custom-extensibility surface — the kernel registers exactly TWO custom things (conditions + field types); there is NO custom computation/function (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: folded → §14.

The umbrella over the two SPI findings above (§8 predefined-type validity, and CustomCondition): the kernel's **project-facing extension API** — `kernel-md-facade/.../DocumentRtCustomExtensionService` — exposes exactly **two** custom-registration hooks, `registerCustomConditions(ICustomConditionFactory)` and `registerCustomFieldTypes(ICustomFieldTypeFactory)` (each with a `…V2`). **There is no `registerCustomComputations`/`registerCustomFunction`/`registerCalculation` — a custom *computation* is not a kernel concept.** Corroborated four independent ways:

- **German-source sweep** (`.java` + `.g4`): the only `Applikations*` construct is `ApplikationsBedingung` (= CustomCondition). No `ApplikationsBerechnung`/`-Funktion`.
- **SPI interfaces:** the only `I(Custom|Application)*` types are condition / field-type / field-validator / **migration** (`ICustomMigrationCode` — a *third* custom hook, but it is data-migration between model versions, not validation or computation). None for functions/computations.
- **Computation vocabulary is closed:** `CalcOp` is an enum `{ADD, MULTIPLY, SUBTRACT, DIVIDE, POWER}`; a computation operation is built solely from it + the standard value-expression catalog (rounding, aggregates, date math, …). `ICalculationService`/`IBaseCalculationService` are the *internal* evaluator (`checkCalculationRule`, no register/custom methods), not a project SPI.
- **The one custom hook that could reach a computation is barred:** `CustomCondition` is **forbidden inside computation rules** (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §14).

So the kernel's custom escape hatches are for **conditions** and **field types** only. (Challenged + re-verified 2026-06-26 across kernel source, BA/semantics docs, the grammar, and this registration API.) rulekit/CLI treatment: a computation's operation is the closed `Calculation` sealed set; the interpreter's treatment (an unknown function fails loud) is INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF22.

## §8 supplement — a CUSTOM-typed field is validated by FULL DELEGATION to a registered validator; an unregistered one THROWS (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: folded → the field-types table.

How the kernel validates a field declared with a **`CustomFieldType{name, minLength?, maxLength?}`** at `validateFull` (the sibling of the §8 `Valid`/`Invalid` *operator* — this is the *declared-field* form). Source: `FormatDefinitionVordefiniert.validiereFormat` (+ its TS twin `FormatDefinitionVordefiniert.ts`, byte-identical):

- **Full delegation, NOT generic enforcement.** Unlike a plain `StringType` (whose `FormatDefinitionString` checks `minLength`/`maxLength` itself → `stringZuKurz`/`stringZuLang`), a `CustomFieldType` field delegates the **entire** check — length included — to the registered `ICustomFieldValidator.validate(value, param, …)`. The kernel performs **no** length comparison of its own.
- **The declared bounds are only HINTS.** The kernel passes `minLength`/`maxLength` to the validator via `ICustomFieldTypeValidationParam` (`-1` = `UNSPECIFIED_LENGTH` → `Optional.empty()`), and the interface javadoc tells the validator it generally should *not* rely on them — they are informational, the validator owns the decision.
- **No registered validator → it THROWS.** `getCustomFieldValidator` resolves the validator from the factory / global `CustomFieldTypeService`; missing → `ValidatorException("Custom field validator '<name>' missing.")`, which `validateFull` surfaces as `FaultException("Unable to invoke validation")`. So a *filled* custom-typed field on a bare model fails the **whole** validation (an empty/not-relevant cell short-circuits before reaching it). There is no "fire a formal error" and no "skip".
- **The error code is the validator's OWN key**, `ICustomFieldTypeCheckError.getErrorKey()` (project-specific, e.g. `"invalidCreditCardNumber"`) — **not** a fixed kernel code like the `StringType` length codes.
- **The bounds DO reach the model + runtime** (`md.model.api.fieldtypes.ICustomFieldType.getMinLength/getMaxLength`; codegen carries them into the validation param) — they are real model properties, just never kernel-enforced.

This is the kernel truth behind the interpreter's treatment (auto-validate each filled custom cell against the consumer-registered predefined type; unregistered → report unsupported + degrade leniently, since the native runtime cannot host the validator) — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF26. JS/TS identical, so a Kotlin/JS consumer mirrors it.

## §12 supplement — Partial validation (`validatePart`) — rule-gating + 3VL relevance (source + probe-confirmed 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §12.

`validatePart(DocumentV2, Set<DocumentMultiPointer> relevantEntities, DocumentProcessingConfig)` validates only a *part* of the document. The contract (kernel javadoc on `IDocumentRtService.validatePart`, probe-confirmed) is two mechanisms:

- **Rule-gating by the error field.** A validation rule is **executed only if its error-field instance is in the relevant set** (this includes the auto-generated formal/mandatory/unique field checks, whose error field is the field itself). So a rule whose error field is not relevant simply does not run — no message.
- **Suppression via 3VL on non-relevant references.** Of the rules that *do* run, "validation errors will be suppressed if the rule references any repetition of a field/group that is not marked as relevant — **except for some cases where the kernel is sure that no possible value of that field/group repetition could have prevented the error**." That exception is exactly **Kleene three-valued logic**: a non-relevant reference is **UNKNOWN**, and `true Or Unknown = true` (still fires — no value could prevent it) while `true And Unknown = Unknown` (suppressed). Probe over a two-field rule confirmed: `And` with only the error field relevant → suppressed; the same as `Or` → fires; only the *other* field relevant (error field not) → not run.

Relevant pointers: `DocumentMultiPointer.of(fullName, repetitions)` with **`ALL_SEMANTIC` (index `0`)** as the per-level wildcard ("all repetitions"); a relevant **group** makes all its **descendants** relevant. The kernel **encourages** wildcarding every path level — a *subset* of repetitions has subtle suppression around wildcards/aggregates (`/RG*/f1` references not-filled reps too) and `NumberOfFilled…`/semantic-index/parallel-iteration.

**GLOBAL fields are auto-added to the relevant set — but by the MD-LAYER, not the core validator (source-verified 2026-06-26).** A field flagged **`global`** (DM-JSON `"global": true` in the Field body; `IField.isGlobal`) is **always relevant in partial validation**, regardless of the caller's set. The mechanism is precise: the **core** validator (`ValidationCommand`) does *not* touch global fields; the **md-runtime-service** layer — exactly the `IDocumentRtService.validatePart(DocumentV2, …, config)` boundary the oracle calls — injects them first (`LowKernelApiUtils.validatePartlyWith3ValueLogic` → `addGlobalFieldsToRelevantEntities`, which adds `metaModel.getValue("GlobalFields")` = each global field's pointer **wildcarded at all reps**, `MetaModelImpl` building them with all-zero `ALL_SEMANTIC` indices). `IField.isGlobal`'s own javadoc states the contract: *"Global fields should always be passed to a partial validation in addition to the usual relevant entities. The caller of the validation is responsible for passing the global fields."* So at the kernel's *public* runtime boundary they ARE auto-added; a rule whose error field is global therefore RUNS even when the caller's relevant set omits it. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.PartialValidationGlobalFieldDiffTest` — global vs. non-global control vs. the kernel; the loader mapping by `cli.interp.InterpreterRealModelDiffTest.loaderReadsTheGlobalFieldFlagIntoModelDef`.)*

**Interpreter treatment:** this maps directly onto the existing 3VL engine — `DmInterpreter.validatePart` gates each emitted message by its error field's relevance and resolves non-relevant referenced instances to `Val.Unknown` (the same `invalid`-operand mechanism formal errors already use), so Kleene logic yields the kernel's exact suppression with no special-casing; whole-document-relevant equals `validateFull`. **Starred-aggregate relevance is PER-OPERATOR (probe-confirmed 2026-06-26).** Under a subset relevant set, an aggregate over a starred reference `G*/f` is relevant per its operator:
- **all-rows aggregates** (`Sum`, `MaxValue`, `MinValue`, `NumberOfDifferentValues`, `SumOfProducts`) are evaluated **only when the relevant set WILDCARDS the starred level** — `ALL_SEMANTIC` at the repeatable group, or a relevant ANCESTOR group (descent makes all rows relevant). Enumerating concrete rows — **even ALL of them** — leaves the aggregate UNKNOWN (no firing): the `*` reference's extent is "fully known" only via a wildcard (the BA's "encourages wildcarding every path level"). Controlled probe (2 rows, no fillable tail): `Sum(Lines* / Price) > 1000` fires under a `Lines` wildcard but NOT under `{Lines[1], Lines[2]}` listed concretely.
- **`FirstFilled`** is **order-aware**: it returns the first FILLED cell's value and is UNKNOWN only when a non-relevant cell precedes that first filled cell. So it fires when the first filled row is relevant (wildcard, both-rows, or that-row-only) and is UNKNOWN when that row is non-relevant (probe: row-1-filled fires under row-1-only, suppresses under row-2-only).

This is exactly the runtime `isWertAngegeben` 3VL (a non-relevant cell read is UNKNOWN; an all-rows fold with any UNKNOWN → UNKNOWN), with the wildcard-vs-concrete distinction governing whether the star's cells are check-relevant. *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.starRelevantOf` + the per-operator `evalFunc` gate, IF42; locked `adapter.laws.PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest` + the randomized `perf.PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest`.)*

The oracle path is the modern `validatePart(DocumentV2, …, config)` (`RuntimeLaws.firedCodesPart`/`messagesPart`). Global fields are auto-relevant (IF37, above); a `CustomCondition` receives the relevant set (null for full, the subset for partial — IF38, source `MainValidatorController` → `getAllPruefungsrelevanteEntitiesExtern`; the set is the caller's *external* pointers **as passed** — wildcards preserved, groups NOT expanded — plus the auto-added globals); and uniqueness needs the duplicate **partner** relevant too (IF39 — the kernel's duplicate caches are built from the relevant fields only, `befuelleCacheMitRelevantenFeldern`). *(Locked: `adapter/…/laws/PartialValidationDiffTest` + `interpreter/commonTest/PartialValidationTest`; subset-repetition aggregates by `PartialValidationSubsetRepDiffTest` (IF35, non-directional); global fields by `PartialValidationGlobalFieldDiffTest` (IF37); custom-condition relevant-set propagation by `PartialValidationCustomConditionDiffTest` (IF38); subset uniqueness by `PartialValidationUniqueIndexDiffTest` + `PartialValidationRepetitionNotUniqueDiffTest` (IF39); per-operator starred-aggregate relevance by `PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest` (IF42, above); and a randomized `PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest` sweeps (document × relevant-subset) combinations across the WHOLE partial surface. The interpreter matches the kernel across partial validation.)*

## §9 supplement — `validateFull` also emits STRUCTURAL over-repetition errors — `zuGrosseZeile` / `zuGrosseKontextnummer` (model-fuzz finding, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §9.

A document instance with **more rows than the group's declared `repeatability`** (or a repetition index beyond it) is flagged by the kernel's `validateFull` with **`zuGrosseZeile`** ("row too large" — the repeatable group) and **`zuGrosseKontextnummer`** ("context number too large" — each over-limit field instance), as VALUE errors; these also **suppress** the ordinary checks (uniqueIndex/mandatory/rules) on the over-limit rows. This is a **structural/consistency** check on the document's *shape*, not a rule-evaluation result — the kernel runtime emits it alongside rule firings. The model-fuzz harness (E) surfaced it by generating documents whose row counts exceeded a low declared repeatability.

The check is per **entity instance** (`FormalChecker.checkIndicesLessOrEqualThanMax`): walk the instance's repetition indices against the per-level declared maxima; the first level over its max emits — the instance's **own** (innermost) over-limit level → `zuGrosseZeile`, an **intermediate** (ancestor) over-limit level → `zuGrosseKontextnummer` — then stops. So a repeatable **group** row whose own index is over → `zuGrosseZeile` (the group entity, its own trailing index omitted in the pointer); a **field** inside an over-limit ancestor → `zuGrosseKontextnummer` (full field pointer). (A third sibling, `FEHLER_LAUFENDE_NR_ZU_GROSS`, rides the USB/multi-form form counter — out of a single-document model's scope.) Interpreter treatment: now **modeled** (emission + suppression), INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF27.

## `mandatoryGroup` (`FEHLER_PFLICHTKONTEXT`) is a DEAD code in 30.8.1 — defined but never emitted; a group cannot be declared mandatory (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: not-foldable — a dead-code inventory fact (defined, never emitted) with its re-open trigger; nothing to fold.

The runtime formal-error code **`mandatoryGroup`** (`IFormaleFehlerConstants.FEHLER_PFLICHTKONTEXT`, "auto generated rule: mandatory group") is **referenced nowhere** in the kernel `30.8.1` source except its own constant definition (Java + the TS twin) — **no code, codegen template, or generated validator emits it**. And **no DM-JSON property declares a *group* mandatory**: the md-serializer and the `a12-kernel-mm` metamodels carry mandatory/`requirednessConfig` only on **fields** (→ the `mandatoryField` rules `AutogeneratedRulesService` generates, KERNEL-FINDINGS §4), not on groups. So `mandatoryGroup` **cannot fire on any loadable DM model** — an unemitted code over an undeclarable property (a probe accordingly returned empty). The interpreter correctly never emits it; this is **parity by construction**, not a gap. *Re-open trigger:* a kernel upgrade that wires `FEHLER_PFLICHTKONTEXT` to an emission site **and** a group-mandatory declaration (re-check on the next [`KERNEL-UPGRADE`](KERNEL-UPGRADE.md)).

## `multiSelect` value uniqueness (`FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT` = `uniqueValue`) is DEAD code in 30.8.1 — generated by NEITHER strategy (source-verified 2026-06-27)

> Fold: not-foldable — a dead-code inventory fact (generated by neither strategy) with its re-open trigger; nothing to fold.

A `usageType == "multi-select"` group (KERNEL-FINDINGS "group templates", the `MultiSelectGroupRule` consistency shape) is described as carrying an auto value-uniqueness check — "no value selected twice". In kernel `30.8.1` **that check is generated by no evaluation path**, so it never fires on any loadable model:

- **md-RUNTIME** (`AutogeneratedRulesService`, the `IDocumentRtService` autogen provider): generates only `MANDATORY_FIELD` / `UNIQUE_FIELD` (index) / `SOLITARY_FIELD` rules — there is **no multi-select method** at all. (Confirmed by differential: a 3-row multi-select group with duplicate values fires `mandatoryField` on an empty row but **nothing** for the duplicate — `adapter.laws.MultiSelectUniquenessDiffTest`.)
- **Groovy CODEGEN** (`CodeGenRuleDefinition`): has a `case UNIQUE_VALUE → "FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT"`, but that case is **provably unreachable**. `RuleInfo.getAutogenType()` returns `((IAutogeneratedRule) rule).getGenerationType()`, and the **only** `IAutogeneratedRule` implementations (`MandatoryFieldRuleImpl` / `UniqueFieldRuleImpl` / `SolitaryFieldRuleImpl`) return `MANDATORY_FIELD` / `UNIQUE_FIELD` / `SOLITARY_FIELD` — **none returns `UNIQUE_VALUE`**, and no code constructs such a rule. So the `case UNIQUE_VALUE` switch arm is dead.

**The COMPLETE `usageType: "multi-select"` handling map (full read/write trace, 2026-06-27).** Tracing every site that writes or reads the flag across the whole kernel shows it is a **model + editor + consistency marker ONLY** — no evaluator touches it:

| layer | site | what it does with `multi-select` |
|---|---|---|
| write | `GroupSerializer` | serializes `usageType` to DM-JSON |
| write | editor / `DocumentModelExpand`+`CollapseService` | sets / propagates / unsets across include expansion |
| read (consistency) | `MultiSelectGroupRule` | **the only active handler** — validates the group SHAPE at `checkConsistency` (repeatable · one enum/string field · required · not-index) → `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_*` problems |
| read (model→runtime) | `ValidationModelConverter:194-209` | stores `usageType` in the validation-model `Group`, but only the `A12_ATTACHMENT` branch *acts* on it — multi-select is stored then **never consumed** |
| read (attachment-only) | `DecorationField/GroupModelJoiner`, `ExpandService:689` | all gated on `A12_ATTACHMENT`/`cockpit_attachment` — never multi-select |
| runtime eval (`kernel-rt`) | — | reads `usageType` **nowhere** (only the dead `FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT` constant) |
| codegen (`kernel-tool`) | — | reads `usageType` **nowhere** (only the dead `UNIQUE_VALUE_ERROR_ID`); **no `.stg` template emits it** |
| generated code | — | no `.stg`/`.groovy` references `multi-select`/`uniqueValue` |

So `usageType: "multi-select"` drives only the UI checkbox-list widget + the `checkConsistency` shape check; it reaches the validation (runtime) model via `ValidationModelConverter` but `kernel-rt` never reads it back (dead storage). For ANY document-validation evaluator (runtime or codegen-equivalent) it is a no-op beyond the shape check.

`uniqueValue` is therefore **vestigial** — the runtime-and-codegen sibling of the dead `mandatoryGroup` above. Both kernel strategies produce nothing, so the interpreter (matching BOTH per the CLAUDE.md #1 rule) correctly does **not** fire it — **parity by construction**, NOT a gap, and NOT a runtime-vs-codegen divergence (they agree: dead in both). **Corroborated from the model side (2026-06-27 sweep):** all **6** real multi-select groups in the 2025.06-ext5 workspaces are the minimal shape (`repeatability` + `usageType: "multi-select"` + one field — **no** `indexFieldName` / `unique` / extra property that could trigger a uniqueness rule), and the **`a12-kernel-mm` MM_Group metamodel** defines `multi-select` as merely a `usageType` **enum value** (beside `attachment`/`complexType`) with **no uniqueness constraint attached** (zero `uniqueValue`/`FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT` anywhere in the metamodels). Source + runtime + codegen + real models + metamodel all agree. The kernel's *consistency/editor* layer still validates the multi-select group **shape** (`MultiSelectGroupRule`) and the UI widget still prevents duplicate selection — but neither produces a `validateFull`/runtime `uniqueValue` message. *Re-open trigger:* a kernel upgrade that adds a multi-select method to `AutogeneratedRulesService` **or** an `IAutogeneratedRule` returning `UNIQUE_VALUE` (re-check on the next [`KERNEL-UPGRADE`](KERNEL-UPGRADE.md)). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.MultiSelectUniquenessDiffTest`.)*

## `IRule.getCustomRefErrorEntitiesIfFilled/IfNotFilled` (modeler-defined error-entity attribution) is UNWIRED in 30.8.1 (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: not-foldable — an unwired kernel API slot (feature not supported in 30.8.1) with its re-open trigger; nothing to fold.

A rule can, *in the metamodel*, carry **custom-ref error entities** (`MM_Common_v2` defines a `CustomRefErrorEntities` slot with `CustomRefErrorEntityIfFilled`/`IfNotFilled`) — modeler-defined relative paths that refine **which field(s) a fired rule blames**, chosen by the referenced field's filled-state (the "nichtangabe-fehlerfelder"). But the feature is **not supported in kernel 30.8.1**: the md-model rule mapping (`kernel-md-model/.../mapping/Rule.java`) returns `List.of()` for both accessors, commented *"update implementation when customRefErrorEntities get supported in A12"* (A12K-2281 / A12K-2361); the `IRule` default and every autogenerated rule impl (Mandatory / UniqueField / SolitaryField) also return empty; and `RuleParser` short-circuits the candidate-relpath expansion whenever both are empty — i.e. **always**. So a loaded rule **never** carries custom-ref error entities, and the kernel **always** uses its default automatic error-entity attribution. The interpreter attributing a fired rule to its declared error entity is therefore **parity by construction** (the default path the kernel always takes); the modeler-defined refinement is moot until the kernel wires it. *Re-open trigger:* a kernel upgrade that lands A12K-2281/2361 (re-check on the next [`KERNEL-UPGRADE`](KERNEL-UPGRADE.md)).

## §8 supplement — the ==/!= string-enum compatibility matrix (display-texts, probe-confirmed 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §8.

For `==`/`!=` between string/enum fields (BA L1712-1742): an enumeration whose values carry **display texts** is incomparable with a plain **string** and with a **texts-less** enum — both draw **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_ENUM_TO_STRING`** ("not allowed to compare enumerations with display values to strings or to enumerations without display values"). A **string ↔ texts-less enum** comparison IS allowed, and **two texts-bearing enums** compare when their value→text maps agree on the overlap (BA: a shared value with mismatched texts — or a shared text with mismatched values — makes them incomparable). So the *presence of display texts*, not just the stored value, governs comparability. (Source `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl.checkEnumVsStringType` + `checkEnumValuesWithoutCategories`, both gated on `FieldUtils.isEnumWithDisplayValues`; a category comparison is exempt.) *(Locked: `validate.laws.EnumComparabilityLawsTest`.)*

## §8 supplement — three enum-ish kinds: "extensible enum" is an IStringType quality, NOT a field type (source-verified 2026-06-17)

> Fold: not-foldable — a type-system/model-structure classification (what is a field type vs a client-SPI quality), outside the evaluation scope.

Three distinct concepts, easy to conflate:

- **Closed `EnumerationType`** — a fixed value set; a normal DM-JSON field type (the md `EnumerationType` DTO: values/categories/errorMessage/alphabeticalSorting/sValue — *no* extensible flag).
- **Extensible (non-closed) enumeration** — **not a field type at all**: an **`IStringType` with `isExtensibleEnum() == true`** (parser `FieldUtils.isExtensibleEnum` = `type instanceof IStringType && isExtensibleEnum()`; `isLikeString` = string ∪ enum ∪ ext-enum). `isExtensibleEnum()` is a **client-SPI/runtime quality** — `IStringType` is "intended to be implemented by clients", the kernel's md `StringType` **defaults it `false`**, and there is **no DM-JSON property** for it (its open/dynamic values are served at runtime, e.g. via `DynamicEnumerationService`). So a DM-JSON field is **just a `StringType` — fully buildable and modelkit-authorable**; the "extensible" quality itself is unauthorable via DM-JSON (always `false` in a plain model) but doesn't need to be: `ValidType`/`InvalidType` accept the field **because it is-a string** (`isLikeString`), which the N7 `Valid(CustomerName, "EMail")` validExample already exercises. **NOT a modelkit gap.**
- **Dynamic / table-backed enumeration** — the genuinely **un-buildable** one (the `ValueNotConsistent` finding below): values from an external table, not DM-JSON-serializable.

(The model/editor metamodel `MM_Common` carries an "ExtensibleEnumerationType" config — maxLength/displayValues — and form-model/UI notions like `fieldConfigurationEntry`/`externalEnumeration` exist, but those are **tooling/FM layers outside rulekit's DM-JSON scope**, not runtime DM field types.) *(Corrects an earlier draft that wrongly called extensible enums "un-buildable" and cited `IEnumerationType.isExtensible` — it conflated the extensible `IStringType` quality with the dynamic/table-backed enum.)*

## §8 supplement — table-backed (dynamic) enums are NOT DM-JSON-serializable ⇒ `ValueNotConsistent` is un-buildable (2026-06-08)

> Fold: not-foldable — a serialization/infrastructure limit (what a self-contained DM-JSON can express), outside the evaluation scope.

`ValueNotConsistent(enumField <toOp> col1, col2, …)` (DE `WertNichtKonsistent`) checks a row's dependent columns against a **table-backed** enumeration. The checker (`CheckFieldAndValueListConditionImpl`) requires its first operand to be an enum field **with a table reference** (`IEnumerationType.getTableName()`/`getDependentColumns()` present), and verifies the dependent-param arity against the column count (value checks are skipped when the runtime data table is absent).

**But that field type cannot be expressed in a document model's DM-JSON.** The public a12internal `EnumerationType` DTO declares exactly **6 properties** — `values`, `annotations`, `categories`, `sValue`, `errorMessage`, `alphabeticalSorting` — and the deserializer is **strict**: injecting `tableName`/`columnName`/`dependentColumns` raises `UnrecognizedPropertyException` (*"not marked as ignorable"*, probed 2026-06-08). The table backing is materialized at **runtime** from a separate enumeration/table catalog (`IEnumerationType` returns empty Optionals when loaded from a plain document model), not from the field's type JSON.

**Consequence:** no self-contained document model can mark a field table-backed, so the checker's precondition can never be met, so rulekit's "author DM-JSON → `checkConsistency`" oracle **cannot author or verify** a `ValueNotConsistent` rule. It is **un-buildable within single-document-model scope** — same root cause as multi-form/Vordruck (needs infrastructure outside the document model), and it makes the would-be "dynamic-enum modelkit authoring" moot (there is nothing in DM-JSON to emit). Stays a tolerant `ReadResult.Opaque` if ever encountered. *(Demonstrated by probe; not locked by a test — there is no expressible fixture to lock.)*

## Header `modelVersion` compatibility — same-major and not-newer than the kernel's model-format (probed 2026-06-12)

> Fold: not-foldable — a model-header compatibility policy (structure/versioning), outside the evaluation scope.

The kernel polices a document's `header.modelVersion` during `checkConsistency` (not at parse): a mismatch surfaces as a **`KERNEL` ERROR** — `"Version mismatch: Document model version is <X> and application version is <28.4.0>"` — so the model is reported `valid:false` (it loads enough to validate; this is a consistency diagnostic, not a load crash). The "application version" is the **model-format version the running kernel implements** (`30.8.1` implements `28.4.0`). Probed band (against `order-ruled`, app-format `28.4.0`):

| `header.modelVersion` | verdict | rule |
|---|---|---|
| `28.4.0` | valid | exact |
| `28.0.0` | **valid** | same major, **≤** app-format → backward-compatible |
| `28.4.1`, `28.5.0`, `28.9.0` | invalid | same major but **newer** than app-format |
| `27.0.0`, `29.0.0`, `30.8.1` | invalid | different major |

So the kernel is **tolerant of an older same-major model and fail-fast on a newer/different-major one**, with a clear message — exactly the posture we want, enforced upstream. rulekit only *classifies* this verdict (it does not re-derive the rule): the un-coded kernel ERROR is enriched to **`RK_MODEL_VERSION_INCOMPATIBLE`** (+ a fix), and an accepted-but-off-reference model gets a tolerant INFO **`RK_MODEL_VERSION_SKEW`**. The reference is `core.ModelFormat.VERSION` (`28.4.0`), bumped in lockstep with the kernel pin (KERNEL-UPGRADE). *(Locked: `cli.ModelVersionPolicyTest` — the band's tolerant/exact/fail-fast cases + the message pattern via `validate.ModelVersion.isVersionMismatch`; the no-implicit-bump round-trip is in the same test.)*

## §1/§3 supplement — value-vs-omission typing is DIRECTIONAL and sign-aware (source + runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §12.
