A fired comparison is an OMISSION error (`TRUE_AF`) only when filling an operand **in the breaking direction** could clear it (`BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleiche`): `GT`/`GE` → `left.kannKleinerWerden || right.kannGroesserWerden`; `LT`/`LE` → the mirror; `EQ` → any operand can move; `NE` → directional on which side is larger (`getFehlerFuerNE`). The per-operand fillability of an **empty number** is asymmetric: `kannGroesserWerden = true` (always fillable larger) but **`kannKleinerWerden = hatVorzeichen`** — fillable smaller only if the field is **signed** (`RuntimeController.feldWertAlsZahl`, TS twin `BigDecimalUtils.makeDecimalFromNonEmptyString`).

- **The sign trigger is `NumberType.positivesOnly`, NOT `minValue`** (the trap): a field with `minValue:1` is still *signed* (probed — `[BirthDay] >= 0` empty types OMISSION); only `positivesOnly:true` makes it unsigned (`[StockOnHand:positivesOnly] >= 0` empty types **VALUE**). `minValue` is a range constraint, independent of `hatVorzeichen`.
- Consequence: `[Unsigned] >= 0` (empty) is a **VALUE** error — an unsigned 0 can't shrink, so no fill breaks `>= 0`; the same over a *signed* field is **OMISSION**. Both `<= X` are OMISSION (filling larger breaks them).

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DirectionalPolarityDiffTest` (unsigned `StockOnHand` vs signed `Quantity`); the signed cases also in `adapter.laws.PolarityDiffTest`/`AggPolarityDiffTest`; the **`NE` directional arm** (`getFehlerFuerNE` — previously documented here but never `!=`-directionally DiffTest-exercised) is **corpus-locked** since 2026-07-17 by the portable `empty-polarity` cases (signedness + literal-direction + filled-state pairs, tri-engine replay incl. the JS oracle). The interpreter's directional treatment: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF9.)*

**Function-operand fillability — `CurrentRepetition` and `FirstFilledValue` (probe + full-message fuzz, 2026-06-24).** The directional fillability above extends to value FUNCTIONS, not just fields/aggregates — and the naive "any empty/short star tail ⇒ fillable" over-reports OMISSION for two:

- **`CurrentRepetition(group)`** is the structural **row index**; no field-fill changes it, so a fired comparison against it is **always VALUE** (probed: `CurrentRepetition(Lines) > 2` on row 3 → VALUE). The generic fallback wrongly reads its group argument as an "unfilled field" and types it OMISSION.
- **`FirstFilledValue(g*/f)`** is **OMISSION iff NO row is filled** (the empty selection folds to the kind's fillable not-given value — the 2026-07-08 refinement below) **or an instantiated EMPTY row PRECEDES the first filled one** — filling that (earlier / any) row could become the new first value and clear the error — else **VALUE** (the first value is fixed; *later* empties are irrelevant). Probed: `(empty, 60)` → OMISSION; `(60, empty)` and `(60)` → VALUE.

The naive star-omission notion (`starOmission`: any empty cell / short tail) is too broad for `FirstFilledValue` — only an empty row *before* the first filled matters. Both were mis-typed OMISSION until the **harness-D full-message fuzz** caught them (117/200 seeds — invisible to the codes-only gate). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FullMessageFuzzDiffTest` + `CurrentRepetitionDiffTest`/`FirstFilledValueDiffTest`, now asserting polarity, not just firing; interpreter `EvalContext.firstStarRowEmpty` + `funcFill`.)*

**`FirstFilledValue` over an EMPTY selection returns its operand kind's OWN not-given value — number → the fillable 0, else NOT-GIVEN (source + differential 2026-07-08).** The kernel's `FirstValueCombiner` is a per-kind family — `FirstValueCombinerNumber`/`Date`/`DateRange`/`String` — and on an empty selection (no filled cell) each returns its own `notGivenValue()`: `NICHT_ANGEGEBENE_ZAHL` for NUMBER (a *value*, the fillable `0` — the empty-as-0 its Sum/Min/Max siblings give), but `NOT_GIVEN_DATE`/`NOT_GIVEN_DATE_RANGE`/`NICHT_ANGEGEBENER_STRING` for the others (NO value → not-given). Observable: a DATE/STRING `FirstFilledValue(empty)` in a comparison does **not** fire (`vergleiche…` on a not-given side), and a computation into a DATE/STRING target is **CLEARED**; only a NUMBER empty `FirstFilledValue` yields the fillable 0. So the kind-following FirstFilledValue's empty identity is NOT uniformly a number (the sibling of the §6 min/max `DateCombiner` NULL-date identity — a numeric identity where a date/string is expected is an interpreter-only type mixture no legal kernel input produces). *(Interpreter: `firstFilledValue`'s empty identity is `Val.Num(0)` iff the operand kind is NUMBER, else `Val.Unknown`; before the fix an unconditional `Num(0)` crashed `ValCompare` against the real value. Locked `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueKindDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF83.)*

**Fill propagates through ARITHMETIC — an empty operand keeps the result fillable (source `VkBigDecimal`, differential 2026-07-07).** The directional fill (`kannGroesserWerden`/`kannKleinerWerden`) is a property of the *value*, and every arithmetic operation carries it forward, so `[F] != [A] + [B]` with `B` an empty (fillable) field types the fired comparison **OMISSION** (the RHS can still grow) — not VALUE. Each op propagates differently (`VkBigDecimal.add`/`subtract`/`multiply`/`divide`/`power`): `+` combines both directions; `−` **flips the subtrahend** (`a−b` grows if `a` grows or `b` shrinks); `×`/`÷` are **SIGN-DEPENDENT** (`produktKannGroesser/KleinerWerden` — a factor pushes the product up by growing when the other factor is positive / shrinking when it is negative; `÷` is `a × (1/b)`); `^` follows `powCanChange` (base magnitude vs 0/1/−1 and exponent parity). This rides the SAME runtime `VkBigDecimal` in both strategies (codegen emits calls to it) ⟹ codegen-parity. **The VALUE FUNCTIONS carry the flags too**, each via its own `VkBigDecimal` op: `setScale` (the `Round*` family) **PRESERVES** them exactly; `abs` **TRANSFORMS** them with the value sign (`g'=g∨s`, `s'=(pos∧s)∨(neg∧g)` — `|x|` grows off either direction, shrinks only toward 0); `min`/`max` (operand-list `Min`/`Max`) **COMBINE** directionally (min shrinks via either operand, grows via the smaller; max mirrors); `bereichAlsZahl` (`RangeAsNumber`) is **GROW-ONLY** (`kannKleiner=false`, value ≥ 0). So `RoundAccounting(<fillable arith>, n)` / `AbsValue(…)` / `Min(…)` / `RangeAsNumber(…)` are likewise fillable. **The starred NUMBER aggregates' omitted-tail fill is directional per combiner** (`NumberCombiner`): `Sum` is `(canGrow = true, canShrink = signed)` — the shrink half is `hatVorzeichen`; `MaxValue` is `(true, false)`; `MinValue` is `(false, true)` — so an unsigned `Sum(G*/f) >= 0` fired over empties types **VALUE** while the signed twin types OMISSION. *(Locked `adapter.laws.AggDirectionalPolarityDiffTest`; interpreter `funcFill`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF9.)* *(Interpreter treatment IF69 for arithmetic + IF71 for the value functions: `ArithFill` + `Interpreter.fill(Expr.Arith)`/`funcFill`; differential-locked `adapter.laws.ArithmeticFillPolarityDiffTest`/`FunctionFillPolarityDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.ArithmeticFillTest`/`FunctionFillTest`.)*

## §5 supplement — tolerance boundary + comparison rescaling (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §5.

- **`DiffersWithToleranceRange{N}` fires strictly beyond the tolerance**: satisfied iff `|a − b| > N` — a difference of *exactly* N is still within tolerance and does **not** fire, in either direction. The boundary is the trap a modeller would guess either way. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ToleranceComparisonLawsTest`.)*
- **Source-read precision policy** (`VkBigDecimal`): a NUMBER **comparison** rescales both operands to scale 19 `HALF_UP` before comparing — and this is **`BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleiche` (lines 164-167), not `VkBigDecimal.compareTo`** (which is plain): so the rescale applies to **all** comparison ops `<`/`>`/`<=`/`>=`/`==`/`!=`, but **NOT to `min`/`max` selection**, which the kernel does at full precision via `VkBigDecimal.compareTo` (`SummeCombiner`/`MinWertCombiner` etc.). Separately, **arithmetic `×`/`÷`/`^` rounds to `MathContext(50)` `HALF_UP`** (`INTERNAL_PRECISION`). The kernel team's note was "no legal model surfaces the scale-19 rounding (field scales cap far below 19)" — but **multiplying two high-scale fields exceeds it** (scale-10 × scale-10 → scale-20), so the rounding *is* reachable through an expression, and `÷` is non-terminating without the MathContext at all. **Interpreter status: reproduced in full.** The comparison scale-19 rounding rides `Dec.scale19` (comparison ops only — `min`/`max` stay full-precision), and the `MathContext(50)` arithmetic cap now applies to **all three of `×`/`÷`/`^`** (`Dec.times`/`div`/`pow` on the JVM actual, the differential-exact target; `+`/`-` stay exact, as the kernel does not cap them). `×` was the last uncapped operator — a 26-digit × 26-digit → 52-digit product lost its last 2 digits in the kernel but not the interpreter, until `Dec.times` took the same `INTERNAL` `MathContext`. *(Interpreter-locked: `adapter.laws.PrecisionDiffTest` — the scale-19 comparison boundary; `adapter.laws.ArithmeticDiffTest.multiplicationCapsAtMathContext50LikeTheKernel` — the `×` cap; `adapter.laws.DivPowDiffTest` — `÷`/`^`. Kernel side not separately law-locked — no rulekit consumer.)*
- **The rounding operators are directional, not truncation** (`BigDecimalUtils`, second drain): `RoundDown` is **FLOOR** (toward −∞: `RoundDown(−1.4) = −2`), `RoundUp` is **CEILING** (`RoundUp(−1.4) = −1`), `RoundAccounting` is **HALF_UP** (away from zero on the .5 boundary, both signs). The negative-value behavior is the modeller trap — "down" does not mean "toward zero".
- **A scale-19 `HALF_UP` pre-rounding precedes every target-scale rounding** — and here it *is* observable: division artifacts can't leak through a rounding, `RoundDown([q]/3*3, 0) == [q]` fires for `q=1` (without the pre-round, the internal `0.99…9` would floor to 0). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.RoundingLawsTest`.)*
- **Interpreter status: the full `Round*` family is reproduced** — the operand-list `RoundDown`/`RoundUp`/`RoundAccounting([expr][, n])` and their bare-field `*Value(field[, n])` siblings. **The decimal-places argument is OPTIONAL on BOTH forms** (grammar `rundenOperOp`/`rundenOp` — `(',' ZiffernFolge)?`; an absent `n` is the integer round, places 0; kernel-pinned 2026-07-10, IF102 — a real workspace model carries the 1-arg `RoundDown([X])`). `Dec.roundTo(places, mode)` replays `BigDecimalUtils.runden`'s two-step exactly (pre-round to scale 19 HALF_UP, then `setScale(places, FLOOR/CEILING/HALF_UP)`); on JS, big.js has no native FLOOR/CEILING, so they are emulated by sign (away-from-zero for negatives under FLOOR, the mirror for CEILING) — `toward −∞ / +∞`. *(Interpreter-locked: `adapter.laws.RoundDiffTest` — the negative-boundary FLOOR/CEILING + the `.5` HALF_UP catchers, against the live kernel; the cross-platform `interpreter.RoundToTest` re-asserts the values on JS; the fuzz net adds three round rules — `PerfCatalog.fuzz()`.)*

## §2 supplement — patterns skip empties; operand-list `Min`/`Max` do not (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §2.

Two more edges of the §2 empty-value table (all three now canonical in §2's override table), drained from `RuntimeController` and the `Combiner` contract's `testValidity` flag:

- **An empty string does not violate a pattern** — `PatternViolated` rides the string column (not evaluated on empty; `vergleichePatternSTRING` returns unknown), so it does **not** fire on an absent value. "Empty surely violates my format" is the trap — absence needs `FieldNotFilled`, not a pattern. (A fired pattern comparison is always a *value* error — there is no fillable branch.)
- **The operand-list `Min`/`Max` substitute an empty number as 0; the starred aggregates skip it.** `Min([A], [B])` with `A` empty evaluates `Min(0, B)` — the 0 wins — while `MinValue(G*/F)` ignores unspecified operands (canonical §2). The two list-op families differ exactly on empty handling (the kernel's `EntityListOperations` vs `ListOperOps` split).
- **Value lists and empties, both directions** (third drain): an empty *subject* field is never in any value list (not evaluated, no fire); and an empty *field entry* of a numeric value list can **never be matched** — the empty-as-0 substitution does *not* apply to list entries (`feldWertInWerteListe`: an entry that could still change never equals), so `FieldValueIncludedInValueList(Q, [Allowed])` with `Q = 0` and `Allowed` empty does **not** fire.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.EmptyValueLawsTest` — `emptyStringDoesNotViolateAPattern` / `operandListMinSeesAnEmptyNumberAsZero` / `anEmptyFieldIsNeverInAValueList` / `anEmptyListEntryFieldNeverMatches_noZeroSubstitution`.)*

## §2 supplement — `Length("")` is 0, evaluated (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §2.

`Length` coerces to NUMBER and reads an empty string as `0` rather than the unknown state (`BigDecimalUtils.laenge`), so `Length(F) < 5` fires on an empty `F` in a filled row — the string family's own empty-as-0 backdoor, canonical in §2's override table. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EmptyValueLawsTest.lengthOfAnEmptyStringIsZero_evaluatedUnlikeAComparison`.)*

## Condition directives — `@SuppressWarning` + `RuleGroup` (2026-06-10, grammar-ledger drain)

> Fold: folded → §5 + §10.

Two raw-DSL forms the grammar carries outside the operator surface (productions `preamble` / `regelKontextEntity`), both probe-verified; rulekit authors neither but the read path may meet them:

- **`@SuppressWarning(NAME)`** (DE `@UnterdrueckeWarnung`) prefixes a condition and suppresses exactly **one** supported warning: `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` — i.e. it is the kernel's own escape hatch for the field-vs-field **equality scale gate** (§5 finding 18). `@SuppressWarning(MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES) [Quantity] == [UnitPrice]` validates across scales 0 vs 2. Any other name is rejected — with the quirk that the diagnostic's *code* is the attempted name itself (`MVK_INVALID_SUPPRESSED_WARNING` supplies only the message text). Treatment note: rulekit's `NumberRef.eq/ne` *pre-check* equal scale — a raw-DSL rule using the directive is therefore kernel-legal but not typed-builder-expressible; the directive matters for read tolerance, not authoring.
- **`RuleGroup`** (DE `RegelKontext`) references the rule's **containing group** as an entity — `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)` validates, and the enclosing group counts as referencing the error field. A star on it draws `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD_FOR_RULEGROUP` (the grammar admits the token solely for the nicer error). rulekit renders qualified group paths instead.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.DirectiveLawsTest`.)*

## Condition lexing — keyword case-variant families; whitespace rides a hidden channel; a bare name is never an arithmetic operand (source `LexerTerminals_en` + grammar, differential-locked 2026-07-06/09)

> Fold: not-foldable — concrete-syntax/lexer tier; the canonical doc defers grammar shape to the origin project's grammar doc.

Three lexer/grammar-tier facts about the condition DSL's concrete syntax (they have no KERNEL-SEMANTICS home — the canonical doc defers grammar shape; clean-room from the lexer terminals + the ANTLR grammar, behavior-locked):

- **Keyword case variants are FIXED per-keyword token families, not case-folding.** `LexerTerminals_en` enumerates each accepted spelling: `And`/`and`/`AND` (UND), `Or`/`or`/`OR` (ODER), `In`/`in`/`IN`, `to`/`To`/`TO` (ZU, the `ValueNotConsistent` range form). Arbitrary mixed case (`aNd`, `oR`) stays **rejected** — a parser that case-folds would over-accept. *(Interpreter: a `KEYWORD_VARIANTS` table, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF55; locked `interpreter … KeywordCaseVariantParseTest` (JVM+JS) + the 100%-parse `adapter.laws.ParserCorpusSweepTest`.)*
- **Whitespace (and `;;` comments, below) ride the lexer's HIDDEN channel** — insignificant between ANY two tokens. Observable in path syntax, where a naive parser is tempted to read spacing as structure: `Group* / Field` ≡ `Group*/Field`, `.. / seg` ≡ `../seg`, and whitespace **within a `..` up-run** (`.. ..` ≡ `....`) is insignificant too. *(Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF75/IF91; locked `interpreter … PathWhitespaceParseTest` + `ParentUpWhitespaceParseTest` (JVM+JS).)*
- **A bare identifier is never an arithmetic operand**, so `/` and `*` after a bare path segment are always PATH syntax (separator / wildcard), never division/multiplication: the grammar's arithmetic atoms (`einfacheOperation`) are **bracketed** `[entity]` references, constants, or named functions — a bare name cannot start one. This is what makes the whitespace insensitivity above unambiguous.

## Condition comments — `;;` line comments are concrete syntax: tolerated; rulekit authors them and preserves the leading one (grammar + 2026-06-12)

> Fold: not-foldable — concrete-syntax tier + rulekit treatment record; comments are not carried in the parsed AST, so there is no evaluation claim.

The condition lexer defines comments — `Base_ConditionLexer.Comment: ';;' ~('\r'|'\n')* …` — so `;;` begins a **line comment** (continued across lines that each restart with `;;`), parsed *around* a `teilBedingung` (GRAMMAR-LEDGER). So the kernel **validates a commented condition** — comments are read-tolerated. But a comment is **concrete syntax not carried in the parsed AST**: the kernel ships `ConditionFormatter.stripComments` to remove it, and rulekit's typed AST stores none.

rulekit's treatment of this kernel fact (detail: KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES "Condition comments"): comments live in a **side-channel** beside the AST (`rulekit.dsl.Comments`, keyed by node identity, covering both a `Condition` branch and a `Calculation` operation), so they can be **authored** — on a rule (`Rule.Builder.comment/commentAfter`) and on a **computation**, its operations *and* its preconditions (`Computation.Builder.comment(...)`) — rendered as `;;` lines the kernel accepts. A **rule's leading comment is preserved by default** through read→modify→write-back (`Comments.preserveLeading`, applied in `ModelWriter.replaceCondition`); a **computation's** leading comments (its common precondition + first operation) are likewise preserved on modify (`ModelWriter.replaceComputation` rebuilds the element, so the old slot comments are carried — per-alternative matching across a re-expression stays positional/out of scope). The residual **#3 lossiness** is now only *inline/trailing* comments and non-canonical formatting: an AST-based re-render still drops those (the AST has no node to anchor a mid-expression comment to across a re-expression). `KernelAdapter.diff` discloses exactly that — it carries the leading comment on both sides and flags *"inline/trailing comments are not preserved by re-rendering (the leading comment is carried over)"* only when such a comment exists. A rule you don't modify keeps its text verbatim (edits are surgical on the DM-JSON). The concrete-syntax-preserving (minimal-edit) re-render that *would* keep every comment in place is the parked Phase-5 residual (PROJECT-DESIGN §16); the side-channel is the cheaper, chosen alternative for the common (leading-comment) case.

*(Locked: `adapter.ConditionCommentLossinessTest` — a leading comment is preserved (carried in `diff.after`, not flagged lossy) and an inline/trailing one is dropped with the loss disclosed; `adapter.CommentRoundTripTest` — read→modify→write-back keeps the leading comment with no manual sourcing; `rulekit.dsl.CommentRenderTest`/`RuleCommentTest` — the side-channel render + typed authoring; `rulekit.dsl.ComputationAuthoringTest` — comments on a computation's operations + preconditions, kernel-accepted. rulekit treatment: KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES "Condition comments"; kernel API: KERNEL-RULE-API-SURVEY `stripComments`.)*

## §6 supplement — the date family (corpus item 9 slice 9a, 2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: folded → §2 + §6.

(The empty-operand coercion row and the literal/arithmetic behaviors are canonical in §2's override table and §6; the bare-vs-bracketed authoring rule, the rejection codes, and the implementation/treatment log stay here.) `Today` (DE `Heute`), `DifferenceInDays|Months|Years` (DE `DifferenzIn{Tagen,Monaten,Jahren}`), `AddDays|Months|Years` (DE `Addiere{Tage,Monate,Jahre}`):

- **Date ordering needs no scale machinery** — dates have no scale (unlike the scale-gated numeric field-vs-field, §finding 18), so `[A] < [B]` / `[A] == [B]` between two date fields validates directly. `[Date] >= Today` and date-vs-date `==`/ordering all work.
- **`Today` composes everywhere** — as a comparison operand (`[OrderDate] >= Today`) and as a function operand (`DifferenceInDays(OrderDate, Today)`).
- **A date compared to a non-date is rejected** — `[Quantity] >= Today` → **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`** ("only allowed to compare dates with other fitting dates").
- **Bare vs bracketed by position** — a date field is bracketed `[OrderDate]` as a *value* (in comparisons) but **bare** `OrderDate` as an *entity argument* inside *any* function/predicate/aggregate's parentheses — `DifferenceInDays(…)`/`AddMonths(…)`, `DateRange(OrderDate, DeliveryDate)`, `StartOfDateRange(CoverageWindow)`, `Sum(Items*/Amount)`. Bracketing a call's argument is a parse error (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`), not extra safety. (rulekit's renderer has a `renderArg` for argument position.) **A cold-agent trap** — the N6 baseline showed agents carrying the comparison-bracket habit into `DateRange([…], …)`; now taught proactively by the catalog `AUTHORING` gotchas on the date-range functions and by the skill's field-path rule (the bracket rule generalized + the `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` hint corrected to name *too-many* brackets).
- `DifferenceIn*` returns a **number** (comparable/arithmetic); `Add*` returns a **date** (comparable). Semantics caveats (kernel, §6): differences are a *floor* not a calendar count; `AddMonths` preserves the day while `AddYears` maps end-of-February — they disagree across leap years.
- **The value functions coerce an EMPTY operand to a fillable `0` and FIRE** (the string/date families' "empty-as-0 backdoor", the mirror of the number column — verified 2026-07-08). `Length(emptyString)` = 0 (`BigDecimalUtils.laenge`, **grow-only**: `kannKleinerWerden=false`); `YearFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`DayFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` and `HoursFromTime`/`MinutesFromTime`/`SecondsFromTime` over an empty date/time = 0 (`getDatumsTeil` — `DEFAULT_VALUE`, **symmetric**; Quarter special-cases month 0 → 0); `DifferenceInDays/Months/Years/Hours/Minutes/Seconds` = 0 when EITHER operand is empty (`getDatumsDiff`, symmetric). A **MALFORMED** operand (`nichtPruefungsrelevant`) → `NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL` → the comparison is SUPPRESSED (malformed is checked BEFORE empty, so it BEATS empty). Unlike a DIRECT comparison on an empty string/date (which is suppressed), these functions coerce — so `Length(EmptyCode) < 5` FIRES (0 < 5) where `[EmptyCode] == "…"` does not. Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF73; differential-locked by `adapter.laws.EmptyOperandFiringDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.EmptyOperandFiringTest`; `Length`'s empty-as-0 also by the pre-existing `EmptyValueLawsTest`.

**Date + time constant literals are implemented (2026-06-07, family 1) — and the earlier "deferred/rejected" claim was wrong, now corrected.** A **German-format** date literal (`"31.12.2020"`, `dd.MM.yyyy`) compares to a date field **regardless of the field's display format** — `[DispatchDate] > "01.01.2022"` is kernel-valid against *both* a `yyyy-MM-dd` and a `dd.MM.yyyy` field (re-verified 2026-06-07; the previous note claiming `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` against a `yyyy-MM-dd` field was a stale mismeasurement). The kernel stores the constant as an `IConstantOperation` typed **DATE**, German-format, with or without the year (`"13.07."`). A **time** literal (`"17:00:00"`) works the same way (also typed DATE). The literal also rides in argument position — `DifferenceInDays(DispatchDate, "01.01.2022")`. Two genuine limits remain: an **ISO-format literal** `"2022-01-01"` is lexed as a *string*, so ordering draws `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON` (correct in the old note); and a **DATE_TIME field can't compare to a bare string literal** (`[ScheduledAt] == "01.01.2022 12:00:00"` → `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`) — a datetime value must be built with `DateTime(...)` (family 3). Modeled as `DateLiteral`/`TimeLiteral` (Calculation, no operator-id — the quote syntax is intrinsic), wrapped in `DateExpr`/`TimeExpr`; authored via `Dsl.dateValue`/`Dsl.timeValue`. `Now`, `DateRange`/`DateRangesOverlap` were since landed elsewhere. *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsDateAndTimeConstantLiterals` + `dsl.operators.DateConditionTest.rendersDateAndTimeConstantLiterals` + BA-corpus `DateLiteral`/`TimeLiteral`/`DateLiteralDiff` rules.)*

**`Date(d,m,y)` construction + `Valid`/`Invalid` are implemented (2026-06-07) — was deferred, now landed.** `Date(day, month, year[, century, decade])` builds a date from numeric components; the kernel requires **bounded-integer** component fields (a plain scale-0 number draws `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`, so the order fixture's `BirthDay`/`BirthMonth`/`BirthYear` carry `minValue`/`maxValue`). `Valid(Date(…))`/`Invalid(Date(…))` test whether the constructed date is a real calendar date (the kernel's `IDateValidityCondition` — distinct from the custom-field-type validity overload, which stays unmodeled). Components may be number fields, constants (a quoted literal like `"01"`, preserved verbatim), or extractions — including `MonthFromDate`/`YearFromDate` over a **`DATE_FRAGMENT`** source (a year-month partial date; `BoundModel.dateFragment`, a `DateOperand` — comparable like a date and usable as an extraction source, §6 supplement). Modeled as `DateConstruction` (Calculation) + `DateValidity` (Condition). *(Locked: `adapter.DateConstructionReadTest` — kernel-valid + round-trip for construction, `Valid`/`Invalid`, and the date-fragment extraction; N7 catalog examples for `Date`/`Valid`/`Invalid`.)*

*(Locked: N7 examples of Today + all three Difference*/Add* operators + the `Today` number-vs-date invalid; `dsl.operators.DateConditionTest` for rendering + the non-date-field handle rejection. The order fixture gained `OrderDate`/`DeliveryDate` DateType fields.)*

### §6 supplement — malformed date-ish VALUES: the `datumBereich*` code trio, calendar validity, malformed TIME/DATE_TIME (probe, 2026-06-25)

> Fold: folded → §3 + §6.

(The formal-error class and the fragment-range completion are canonical in §3 and §6; the specific codes and side-parse rules stay as evidence.) What the formal check emits for a malformed date-family **value** (all probe-verified; each suppresses a referencing rule like any formal error):

- **DATE_RANGE — three distinct codes:** **`datumBereichTrennerFehlt`** when the value does not split into two on the declared separator; **`datumBereichFormatFalsch`** when a side is not a valid date for the format (including a calendar-invalid side); **`datumBereichNichtGueltig`** when the start is *after* the end — `start == end` is **allowed** (a one-day range).
- **Calendar validity is checked for every stored date** — the day must exist in its month (Gregorian leap Februarys): `2021-02-31`, non-leap `2023-02-29`, `2024-04-31` are all **`datumFormatFalsch`**, for DATE, DATE_TIME, and each side of a DATE_RANGE.
- **A malformed TIME or DATE_TIME value emits `datumFormatFalsch` too** — the same code as a bad DATE (VALUE-typed), not a time-specific code.
- **A range is not a direct comparison operand** — `==`/`<` over a DATE_RANGE value is authoring-rejected (type error); ranges are consumed via `StartOfDateRange`/`EndOfDateRange` and the overlap predicates.
- **A FRAGMENT-format range is well-formed (differential 2026-07-10):** a `DateRangeType` may declare a fragment format (`yyyy` — a year-range like `2024/2025`, a real workspace idiom), and each side is parsed **by the declared format at whatever granularity** — `2024/2024` carries no formal error; a non-year side is `datumBereichFormatFalsch`, start-year after end-year is `datumBereichNichtGueltig`, and the Gregorian floor applies to the completed start (`datumBereichDatumFalsch`, `1500/1600`). *(Interpreter: the side-parse completes fragments like a fragment DATE field; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF112, locked `adapter.laws.FragmentRangeFormalDiffTest`.)*
- **A FRAGMENT-format range's two endpoints complete ASYMMETRICALLY at EVAL — start = interval START, end = interval END (source + differential 2026-07-11).** When a `DateRangeType` fragment value (`yyyy` year-range) is read at runtime, the kernel completes each endpoint to a concrete date by OPPOSITE interval rules: `RuntimeController.datesAndFormatsToDateRange` calls `DateUtil.createDateFromDateFragment(dates[0], INTERVAL_START, …)` for the START and `INTERVAL_END` for the END, and `DateAndFormat.addMonthAndDay(intervalSpec)` fills a missing MONTH → `01` (start) / `12` (end) and a missing DAY → `01` (start) / the completed month's LAST day (end, via `getLastDayOfMonth`, leap-aware); a missing YEAR → the base year on both. So `2024/2025` (format `yyyy`) reads as the interval `2024-01-01 .. 2025-12-31` — the WIDEST dates the endpoints denote (a plain fragment DATE field, by contrast, always completes `INTERVAL_START` — `feldWertAlsDatumIntern` — so only a range's END side gets the latest-date completion). This is the runtime twin of the `StartOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 01.01 / `EndOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 31.12 rule below, and it is exactly what `StartOfDateRange`/`EndOfDateRange`, the overlap predicates, and `==`/`!=` read off the completed `VkDateRange`. Sub-year extraction (`MonthFromDate`/`DayFromDate`) over a `yyyy`-granularity range is AUTHORING-rejected (the granularity gate, KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 date-extraction), so the sub-year completion is observable only through overlap with a full-format range at the year's far ends (a `2024/2024` season covers a late-December window ⇒ end reached 12-31). *(Interpreter: `DateMath.parseRangeToIso` completes each endpoint per side; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF118, graduated from IG58, locked `adapter.laws.FragmentRangeEvalDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.FragmentRangeEvalTest`.)*

*(Interpreter treatment: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF8/IF12 — `formalError(DATE_RANGE …)`, `parseRangeToIso`, calendar-checking `buildIso`. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeFormalDiffTest` (trio + `calendarInvalidPlainDateMatchesKernel`) + `TimeDateTimeFormalDiffTest` (formal message + suppression).)*

### §6 supplement — date-range overlap: `DateRangesOverlap` any-pair, `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` scalar-vs-list, the predicate-arg `Having` keep-law, filter-derived polarity (probe + source, 2026-07-08)

> Fold: folded → §6.

Clean-roomed from `RuntimeController.datumsBereicheUeberlappend` / `mindestensEinDatumsBereichUeberlappendFL` + `DatumBereich.ueberlappt`, and (for the filter) the codegen `CodeGenFilterCondition` (a compiled boolean); confirmed on BOTH kernel strategies (groovy-dynamic == java-static).

- **`DateRangesOverlap(op0, op1, …)` is ANY-PAIR among ALL operands' kept, filled ranges** — one growing `HashSet<DatumBereich>` in operand order, fire on the FIRST range overlapping any already-seen member; there is **no distinguished first operand**. Consequences: (a) the same row's cell reached via a scalar ref AND via the star is two members → the **self-pair** fires (a per-row `DateRangesOverlap(Period, ../Group* / Period)` fires every filled row); (b) a **list-internal** pair fires even when the scalar operand is disjoint from every list cell.
- **`AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps(scalar In list…)` is genuinely scalar-vs-list** (the parser enforces a single unstarred first param): the scalar is compared against each list cell and is **NOT** a set member, so two LIST cells overlapping each other do NOT fire. A **not-filled/malformed scalar → FALSE** (no fire — `isWertAngegeben != TRUE → FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`, not unknown-suppression). The two operators are **not** interchangeable (they differ exactly where the set semantics bites).
- **Empty/malformed cells are SKIPPED** (`isWertAngegeben != TRUE → continue`); an **INVALID range** (start > end) never overlaps anything (`ueberlappt` returns false for either side invalid). Intervals are **CLOSED** (`end1 ≥ start2 ∧ end2 ≥ start1`; touching ranges overlap).
- **A `Having` filter on a predicate's rep-list arg is legal and applied per cell.** The kernel compiles the filter to a **boolean**, so **keep iff the filter is TRUE** (`CurrentRepetition(G) <= 2` keeps rows 1–2; `x != x` drops all; a **malformed** filter operand → runtime-UNKNOWN → not-TRUE → **DROP**). A **`$`-correlation term has NO enclosing iteration** for a plain predicate's arg (unlike a quantifier's `Having`, whose star iterates), so the codegen cannot compile it and the whole comparison/predicate is **trivially TRUE** — hence `!= $RuleGroup` AND `== $RuleGroup` both KEEP every row (the self-pair then fires all rows), while `(x != x) And (== $RuleGroup)` = `FALSE And TRUE` = FALSE drops all.
- **Polarity is filter-derived, not fill-derived.** `datumsBereicheUeberlappend` returns `TRUE_AF` (OMISSION) iff a filled cell of a **filter-bearing** operand was processed at/before the firing pair (`containsFilter` evolves in operand order), else `TRUE_WF` (VALUE). `mindestensEinDatumsBereichUeberlappendFL` returns OMISSION iff the **matched** container carries a filter, else VALUE.
- **Related consumers of a starred rep-list arg:** `FieldValuesNotUnique` also **accepts + evaluates** a `Having` (same keep-law — probed: `<=2` keeps rows 1–2, `>=2` keeps rows 2–3, changing distinctness); `SumOfProducts` **authoring-rejects** a filter (`MVK_WILDCARD_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_REQUIRED` — the lowest repetition must be an unrestricted `*`).

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeOverlapSemanticsDiffTest` — every point above as a groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter tri-check — plus `FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest`'s Having cases; multiplatform `interpreter.DateRangeOverlapTest` (JVM+JS). Interpreter treatment: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF72.)*

### §6 supplement — DATE/DATERANGE/TIME field *format strings* are a fixed lowercase allowlist, locale-independent (probe 08, 2026-06-22)

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier format-declaration rules (what `field add`/consistency accepts), outside the evaluation scope.

A field's declared date **format** must be a **lowercase Java date pattern** from a **fixed allowed set** — and that set is **independent of the model's display locale AND its `conditionLanguage`** (verified: `en_US` and `de_DE` conditionLanguage accept the identical set). The A12 editor's UPPERCASE preset labels (`YYYY-MM-DD`, `MM/MM`, …) are display labels, **rejected** as `format` values (`The date range format is invalid` / `The date format is invalid` — a kernel rejection carrying **no code and empty `where`**). Verified by `field add` against fresh `en_US`- and `de_DE`-conditionLanguage models:

- **DATE** — both `yyyy-MM-dd` and `dd.MM.yyyy` validate; `YYYY-MM-DD` rejected.
- **DATERANGE** (`format` + `separator`) — the **(format, separator) pair** is allowlisted, not free: verified-accepted **`dd.MM.yyyy`+`-`** and **`yyyy-MM-dd`+`/`** (identical under en_US and de_DE). Rejected: a spaced `" - "`; a mismatched separator (`yyyy-MM-dd`+`-`, `dd.MM.yyyy`+`/`); a non-standard format (`MM/dd/yyyy`). The separator is a single char that goes with the format.
- **DATEFRAGMENT** — lowercase `MM`/`yyyy`/`yyyy-MM`/`MM-dd` accepted; uppercase rejected. (Partial dates are DATEFRAGMENT, not DATERANGE.)
- **TIME** — the **full `HH:mm:ss` is required**; `HH:mm` (no seconds) is rejected.
- **DATE_TIME** — the **only date-bearing format is the ISO `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`** (probe-enumerated 2026-06-26); every other date-bearing form is rejected `The date format is invalid` — `dd.MM.yyyy'T'HH:mm:ss`, `MM/dd/yyyy'T'HH:mm:ss`, `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` (space, no `'T'`), and `…'T'HH:mm` (no seconds) all fail. The degenerate **time-only `HH:mm:ss`** is also accepted as a DATE_TIME format, but it cannot receive a full computed datetime (next paragraph). So a DATE_TIME field that holds a date is effectively format-locked to ISO.

**A full `DateTime(...)` result can only target the ISO DATE_TIME field — the compatibility is enforced at CODE GENERATION (probe, 2026-06-26).** Because every computation auto-generates an implicit validation rule `FieldFilled(F) And [F] != <op>` (the "every computation is also a validation rule" finding), a computation whose operation is a full `DateTime(D,T)` but whose target `F` is the **time-only `HH:mm:ss`** DATE_TIME field is rejected at codegen with **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`** ("only allowed to compare dates with other fitting dates") — the implicit compare pits a full datetime against a time-only one, whose date-component sets don't fit (the same `formatsConsistentRegardingFormat` gate as the `Now` finding above). Net for **`compute()`**: every *authorable* computed-datetime target is the ISO `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss` field, so the stored datetime is always ISO — exactly what a clean-room interpreter emits. There is no custom datetime target format to reformat to; a computed-DATE_TIME re-formatter would be dead code. *(This is why the IG19 residual closed as parity-by-construction, not a fix — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF33; locked by `adapter.laws.ComputeTargetFormatDiffTest.computedDateTimeUsesTargetFormatLikeKernel`.)*

**Caveat (don't conflate two validators):** the kernel-rt **runtime value** validator `…rt._30_8.internal.formatdef.FormatDefinitionDatum#validiereFormat` *does* key off the language (`convertPruefFormat2FehlermeldungFormat(pruefFormat, param.getSprache())`, German `'d'→'T'`/`'y'→'J'`) — but that validates a date **value** entered against a format at form-fill time, NOT the field-format **declaration** at model-consistency time (the `field add` check here), which is locale-independent. An earlier "language-keyed" reading of this was a measurement artifact (a reused field name made later iterations fail on `RK_NAME_EXISTS`, misread as a format rejection). Found by probe `08-booking-date-ranges`, where an agent burned 23 `field add` attempts brute-forcing the format because the `field-add` schema mislabeled the editor presets as usable `format` values (now corrected: the schema states the lowercase fixed-set rule and ships a CI-executed working example). *(Locked: the `field-add.schema` dateRange example `dd.MM.yyyy`+`-` is CI-executed by `SchemaExamplesGuardTest`; the reject + locale-independence facts by `FieldVerbTest.dateAndTimeFieldFormatsAreFixedAndLocaleIndependent`.)*

### §6 supplement — `Now` is a date-time; its date-field rejection is a CODE-GENERATION check, not a parse check (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §6.

`Now` (DE `Jetzt`) is the current **date-time** — the date-time sibling of `Today`. It compares only to an operand whose **date format carries a time component** (a `DateTimeType` field like `ScheduledAt`, `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`). Comparing `Now` to a plain **date** field is **rejected** — the kernel's `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl.checkDateType` requires the two operands' formats to be consistent (`formatsConsistentRegardingFormat`), and a date format (no time) vs a date-time format (with time) is not.

The non-obvious part — and the trap: **that rejection fires at CODE GENERATION, not at the parser consistency check.** Probed: `[OrderDate] >= Now` (a `DateType` field) **passes** `RuleValidator.check` (the parse/consistency phase) but throws `DocumentValidationException … while generating code: … [MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE]` once the model is compiled. By contrast a *number*-vs-`Now` mismatch (`[Quantity] >= Now`) is caught at the **parse** phase (like the `Today` number case in the §6 date-family supplement). Consequence: the N7 catalog harness (`CatalogExamplesAreKernelValidTest`, which runs only `RuleValidator.check`) **cannot** verify a codegen-only `invalidExample` — the `Now` catalog entry therefore uses the parse-rejected number mismatch as its executable `invalidExample`, and documents the date-field rejection in prose. *(Interpreter side: `Now` reuses the injected `EvalClock` — `EvalClock.now`, an ISO `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss` — exactly as `Today` reuses `EvalClock.today`; `Kind.DATE_TIME` canonicalizes a date-time field to ISO text so the comparison stays lexicographic-chronological. Locked: `adapter.laws.NowDiffTest` (FAR-instant firing + polarity) + `InterpreterMultiplatformTest.nowConstantResolvesToInjectedClock` (exact-instant boundary, JVM+JS).)*

### §6 supplement — date-time sub-unit arithmetic: day-carry on add, TRUNCATE-toward-zero on difference (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §6.

The date-time arithmetic family over DATE_TIME operands — `AddHours`/`AddMinutes`/`AddSeconds` and `DifferenceInHours`/`DifferenceInMinutes`/`DifferenceInSeconds` — reduces to integer **seconds since epoch**, so:

- **`Add*` carries across the day boundary.** `AddHours(2024-06-15T22:00:00, 5)` → `2024-06-16T03:00:00` (probe-confirmed: the resulting DATE part is the 16th, the TIME part 03:00). `AddMinutes`/`AddSeconds` likewise.
- **`DifferenceInUnit(a, b) = (epochSecond(b) − epochSecond(a)) / unit`, TRUNCATED toward zero — not floored.** Probed decisively with a reverse difference: `DifferenceInHours(later, earlier)` of −5h30m15s is **−5, not −6**. So it is plain integer division (magnitude = the forward floor, sign by direction) — the same truncate-toward-zero the `DifferenceInMonths`/`DifferenceInYears` "whole periods" implementation already produces (`wholePeriods` computes the positive count then negates). The arg order matches `DifferenceInDays`: `b − a`.

*(Interpreter: `DateMath.epochSecond`/`isoFromEpochSecond`/`addDateTimeSeconds` + `Interpreter.dateTimeShift`/`dateTimeDiff`. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateTimeArithmeticDiffTest` — the add day-carry cases + the forward/reverse difference sign cases.)*

### §6 supplement — `Date(...)` construction value + `Valid`/`Invalid` 3VL & polarity (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §6.

Runtime semantics of the date construction family (the catalog/DSL modeling is the §6 date-family supplement above; this is the *evaluation* behavior, probe-confirmed):

- **`Date(day, month, year)` of an UNREAL date is NON-EVALUABLE** — it does **not** roll over and does **not** keep an out-of-range value. Probed: with all parts present and valid numbers but `(31, 2, 2024)`, every extractor (`YearFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`DayFromDate`) over `Date(...)` returns no value (the guarding rule does not fire, no code). So the constructor yields a value only when the date is a **real calendar date**; otherwise Unknown.
- **`Valid(Date(...))` = all parts present (non-empty) ∧ all well-formed ∧ the date is real.** Fires VALUE.
- **`Invalid(Date(...))` = present-and-well-formed ∧ (not real ∨ some part empty)** — and its **polarity is directional**: an **empty (fillable) part → OMISSION** (filling it could make the date real), a **present-but-unreal date → VALUE**. It even fires (OMISSION) on an **all-empty row** (every part unspecified is invalid — so `Invalid` "can fire empty", unlike `Valid`).
- **A MALFORMED part (a formal error, e.g. a non-numeric in a number field) suppresses BOTH** `Valid` and `Invalid` (each goes UNKNOWN → quiet; the `zahl…` formal error fires instead). This is why the catalog calls Invalid "not the pure negation of Valid": they are complementary only when no part is malformed; a malformed part sends both to UNKNOWN.

So the interpreter classifies a `Date(...)` construction as REAL / UNREAL_PRESENT / HAS_EMPTY / MALFORMED, and drives the constructor value, the two predicates, and Invalid's polarity off that one classification.

*(Interpreter: `Interpreter.analyzeDate`/`dateStatusOf` + `DateMath.isRealDate`/`isoDate`. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateConstructionDiffTest` — value-via-extractor, the Valid/Invalid truth table, the OMISSION-vs-VALUE polarity split, the malformed-suppression, the empty-row firing, and `Time("h","m","s")` compose.)*

**4-arg century form `Date(day, month, century, year)`** (probe-verified 2026-06-26): the year is split into a 2-digit century + 2-digit year, full year = `century*100 + year` (`Date(15,6,20,24)` → 2024). The grammar accepts up to 4 `datumParam` (`datumKonstrukt`), and the runtime evaluates it as a real date through the same REAL/UNREAL/HAS_EMPTY/MALFORMED classification. **Operand constraints:** each `Date` param must be a **field reference** (integer literals are rejected), and the field must carry a **date-compatible NumberType range** or the model fails to build (`MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`). Interpreter: arity-flexible `analyzeDate` (IF31); locked by `adapter.laws.Date4ArgConstructionDiffTest`.

### §6 supplement — `BaseYear` is polymorphic by the other operand: base-year-START date vs the year number (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §6.

`BaseYear` (DE `Referenzjahr`) is the model's configured base year (`content.modelInfo.baseYear`, e.g. `2020`) — the third point-in-time constant alongside `Today`/`Now` (kernel `PointInTimeOperationName`). It is **polymorphic, resolved by the comparison's OTHER operand** (probe-confirmed against the order fixture's `baseYear = 2020`):

- **vs a date field → the base-year-START date `2020-01-01`.** Pinned: `[OrderDate] >= BaseYear` fires at `2020-01-01` (and `< BaseYear` does not), and does not fire at `2019-12-31` — so `BaseYear` as a date equals exactly Jan 1 of the base year.
- **vs a number field → the year NUMBER `2020`.** Pinned: `[Quantity] == BaseYear` fires at `2020`; `> BaseYear` fires at `2021` but not `5` or `2020`.

VALUE polarity (a constant, not fillable). It is **model config, not the clock** — so the interpreter resolves it from `ModelDef.baseYear` (no `EvalClock` needed, unlike `Today`/`Now`). A model that uses `BaseYear` without a declared base year is a kernel `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR` error, so a valid model always carries one.

*(Interpreter: `ModelDef.baseYear` ← `modelInfo.baseYear`; `Interpreter.evalCmpOperand` resolves the date/number form by the other operand's evaluated type, `evalConst` defaults to the date form for non-comparison uses. Locked: `adapter.laws.BaseYearDiffTest`.)*

**A point-in-time as a temporal SOURCE (source-read, 2026-07-05).** Beyond a plain operand, a point-in-time can be the *source* of an extraction/range op, and the F10 converter reads all such forms (was `Today`-only):

- **Date extraction source** (`YearFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/…): `Today`, `Now`, and `BaseYear` are all valid — `YearFromDate(BaseYear)` (kernel short-circuits a `YEAR` extraction when a base year is declared), `MonthFromDate(Now)` (Now is a date-time, so it has a month). The converter resolves any `IPointInTimeOperation` extraction source via the shared date-operand path.
- **Date-range source** (`StartOfDateRange`/`EndOfDateRange`): **`BaseYear` is the ONE point-in-time the grammar allows here** (`datumBereichAngabe: entitySpec | konstanteReferenzjahr`). It reads the base year as a range: **`StartOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 01.01, `EndOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 31.12** of the reference year (kernel `CodeGenCreator` picks the `monatTag`). The discriminator is the enum `operatorName == BaseYear` — its `getType()` reports NUMBER in the range-source position (vs DATE in the extraction-source position), so never branch on the type there. rulekit authoring: `Dsl.startOfBaseYear()`/`endOfBaseYear()`. *(Locked: `adapter.PointInTimeSourceReadTest` — kernel-valid + round-trip for both extraction and range sources.)*

### §6 supplement — a quoted date LITERAL is typed by its own SYNTAX, not by context (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §6.

A German-format date literal (`"15.06.2024"`, `dd.MM.yyyy`) is typed a **DATE constant by the kernel from its own syntax** — NOT by the operand it is compared to (clarifies the earlier date-literal family note above). Probed decisively: `[CustomerName(string)] == "15.06.2024"` is **rejected** with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` (string vs a date constant) — so there is no valid "string field vs a `dd.MM.yyyy` literal" comparison. Confirmed behaviors the interpreter must match:

- A `dd.MM.yyyy` literal compares chronologically to a DATE field even though the field's stored format differs (`[OrderDate(yyyy-MM-dd)] == "15.06.2024"` fires for `2024-06-15`).
- The literal also rides in **argument position** — `DifferenceInDays(OrderDate, "01.01.2022")` (the second arg is the date constant).
- A date-looking value inside a value LIST (`FieldValueIncludedInValueList(field, "15.06.2024")`) is a plain **string** value, not a date — so canonicalization must be **position-scoped** (a comparison operand opposite a date, or a date-function arg), never a blanket literal rewrite.

**Interpreter consequence:** since a date literal in a *valid* comparison always faces a date, the interpreter canonicalizes a `dd.MM.yyyy` `StrLit` to ISO exactly where it is used as a date — opposite a date-producing operand (`Interpreter.evalCmpOperand` + `producesDate`, which reads the new `EvalContext.declaredKind`) and inside the date functions' date-arg reads (`coerceDate` in `epochOf`/`dateComponent`/`dateShift`). An ISO-format literal (`"2022-01-01"`) is a string (kernel `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`), so `coerceDate` only rewrites the German form. The **omitted-year** form (`"13.07."`, day.month., trailing dot) IS modeled (IF52): it is a **reference-year** feature — the kernel injects the model's `baseYear`, so `"13.07."` means `13.07.<baseYear>` (probe-confirmed: on `baseYear = 2020`, `[OrderDate] > "13.07."` fires for 2024/2020-07-20 but not 2019-07-20 or 2020-07-10 — exactly `baseYear`, **not** a floor). A model with NO base year **rejects** it at validation (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`), so the interpreter only sees it with a base year present; `coerceDate` appends `baseYear` before the ISO parse. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateLiteralDiffTest` — ==, ordering, arg-position, the value-list non-canonicalization guard — + `adapter.laws.OmittedYearDateDiffTest` for the omitted-year base-year injection.)*

### §6 supplement — partial dates: the `00.MM.yyyy` form and `ValueAsDate`/`FirstDay`/`LastDay` (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §6.

A `DateType` with `datePrecision: DAY_OPTIONAL` (the order fixture's `ApproxDate`, `dd.MM.yyyy`) may omit the **day** — stored as the sentinel **`00` in the day position** (`00.06.2024`). Probe-confirmed:

- **`00.MM.yyyy` is well-formed** (no `datumFormatFalsch`); `06.2024` / `.06.2024` / `MM.2024` are NOT (`datumFormatFalsch`). So only a literal `00` day omits the day.
- **`ValueAsDate(field, FirstDay|LastDay)` resolves a partial value**: `FirstDay` → day **01**, `LastDay` → the **month's last day** (`00.06.2024` → `01.06.2024` / `30.06.2024`). A **full** value resolves to **itself** for both day-specifications. `FirstDay`/`LastDay` are operand-less day-specification keywords (the 2nd arg).
- **`ValueAsDate` is only valid as a direct comparison operand** — nesting it in an extractor (`DayFromDate(ValueAsDate(…))`) is `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`. Its headline form compares to a date literal (`> "01.01.2020"`) — which is why date literals (above) had to land first.

**Interpreter consequence:** a `DAY_OPTIONAL` field's day-omitted value is well-formed (precision-aware `formalError`) but is **not a single concrete date**, so a direct reference resolves to `Val.Unknown` — only `ValueAsDate` resolves it (off the field's RAW value + format, via `DateMath.resolvePartialDate`). `FieldDef.datePrecision` + `EvalContext.dateFormatOf` carry the inputs. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ValueAsDateDiffTest` — FirstDay/LastDay of a partial, ordering vs a literal, and full→itself.)*

### §6 supplement — `AddYears`/`AddMonths` calendar corrections (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §6.

KERNEL-SEMANTICS §6 carries the exact corrections; the drained source (`BedingungsOperatorHelper.addiereJahre/addiereMonate`) pins the exact corrections, both probe-confirmed:

- **`AddYears` corrects the Feb-28 boundary**: a Feb-28 in a non-leap year shifted into a leap year lands on the **leap day** — `28.02.1999 + 1 year = 29.02.2000`, *not* the plain-`Calendar.add` answer `28.02.2000`.
- **`AddMonths` clamps end-of-month overflow**: `31.01.2008 + 1 month = 29.02.2008` — clamped to the shorter month's last day, never a carry into March.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateArithmeticLawsTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — date ranges are closed intervals: touching ranges overlap (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §6.

`DateRangesOverlap` / `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` treat both the start **and** the end day as belonging to the range (`DatumBereich.ueberlappt`: overlap ⇔ `end1 ≥ start2 ∧ end2 ≥ start1`). Consequences, both probe-locked:

- **Back-to-back periods sharing a boundary day overlap** — a window ending 31.01 and a coverage starting 31.01 fire the overlap. Seamless non-overlapping periods must start the day *after* the previous end.
- Adjacent-day ranges (end 31.01, start 01.02) do not overlap; a contained range does.
- Source note: an *inverted* range (start after end) silently never overlaps — defensively handled, though the format tier normally prevents one from existing.

**`AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps(A IN B*)` is the `IN`-syntax sibling of `DateRangesOverlap(A, B*)` — semantically identical (probe 2026-06-24): A overlaps ≥1 range of the second operand, same closed-interval rule.** The only difference is the surface syntax — the kernel grammar has three distinct `In`/`in`/`IN` tokens (operator-specific): the value-list family + `NumberOfValueInFields` use **`In`**, this overlap predicate uses **`IN`**. The interpreter parses both (the `readArgs` arg0-separator accepts `In` or `IN`) and routes `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` to the same `dateRangesOverlap` eval; a fired overlap is a VALUE error either way. *(Interpreter: `PredicateOp.ATLEAST_ONE_DATE_RANGE_OVERLAPS` shares the eval; locked by `adapter.laws.DateRangeDiffTest` — the same five overlap scenarios in the `IN` form.)*

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateRangeOverlapLawsTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — partially-known-date extraction gating + the *FromDate date-time source (source + probe, 2026-06-17)

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier gating (parse/consistency codes over precision × operator), outside the evaluation scope; the datetime-source acceptance is canonical in §6.

`DayFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`YearFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` (and `AddDays`) accept a **DateTime** source, not only a Date (kernel date table: "X can be Date or DateTime") — `DifferenceInDays`/`AddYears`/`AddMonths` stay Date-only. On a **partially-known date** (a `DateType` carrying a `datePrecision` other than FULL): **`DayFromDate` is rejected outright** — `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE` (probed on a DAY_OPTIONAL field; distinct from `ValueAsDate`'s `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE_FOR_OP`) — while `MonthFromDate`/`YearFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` are **accepted on a DAY_OPTIONAL** date (probed). **The full precision gradient is now source-confirmed and locked** (was BA-stated, IG66→graduated): `DateUtils.checkOperationConsistentToPartialKnown` rejects extracting a component the field's `datePrecision` allows to be unknown, keyed on the fragment (`d`/`m`/`y`). The 4×3 matrix (op × precision, all rejects `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`): **DayFromDate** — rejected on DAY/MONTH/YEAR_OPTIONAL (never usable on a partial date); **MonthFromDate** & **QuarterFromDate** (Quarter maps to the MONTH component) — accept DAY_OPTIONAL only, reject MONTH/YEAR_OPTIONAL; **YearFromDate** — accepts DAY_OPTIONAL + MONTH_OPTIONAL, rejects YEAR_OPTIONAL. Monotone: a component is extractable iff guaranteed present under the declared optionality (MONTH_OPTIONAL ⇒ day also unknown; YEAR_OPTIONAL ⇒ all unknown), so YEAR_OPTIONAL rejects every extraction. Confirms BA L4123-4125 exactly. **Add\* on a date fragment is gated by the format's granularity** — `AddDays` needs a day, `AddMonths` a month, `AddYears` a year, else **`MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`** (probed: `AddMonths(MM-fragment)` and `AddYears(yyyy-fragment)` accept, `AddDays(MM-fragment)` rejects; BA L4076-4091). **Source:** the extract gating is `CheckDatumExtractOpImpl` → `DateUtils.checkOperationConsistentToPartialKnown` (rejects extracting the field's *optional* component → `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`); the Add\* granularity is `CheckDatumAddOpImpl.check` → `DateUtils.checkOperationConsistentToDateFormat`, gated on `format.hasComponent(dateFragment)` → `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`. *(Locked: `validate.laws.DateExtractionLawsTest` + `validate.laws.DateFragmentArithmeticLawsTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — Valid/Invalid over a constructed date are NOT complements (runtime, 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §6.

`Valid(Date(...))` fires only when **all referenced parts are specified AND** they form a real calendar date; `Invalid(Date(...))` fires when the date is impossible (e.g. 31 February) **OR any referenced part is unspecified**. So they are **not logical complements** — an unspecified part fires `Invalid` but not `Valid` (runtime-verified). To fire `Invalid` only on a *complete-but-impossible* date, prefix `AllFieldsFilled(...)`. (BA "Valid or invalid dates"; corrects the catalog's earlier "Invalid = negation of Valid".) *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DateValidityLawsTest`.)*

**The BA's "1583 Gregorian floor" (L3833) is NOT a runtime `Date(...)`-construct criterion (probed 2026-06-17, probe-evidence).** `Invalid(Date(1,1,1500))` does **not** fire — the runtime treats `01.01.1500` as a valid constructed date (the same rule fires for an impossible date, so the mechanism works). The floor **is** enforced — at the *value* tier: the later §3 supplement ("a DATE value has an always-on lower bound", 2026-07-09) verified and locked the formal check on stored/computed values (`datumFalsch`, `ComputedDateGregorianFloorDiffTest`). (A 4th BA-vs-source/runtime discrepancy found this review, after `INVALID_INTERVAL`, the semantic-index operator, and the fragment-comparison code.)

### §6 supplement — date-fragment comparability partition (probe-confirmed, 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §6.

Without a base year, two date(-fragment) values compare (`==`/`!=`/ordering) **only if both carry a year or neither does** — a year-bearing fragment/full date and a non-year fragment are incomparable, rejected with **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`** ("only allowed to compare dates with other fitting dates"). Probed: `[YearOnly:yyyy] == [FullDate]` accepts; `[MonthOnly:MM] == [YearOnly:yyyy]` rejects. **Note the code:** a *direct comparison* gives `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`, **not** the BA's `MVK_DATE_WITH_AND_WITHOUT_YEAR` (that is the `DifferenceIn…`/overlap-op code for mixed-year args). A declared **base year** relaxes the partition (a missing year reads as the base year — BA L4069-4074, not probed here). (Source `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl.checkDateType` → `DateFormat.formatsConsistentRegardingFormat(fmt1, fmt2, hasBaseYear())`; the same method also fires `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` for a BASE_YEAR vs a non-date-specification operand — confirming the code is this comparison path's, not the `DifferenceIn…` path's.) *(Locked: `validate.laws.DateFragmentComparabilityLawsTest`.)*

**rulekit treatment — a date fragment IS a `DateOperand` (read + author, 2026-07-05).** Because the kernel collapses a fragment to a plain `Date` for parse/check/render/compare, `DateFragmentRef` is a full `DateOperand` (not the earlier "source-only" kind): it compares with all six operators against a date field, another fragment, `Date(...)`, `Today`/`Now`, `Base Year`, or a date expression, rendering as a bare `[field]`. F10 read converts a fragment comparison through the same date machinery — on the left (`fieldValueComparison` `case DATE_FRAGMENT`) and as a right/date operand (`convertDateOperand`, which previously threw `IllegalArgumentException: … is not a date field (kind=DATE_FRAGMENT)` when a fragment sat in a date-operand slot). rulekit does **not** re-enforce the year-class partition on read — the kernel already accepted the rule. *(Locked: `adapter.DateFragmentComparisonReadTest` — kernel-valid + round-trip for fragment-vs-date, date-vs-fragment, and fragment-vs-fragment.)*

**§6 supplement — date-fragment RUNTIME completion: earliest concrete date, base-year for the year (probe-confirmed, 2026-07-07 — canonical §6).** The *evaluation* companion to the comparability partition above (that is the authoring gate; this is what a valid fragment then does at runtime). A `DateFragmentType` value is stored in its `formatOfFragment` (one of `yyyy-MM`, `yyyy`, `MM`, `MM-dd`); when the runtime reads a fragment field for a comparison / `Add*` / extraction it **completes the missing components to the *earliest* concrete date** and compares as a plain `Date`:

- **missing MONTH → `01`, missing DAY → `01`, missing YEAR → the model base year** (`modelInfo.baseYear`, default 2000). Probed by comparing against a full DATE field of a known value: `[CardExpiry:yyyy-MM = "2024-06"] == [Full = "2024-06-01"]` **fires** (not `2024-06-15`); `[WarrantyYear:yyyy = "2024"] == [Full = "2024-01-01"]` fires (not `2024-06-15`); `[SaleWindow:MM-dd = "06-01"] == [BillMonth:MM = "06"]` fires (`MM` completes day → `01`).
- The **base year is observable**: with `modelInfo.baseYear = 2024`, `[BillMonth:MM = "06"] == [Full = "2024-06-01"]` fires but `== "2023-06-01"` does not (the year-less fragment completes its year to the *declared* base year, which is also what makes it comparable to a year-bearing date — the partition's base-year relaxation).
- **Both kernel strategies share the completion** — it lives in `RuntimeController.feldWertAlsDatum` (kernel-core-runtime), and the codegen's `FieldIdOperation` emits `controller.feldWertAlsDatum(idx, …)` for a date field — so runtime and codegen agree by construction (`DateUtil.getValueAsDate` + `PartiallyKnownDatesUtil.replaceMonthAndYearIfUnknown(…, INTERVAL_START)`). A malformed fragment value (bad month, non-numeric) is `datumFormatFalsch` and suppresses the referencing rule, like any date.

*(Interpreter: `DatePrecision.FRAGMENT` + `DateMath.completeFragmentToIso` + `Document.resolveValue`/`FormalError` — IF57. Locked: `adapter.laws.DateFragmentComparisonDiffTest` — the three-way differential incl. the java-static codegen leg — + multiplatform `interpreter.DateFragmentEvalTest`.)*

### §6 supplement — TIME and DATE_TIME value kinds (implemented 2026-06-07)

> Fold: folded → §6.

(The kernel truths — TIME orderable like DATE, DATE_TIME decomposing via the extraction lattice — are canonical in §6/the field-types table; the modeling/treatment log stays.) **`TIME` is orderable exactly like `DATE`** (no scale, no D13 machinery): `[ServiceWindowStart] >= [ServiceWindowEnd]` is kernel-valid (a time field vs another time field). Modeled as `TimeRef`/`TimeOperand`/`TimeExpr`, obtained from `BoundModel.time(path)`; the same six order operators as dates. Time constant literals (`"17:00:00"`) are implemented (family 1, see the §6 supplement above).

**`DATE_TIME` decomposes into its parts and components** — the kernel's `DateExtractionOperationName` covers the whole lattice, and **the extractions accept a `DATE_TIME` source, not only their nominal kind** (verified against the workspace `DateTimeValues_DM` model, where a single `DateTimeType Source` feeds every extraction):

| operator | source kind(s) | result |
|---|---|---|
| `YearFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`DayFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` | DATE **or** DATE_TIME | number |
| `HoursFromTime`/`MinutesFromTime`/`SecondsFromTime` | TIME **or** DATE_TIME | number |
| `DateFromDateTime` | DATE_TIME | date |
| `TimeFromDateTime` | DATE_TIME | time |

rulekit models the numeric ones as `DateExtraction` (kind NUMBER), the part-extractions as `DateTimePart` (kind DATE/TIME), with a common `TemporalOperand` source supertype (`DateOperand`/`TimeOperand`/`DateTimeOperand`). DATE_TIME is both a decomposition source **and** (since family 3) a directly comparable kind — see the construction supplement below. *(Locked: `adapter.TimeAndDateTimeReadTest` — render + kernel-valid + round-trip for TIME ordering, time-of-day extraction, year-from-DATE_TIME, and Date/TimeFromDateTime; N7 catalog examples for all five new operators. The order fixture gained `PickupTime` TimeType + `ScheduledAt` DateTimeType fields; modelkit gained `timeField`/`dateTimeField`.)*

**`Time` / `DateTime` construction + direct DATE_TIME comparison (implemented 2026-06-07, family 3).** Two more kernel constructors build temporal values from parts: `Time(hour, minute, second)` (DE `Zeit`, the `ITimeConstructionOperation`) → a TIME; and `DateTime(date, time)` (DE keyword is the untranslated `DateTime`, the `IDateTimeConstructionOperation`) → a DATE_TIME. Component grammar mirrors `Date(...)`: a component is a **bare number field** or a **quoted constant** (`Time("10", "30", "50")`) — a bracketed value `[H]` is a parse error (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`). For `DateTime`, the date operand is any date value (field / German date literal / `Date(...)`) and the time operand any time value (field / time literal / `Time(...)`). This also unlocks **direct DATE_TIME comparison**: `[ScheduledAt] == DateTime(OrderDate, PickupTime)` is kernel-valid, so a date-time field compares (`==`/`!=` and ordering) to another date-time value — a field, a `DateTime(...)`, or an `AddHours`-style shift. (Still rejected: a date-time field vs a *bare string literal* — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`; build the value with `DateTime(...)`.) Modeled as `TimeConstruction`/`DateTimeConstruction` (Calculation) + a `DateTimeOperand` sealed interface (`DateTimeRef`/`DateTimeExpr`, orderable like dates/times) folded under `TemporalOperand`. Authored via `Dsl.time(...)`/`Dsl.dateTime(date, time)`. *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsTimeAndDateTimeConstruction` + `dsl.operators.DateConditionTest.rendersTimeAndDateTimeConstruction` + BA-corpus `TimeConstruct`/`DateTimeConstruct`; N7 catalog examples for `Time`/`DateTime`.)*

**Point-in-time constants `Now` and `BaseYear` (implemented 2026-06-07).** The kernel's `PointInTimeOperationName` has three members — `Today` (a date, long modeled), `Now` (DE `Jetzt`), and `BaseYear` (DE `Referenzjahr`) — all carried as `IPointInTimeOperation`. `Now` is the current **date-time**; it compares to a date-time field *and* (truncated to the day) to a date field — its operation type is `DATE` in both. `BaseYear` is the model's configured base year and is **polymorphic by comparison context** (verified): compared to a date/date-time field its operation type is `DATE` (the base-year start); compared to a *number* field it is `NUMBER` (the year). `BaseYear` is only valid in a model that declares a base year — `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR` otherwise — so **modelkit gained `baseYear(int)`** (emits `modelInfo.baseYear` as a string; the kernel default is 2000). Modeled as `Now`/`BaseYear` (Calculation); the converter handles `Now` as a date and date-time operand, and `BaseYear` as a date, date-time, and number operand. Authored via `Dsl.now()` (→ `DateTimeExpr`), `Dsl.baseYear()` (→ `DateExpr`), `Dsl.baseYearNumber()` (→ `NumberExpr`). *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsNowAndBaseYear` + BA-corpus `NowCmp`/`BaseYearCmp`; N7 catalog examples. The order fixture now declares a base year.)*

**Sub-day time arithmetic over date-times (implemented 2026-06-07, family 2).** The kernel's `Add*`/`DifferenceIn*` families each carry three sub-day members alongside the date ones: `AddHours`/`AddMinutes`/`AddSeconds` (DE `Addiere{Stunden,Minuten,Sekunden}`) and `DifferenceInHours`/`DifferenceInMinutes`/`DifferenceInSeconds` (DE `DifferenzIn{Stunden,Minuten,Sekunden}`). They operate on **DATE_TIME** operands: `Add*` shifts a date-time and **returns a date-time** (it can roll the calendar day over — distinct from the date-returning `AddDays`/`AddMonths`/`AddYears`); `DifferenceIn*` returns a number (a floor, sign = second − first, like the date differences). The shifted date-time composes as an extraction source — `HoursFromTime(AddHours(ScheduledAt, 2))` is kernel-valid. Modeled by widening `DateShift.Unit`/`DateDifference.Unit` with the three sub-day members (the shift unit carries its result kind, DATE vs DATE_TIME); the converter routes a DATE_TIME field through a `temporalOperand` helper (a `DateTimeRef` is a `TemporalOperand`, so it renders bare in argument position) and `convertTemporalSource` now accepts a shift as the extraction source. Authored via `Dsl.addHours`/`differenceInHours`/… over a `TemporalOperand` (the date `Add*`/`DifferenceIn*` factories widened from `DateOperand` to `TemporalOperand` too). *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsSubDayTimeArithmetic` + `dsl.operators.DateConditionTest.rendersSubDayTimeArithmeticBilingually` + BA-corpus `DiffInHours`/`DiffInMinutes`/`AddMinutesShift`; N7 catalog examples. The order fixture gained a second DateTimeType field `CompletedAt` so the difference examples reference two distinct date-times.)*

## §6 supplement — min/max over DATES ride a dedicated `DateCombiner`: NULL-date empty identity, valueless-operand skip, symmetric omission (source + differential, 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §2 + §12.

(The NULL-date empty identity is canonical in §2 "aggregate identities"; the symmetric date polarity in §12's directional machinery; the combiner-dispatch mechanism stays as evidence.) The kernel dispatches `Min`/`Max`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue` **statically by operand type onto two combiner families** — numbers → `NumberCombiner` (`min`/`minWert`), dates → **`DateCombiner`** (`minDatum`/`minWertDatum`; the only non-number family — min/max over strings is not authorable) — and the two differ in three observable ways:

- **Empty identity.** The number combiner seeds `0` (§1 supplement — `MaxValue()` over all-empty == 0). The date combiner seeds `VkDate.NULL_DATE` — *no value* — and `BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleicheDATUM` returns **false when either side's date is null**, so a comparison against an all-empty (or zero-row) date aggregate **never fires** (`FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`), and a computation of it **clears** the target.
- **Operand-list emptiness.** `minDatum(d1, d2)`'s `VkDate.combine` **keeps the date-bearing side** when the other operand is valueless — an empty date operand is SKIPPED (`Min([OrderDate], [DeliveryDate])` with `OrderDate` empty is `DeliveryDate`), unlike the number form where an empty operand arrives as the substituted `0` and COUNTS. A malformed / required-empty date operand is nicht-prüfungsrelevant → the whole aggregate is suppressed.
- **Symmetric polarity.** `DateCombiner` tracks no directional `kannGroesserWerden`/`kannKleinerWerden` — only the symmetric **`nichtAngegeben`** (ORed over the operands' emptiness, plus `getValue(containsFilter)` marks any filtered result, plus the omitted-tail flag). A fired date comparison types **`TRUE_AF` (OMISSION) iff either side is nichtAngegeben — regardless of the comparison direction** (`RuntimeController.vergleicheDATUM`), where the number star's polarity is directional (§1/§3 supplement).

Comparison **typing** stays static and nominal: a quoted literal beside a date-valued min/max is a DATE literal (`"20.03.2026"`), and no date/text-vs-number comparison is authorable at all (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` / `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`) — so a runtime date-vs-number pair can only ever be evaluator-made, never input-legal. *(Interpreter: kind-aware fold identity + `minMaxOperandVals` + the `starFill` date branch + `producesDate` over the min/max family; locked by `adapter.laws.DateAggregateDiffTest` incl. a codegen tri-check — IF58.)*

## §14 supplement — CustomCondition's two forbidden contexts (corpus item 8, 2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: folded → §14.

`CustomCondition <Name>` (DE `ApplikationsBedingung`; grammar `applikationsBedingung: 'ApplikationsBedingung' nameOhnePunkt`) is a bare predicate — a host-registered name, no operands. KERNEL-SEMANTICS §14 names two bans; both verified and now **pre-checked** (the constructs are representable):

- **Inside a `Having` filter** → **`MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION`** ("The language feature 'CustomCondition' is not supported for filter conditions"). *(Isolating it needs a filter that already references the iteration — otherwise `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` fires first.)*
- **In computation rules** (§14) — pre-checked in `Computation.build()` by walking preconditions for a `CustomConditionRef`.
- It does **not** satisfy the error-field rule on its own (a bare `CustomCondition X` → `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`); pair it with a field reference — rulekit's generic `Rule.build()` error-field pre-check already covers this.

*(Locked: N7 `CustomCondition` valid; `dsl.operators.CustomConditionTest` for both bans + the bare-identifier name rule. DE keyword pairing verified from the lexer bundle (can't N7 against the en_US fixture — model condition-language gates parsing).)*

## §2 supplement — the filled-count aggregates (2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: folded → §2 + §12.

The behavioral half is canonical (§2's count-family override row; §12's grow-only counts; §3's invalid-operand non-evaluability); the authoring slot rules below stay here as evidence. `NumberOfFilledFields` / `NumberOfFilledGroups` (DE: `AnzahlAngegebenerFelder`/`AnzahlAngegebenerKontexte`; the grammar's `feldListenAnzahlOp` — its third sibling `NumberOfDifferentValues` is also implemented, see the list-aggregate slot laws below) are **numeric expressions**, freely comparable and usable in arithmetic (`NumberOfFilledFields(a, b) + 1 < [Q]` validates). Slot rules:

- **`NumberOfFilledFields` needs more than one field *or* at least one group *or* a single flattened repetition list** — a single plain field draws **`MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`** ("There must be more than one field or at least one group"), but a single wildcard scope `Group*/field` is accepted (it counts filled reps). The group form counts the filled fields *inside* the group(s); multiple groups are legal. Field kinds may be **mixed** (boolean, enum, number together — it counts presence, not values).
- **`NumberOfFilledGroups` needs more than one group *or* a single repeatable group as a wildcard scope** — two-or-more *distinct* groups are legal, and so is **one repeatable group written `Group*`** (counting that group's filled repetitions, kernel-verified 2026-06-06 against `NumberOfFilledGroups(/Order/Items*)`). A single *non-repeating* group still draws **`MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDGN`**; a field argument draws the type-direction code **`MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`**.
- **Root groups: banned in `NumberOfFilledFields`** (**`MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED`**, "It is not allowed to reference a rootgroup"), but **allowed in `NumberOfFilledGroups` as the sole, starred operand** — `NumberOfFilledGroups(/Persons*)` over a repeatable root group validates (source `checkNoRootGroupWhenMoreThanOneParameter`; probe-confirmed 2026-06-17, **correcting the earlier "banned in both"**).
- Comparing the count to a **decimal literal** (`<= 1.5`) is accepted (consistent with the §5 literal-scale exemption).

*(Locked: N7 examples of both operators incl. the param-size/type-direction invalids; `NumberOfFilledGroups`'s sole-starred-root-group **acceptance** in `validate.laws.RootGroupCountLawsTest`; the `NumberOfFilledFields` root-group ban pre-checked in `CountAggregate` — `dsl.operators.AggregateConditionTest`. NB rulekit's typed `CountAggregate` builder is conservative — it can't yet author the root-group `NumberOfFilledGroups` form the kernel accepts; a DSL authoring gap, not a kernel limit.)*

**Runtime counting semantics (2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain — `anzahlAngegebenerEntities`):**

- **A formal-error operand makes the whole count non-evaluable** — the rule is suppressed; the broken field is neither counted as filled nor skipped (contrast `FirstFilledValue`, where a broken operand *after* the first filled one is invisible).
- **The count can grow but never shrink**: with unfilled operands left, a fired comparison is an `OMISSION_ERROR`; with every operand filled, a `VALUE_ERROR` — a plain field list has no omitted-tail problem (unlike the starred aggregates, §9 supplement).

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.FilledCountLawsTest`.)*

**Generalizes to every NUMERIC COMBINER (2026-06-23, fuzz-found):** a malformed element makes the **whole combiner non-evaluable → rule suppressed**, not just counts. Probed: `Sum(Lines* / Price) > 100` with one `"abc"` element fires **only** `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen`, never the rule. Holds for the starred aggregates (`Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues`) and the operand-list `Min`/`Max` alike — a formal-error operand is "unknown", and an aggregate over an unknown is unknown. (Empties are different: the star drops absent/empty cells, so an empty row is *skipped*, not a suppressor; only a FILLED-but-malformed value suppresses.) **"Malformed" means FORMALLY invalid, not merely unparseable** — a fuzz seed caught that `-0` (a signed zero) *parses* as `0` yet is a formal error, so `Sum` over a `-0` element must suppress (the kernel does); the interpreter therefore decides aggregate-element validity by the **formal check**, not by parseability. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.AggMalformedDiffTest`.)*

## §4 supplement — index-field uniqueness: `uniqueIndex` flags EVERY duplicate participant, scoped per parent (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §4.

Canonical in §4 "The index field's auto-generated checks"; the evidence skeleton: `uniqueIndex` fires on **every** duplicate participant (`[A, A, B]` flags rows 1 AND 2), per **parent-row** scope, VALUE-typed, via an autogenerated rule; an empty index value is excluded and draws `mandatoryField` instead (fuzz-found, duplicate-key pool). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.UniquenessDiffTest`.)*

**The uniqueIndex error is operand-level "unknown", branch-scoped (fuzz-found 2026-06-24):** rules reading the duplicated field are suppressed; a sibling field's rule on the same rows still fires. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.UniqSuppressionDiffTest`.)*

**…and it makes an AGGREGATE / COUNT over a starred index field NON-EVALUABLE too (differential + source, 2026-07-08).** An index field's not-check-relevant cell — mandatory-empty OR duplicated — suppresses an aggregate/count over `Group* / indexField` exactly as a malformed cell does: the shared loop `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe` (and the count `anzahlAngegebenerFelder`) returns `NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL` on the first such cell; the fill quantifiers count it in neither bucket. Both strategies share these runtime methods ⟹ codegen-parity. An index field can be numeric (a `Sum` over one is authorable). *(IF86; locked: `adapter.laws.IndexAggregateDiffTest` + `interpreter … IndexAggregateTest`.)*

## §4 supplement — `FieldValuesNotUnique`: a duplicate among the operands' values, plain-list OR starred (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §9.

Canonical in §9 "Other repetition rules"; the evidence skeleton: two operand shapes with identical semantics — a plain field list (distinctness across named siblings) and a starred operand (distinctness across a repetition's rows; the no-`--group` alternative to `RepetitionNotUnique` on a non-index field — on an index field the auto `uniqueIndex` fires first and suppresses it).
